# Mod API reference

This page is a generated index of the catalog-backed trait hooks (`crates/sunrise-mod-vocab/src/hooks/`) and
area-declared host methods (`crates/sunrise-mod-vocab/src/host/`). For complete trait definitions (including
non-catalog methods), handles, and cross-linked rustdoc, build the crate docs locally with
`cargo doc -p sunrise-mod-api --no-deps --open`.

- [Hooks (`Hooks` trait)](#hooks-hooks-trait)
- [Part hooks](#part-hooks)
- [Host methods (`Host`, `ServerHost`, `ClientHost`)](#host-methods-host-serverhost-clienthost)

## Hooks (`Hooks` trait)

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `add_attribute_tooltips` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `AddAttributeTooltipsEvent` | `AddAttributeTooltipsEvent`: the lines that follow a stack's attribute lines (`ItemStack.addAttributeTooltips`), after the modifiers the stack shows and `gather_skipped_attribute_tooltips` left in. Push lines to `lines`, each with a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own); they land beside the attribute lines without finding where those end. `shown` is whether the stack's `minecraft:tooltip_display` shows the attribute modifiers (`shouldShow`); the hook is asked either way. `player` and `player_entity_id` are the local player's, `None` for the recipe book's search text, which has none and drops the colours, and for a stack nested in another's tooltip (a crossbow's loaded projectile); `advanced` (F3+H) and `creative` (a creative tab's own slot) are the tooltip's `TooltipFlag`. Asked when lines are built for display, recipe book search or `ItemResource::tooltip`. Display and resource-query builds skip it when the stack hides its entire tooltip. Reentrant line queries do not dispatch it. Client-side, only while a mod listens. A component deeper than 512 nodes refuses capture or the whole writeback; no event field is applied if any Text field fails conversion. |
| `add_client_reload_listeners` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientReloadListenerContext` | `AddClientReloadListenersEvent` | Register client asset reload listeners before a reload stages its assets (NeoForge's `AddClientReloadListenersEvent`, the client's counterpart of `add_server_reload_listeners`): the first load on the loader thread, then every later reload — the player changed resource packs, F3+T was pressed, or a server sent its packs. The listener's `prepare` then reads the merged asset view with resource-pack overrides resolved, and its returned apply publishes the parsed state, all applies after all prepares, before `client_resources_reloaded`. Dependencies on the model manager order both its real model build and its publication. Atlas upload precedes the model manager. A listener prepare or engine preflight that refuses the candidate aborts the load before any listener applies. Client-side, taking a `ClientReloadListenerContext`. Later registration is on the render thread, prepare on the loader, and apply on the owning game thread. On initial startup before any resources are published, `context.published()` is `None`; on later reloads it returns `Some(&dyn ClientReloadHost)`. Registration errors are returned and logged at the call, without stopping the reload. A panicking registration callback contributes no listeners or dependencies; unrelated callbacks still run. |
| `add_pack_finders` | both | listen | `&mut dyn PackDiscoveryContext` | `AddPackFindersEvent` | Add discoverable resource or data packs before a repository selects its packs (`AddPackFindersEvent`). The builder reports the repository's pack type. Runs at startup and whenever that repository is rediscovered; no world state is available during startup registration. Disabled native mods' listeners are skipped from the first startup discovery onwards. A callback that panics contributes no packs; later callbacks still run. |
| `add_section_geometry` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `AddSectionGeometryEvent` | Extra geometry in chunk section meshes (NeoForge's `AddSectionGeometryEvent`): `register(renderer)` adds the mod's own `SectionGeometry`, which the mesher asks to add its quads every time it meshes a section, on its worker threads. The quads are part of that section's mesh: they follow its rebuilds, are never drawn twice, and go when it unloads. Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. A registration callback that panics or incurs a phase refusal contributes no renderers. |
| `add_server_reload_listeners` | server | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `AddServerReloadListenersEvent` | Register server data reload listeners after packs are selected. The `ReloadContext` gives registry access at registration, as `AddServerReloadListenersEvent.getRegistryAccess` does. Runs every data load on the owning server thread; world handles, services and transactions are not present on this context. Existing data-load callbacks retain their `DataLoadContext`. Each listener's `prepare` returns an owned action, consumed once on the server thread after all prepares succeed. Registration errors are returned and logged at the call, without stopping the reload. A panicking registration callback contributes no ordered or retained listeners or dependencies; unrelated callbacks still run. |
| `alter_ground` | server | collect | `&dyn WorldgenHost` | `AlterGroundEvent` | A tree's `alter_ground` decorator (`AlterGroundDecorator.place`, the podzol under a big spruce) is about to turn the ground around `positions`: the lowest of its trunk or roots (`logs` if it has no roots, `logs` and `roots` when they start level, else `roots`); only those at the first one's Y are placed around. `logs`, `leaves` and `roots` are the decorator context's lists, each sorted by Y. Set `provider` to your own `GroundProvider::Mod` to choose a canonical `BlockState` for the ground; to change it only where you like, wrap the provider you found and ask it for the rest, since several mods may each wrap the one before. Once per decorated tree, before any ground is placed; the hook itself draws nothing. Server-side, on the worldgen worker threads (and on the server thread when a sapling grows), with the server's worldgen `context`: no `Host`, no writes. |
| `animal_tame` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AnimalTameEvent` | The player `tamer` (UUID) and `tamer_entity_id` is about to tame the mob `entity` of `kind` (`AnimalTameEvent`): its taming roll succeeded (a wolf's bone, a cat's fish, a parrot's seeds or a nautilus's food; a horse, donkey, mule or llama the player rides). Set `cancel` for the attempt to fail instead, as a failed roll does: smoke for a pet, and a horse bucks its rider off. Before `entity_tame`. Server-side. |
| `anvil_craft_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AnvilCraftEvent.Post` | A player took an anvil's result at `pos` and the levels, inputs and anvil wear are done (`AnvilCraftEvent.Post`); a broken anvil is already air. `inputs` borrows the same pre-take input stacks and result as `anvil_craft_pre`. Not called when `anvil_craft_pre` cancelled. |
| `anvil_craft_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AnvilCraftEvent.Pre` | A player took an anvil's result at `pos` (`AnvilCraftEvent.Pre`, matching NeoForge `AnvilMenu.onTake`), before the levels are charged, the inputs used up and the anvil worn. `inputs` borrows the input stacks and result from before the take. Set `cancel` to return early before charging levels, consuming inputs, damaging the anvil or firing `anvil_craft_post`, without undoing the click's item move (the listener handles any post-processing itself). |
| `anvil_damaged` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An anvil at `pos` took damage, changing its block from `from` to `to`. `to` is air when the anvil broke. Server-side. |
| `anvil_update` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `AnvilUpdateEvent` | An anvil at `pos` made vanilla's result with a left input, for `player` (UUID) and `player_entity_id` (`AnvilUpdateEvent`, matching NeoForge `AnvilMenu.createResult` / `CommonHooks.onAnvilUpdate`), live on every `createResult` with no answer cache: `inputs` lends the stacks, rename text and vanilla result; `edits` starts with `Keep` and vanilla's costs. Set an output or edit the costs without changing the inputs. Cancellation clears the result and both costs. An invalid replacement output is logged and ignored (keeping vanilla's output while still applying the cost edits). `edits.xp_cost` stays signed during listener dispatch and clamps to `0` when applied (`AnvilMenu.setCost`). Both sides, including the update after material consumption with the left slot still present. On the server that update observes the player after `AnvilMenu.onTake` has charged the levels. |
| `arm_pose_transform` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `HumanoidModel.ArmPose` | Apply a registered pose (`HumanoidModel.ArmPose`'s transformer). Asked only for a selected registered pose, once per model and arm during humanoid extraction, in vanilla's physical-arm application order. Suppressed poses do not call the transformer. `humanoid` contains the selected poses after `get_arm_pose` answers. `right_arm` identifies the physical arm, not the hand. Write absolute pitch, yaw and roll in radians into `angles`; initially all components are non-finite, meaning preserve the base model angle. Each non-finite component is ignored. The ordinary swing and species animations still run afterward. |
| `armor_hurt` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ArmorHurtEvent` | Armor is about to take durability damage from a hit (NeoForge's `ArmorHurtEvent`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.doHurtEquipment`): once per hit, for the pieces that would wear, and first for the helmet alone when a falling block hits a player (`hurtHelmet`). `entity` is the wearer's entity id, `player` its UUID (None for a mob), `kind` its type and `source` the hit's damage type (`causing` and `direct` are 0: the hit does not carry them here). Each entry has its `slot`, the `stack`, the `damage` vanilla worked out and a `new_damage` a mod may change (0 for none; Unbreaking is applied after). Canceling means no armor damage. A server player's `feet`, `legs`, `chest` and `head`; a mob's `body` (a horse's armor). Server-side. |
| `armor_stand_manipulate` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player takes an item from an armor stand or puts one on it (`ArmorStand.interactAt`, after the stand's disabled-slot checks and `swapItem`'s take/put locks, before anything moves): `stand_id` is the stand's `Entity.getId` and `slot` the one the click reached (`mainhand`, `offhand`, `feet`, `legs`, `chest` or `head`). Set `cancel` and nothing changes: neither the stand nor the player's hand, and the click is spent. The player's state is swapped in for the call; `player` is the UUID the helpers take. Not told of a click that changes nothing (an empty hand on an empty slot). The listener may change the world: straight after it returns without cancelling, the game finds the stand again by `stand_id` and checks that it still exists, that the slot's stack and the held stack keep their item and count, and that the slot is not now locked; if not, the click is spent and nothing moves. Not covered, other ways a stand's equipment moves: breaking or blasting the stand (its items drop), a dispenser putting armour on one, a mod's own changes. Server-side, for every player of the world. |
| `arrow_loose` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ArrowLooseEvent` | NeoForge's `ArrowLooseEvent`: the player releases a bow `stack` with `has_ammo` and `charge` ticks held. Change `charge` or set `canceled` to prevent the shot; negative charge also prevents it. Unlike `living_entity_use_item_stop`, this does not skip the whole item release; `entity_shoot_bow` reports a spawned arrow afterward with read-only force. Fires on the Game running the bow release, including LAN guests; a remote client's own release path is not covered. Crossbows are not covered; `arrow_nock` is the bow's start. |
| `arrow_nock` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ArrowNockEvent` | A player is about to nock `bow` with `hand` (`BowItem.use`, NeoForge's `ArrowNockEvent`): `has_ammo` is whether `getProjectile` found ammunition or the player is in creative. Set `cancel` to fail the use. |
| `attack_entity` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AttackEntityEvent` | A player attacks the entity `entity` (`Entity.getId`) of type `kind` (`minecraft:item_frame`, `minecraft:cow`, `minecraft:player`) standing at `pos`: `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handleInteract`'s attack, once in reach, where `Player.attack` starts. The swing has not begun, so the attacking player's attack strength is still the charge the blow uses (vanilla reads it at the start of `Player.attack`; the swing resets it afterwards). Set `canceled` and the blow does nothing (no damage, no knockback, the frame keeps its item). Server-side, for every player of the world (`host.players().acting_player()` says who). |
| `baby_entity_spawn` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BabyEntitySpawnEvent` | Two animals bred and the baby they made is about to join the world (NeoForge's `BabyEntitySpawnEvent`, vanilla's `Animal.spawnChildFromBreeding`): `dimension` is the level they are in (a replacement baby's `PendingMobSpec` names it), `parent` and `partner` are entity ids, `player` and `player_entity_id` the UUID and entity id of the player who fed them into love (`getCausedByPlayer`, None when none). The baby (a mule for a horse and a donkey) is already constructed, with its inheritance drawn, but not finalized or in any level: it is the single entry of the engine-owned list `edits.children()` names, which `edits` changes. Edit the baby in place (`edits.set_position`, `edits.set_yaw`), replace it with another baby (`edits.construct` a baby `PendingMobSpec`, then `edits.replace`), or remove it (`edits.remove`) for none. A second baby is refused with `Capacity`, and a replacement must be a baby. Set `cancel` for no baby either: the parents still rest and lose their love (their ages were already set to 6000), but there is no baby, no experience, no `animals_bred` statistic, no advancement and no heart particles. The construction draws stay spent. A panicking callback is reported and disabled for the session, and this breeding is cancelled. A refusal (including an explicit veto or a stale parent) rolls back journaled listener writes in the breeding level. An accepted baby is applied before deferred join callbacks run. Every handle is borrowed for one listener only. Not fired for turtles, frogs and sniffers (an egg, no baby), or their hatching; armadillos take the ordinary animal path and are included. Villagers do not post this animal-only event, matching NeoForge's documented `Animal.spawnChildFromBreeding` posting site; a rejected villager join instead leaves the child's bed free. On the server. |
| `beacon_state_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A beacon at `pos` activated or deactivated after its beam was checked. `active` says whether it now has a complete pyramid. Server-side. |
| `bell_ring` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | On an authoritative server, a bell at `pos` is about to ring. Set `cancel` to stop the ring and its raider reveal. |
| `biome_colors` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Change the colours of `biome` (`minecraft:plains`) on this client: the grass, foliage (leaves, vines), dry foliage (leaf litter) and water tints, 0xRRGGBB, as its biome file and the colormaps set them. Asked for every biome when a mod calls `native_refresh_biome_colors`, which rebuilds the terrain with them; a season mod answers from its season and calls that when the season turns. The dark forest's modifier still applies to the grass set here; the swamp's noise colours replace it. |
| `block_break_progress_update` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server guest's block-breaking animation changed to `progress` at `pos` (0 through 9). |
| `block_burn` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | On an authoritative server, fire is about to burn the block at `pos` away. Set `cancel` to keep that block. |
| `block_changed` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | An authoritative server committed a changed state through `Level.setBlock`. The world contains `new`; block side effects and neighbour updates follow. `flags` are the `BlockUpdateFlags` applied to the write that reached the level, not to any intermediate changes staged in a transaction. `KNOWN_SHAPE` skips the neighbour shape passes, not this notice. Each listener gets its own event. |
| `block_cook` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A block finished cooking `item` into `result` at `pos`. |
| `block_damage_abort` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player stopped digging `pos` before the block broke, after the server cleared the dig and sent the block back to the client. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `block_dispense_armor` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A dispenser equipped `item` on the first eligible `target` in front of its block at `pos`. `target` is `player`, `mob` or `armor_stand`. The equip, item consumption, sound and game event have completed. Server-side. |
| `block_drops` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `BlockDropsEvent` | NeoForge's `BlockDropsEvent`: `Block.dropResources` has prepared the item entities but has not joined them to the level. Fires for player and mob block breaks using this path, and level-side breaks (pistons, water, neighbour updates, `/setblock destroy`). Not fired for explosions, dispensers or other loot-table drops. Use `can_drop_from_explosion` to decide whether a blasted block drops, and `loot_drops` to edit its stacks before the blast merges them. Player and mob loot also runs `loot_drops` first; other level-side breaks do not. |
| `block_entity_type_add_blocks` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `BlockEntityTypeAddBlocksEvent` | Extend existing types' valid blocks (NeoForge `BlockEntityTypeAddBlocksEvent`). Fired after content declarations, before worlds load. `event.types` is the list of registered types. Use `event.modify(kind, blocks)` for a direct addition, or `event.modify_matching(predicate, blocks)` for all matching registered keys. Neither changes the block's factory or ticker. Duplicate additions are harmless; unknown keys or incompatible classes fail startup. Existing sign-template memberships remain valid without a listener. Blocks must share the common vanilla superclass of the type's existing blocks. |
| `block_fade` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | A block at `pos` is about to fade from `from` to `to` because of melting or coral death. Set `cancel` to keep it. Server-side. |
| `block_failed_dispense` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A dispenser at `pos` failed to dispense `item`. Server-side. |
| `block_form` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Precipitation is about to form ice or place or thicken snow at `pos`. `from` and `to` are block state IDs. Set `cancel` to keep the old state. Server-side. |
| `block_grow_feature` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `BlockGrowFeatureEvent` | A sapling, azalea, mushroom or fungus at `pos` is about to grow into the configured `feature`, `None` when vanilla has none to place (a lone dark oak sapling). Set `cancel`, or `feature` to `None`, to keep the block; set `feature` to another key to grow that one. Server-side. Draw from the level's live stream with `host.with_random(\|rng\| rng.next_int_bound(bound))`. |
| `block_highlight` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ExtractBlockOutlineRenderStateEvent` | Before an eligible targeted block's selection outline is built. Cancel skips only the default block highlight, after normal HUD and game-mode gates. |
| `block_ignite` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | On an authoritative server, fire is about to be placed at `pos` by flint and steel, a fire charge, lava, lightning or fire spread. Set `player` is the acting server player when there is one. Set `cancel` to leave the position unchanged. |
| `block_lock_check` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's block inventory lock check at `pos`. `locked` is the vanilla result and may be changed. The guest's state is not swapped in. |
| `block_receive_game` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A sculk sensor or shrieker received a vibration for `event` at `pos`. Set `cancel` to suppress its response. Server-side. |
| `block_shear_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A dispenser sheared `entity` of kind `kind` at the block in front of its own block at `pos`, after the shearing effects. Server-side. |
| `block_spread` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | On an authoritative server, a fluid flows or fire spreads from `from` into `to` (`kind` is `fluid` or `fire`): `FlowingFluid.spreadTo`, and `FireBlock.tick` setting fire to `to` or burning it (`checkBurnOut`). Set `canceled` to leave the block at `to` as it is. Fluids ask on every flow: answer fast. |
| `block_tool_modification` | both | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `BlockEvent.BlockToolModificationEvent` | A tool is about to transform a block. Cancel or replace the final state; `None` suppresses the transformation. The block's own `tool_modified_state` part answers first, and a block whose part cancelled the action never reaches this hook. |
| `blocks` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Declare blocks in code once at startup on each side, before resources load. Pass each `mod_api::Block` to `event.register`. The declaration belongs to the submitting package — in its own namespaces or in a content package it serves (`served_by`) — and works like `data/<id>/sunrise/block/<path>.json` in a content mod, including a block item unless disabled with `Block::with_item(false)`. Duplicate IDs across the host and served layers, and declarations into a `side = "server"` package's namespace, are refused. `register` returns a typed `DeclarationError` for invalid IDs or fields. The game records the refusal and stops startup even if a returning listener ignores it. A panic discards this listener's declarations and diagnostics without rejecting startup. |
| `bonemeal` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `BonemealEvent` | `BonemealEvent`: bone meal from `stack` used on the block `state` at `pos` (`BoneMealItem.growCrop`), by `player` or, when it is `None`, by a dispenser or a villager. `valid_bonemeal_target` is the block's `isValidBonemealTarget`. Cancel skips vanilla's growth, the block's own bone meal Parts and the meal's use: `growCrop` then answers `successful` (`setSuccessful`), which is `false` unless a mod sets it. Fired on both sides; changes to `stack` stay. Set it to None to consume the whole stack; a nonempty result is validated before any write-back. |
| `break_block` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BreakBlockEvent` | A player breaks the block `state` at `pos`, once the game allowed it (the held item can break blocks, the debug stick didn't take the click): where `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock` starts. Set `canceled` to keep the block: nothing is destroyed or dropped, and a guest sees it back. Set `exp_to_drop` to `Some(amount)` to replace the experience sample later, after item drops are prepared; `None` keeps sampling. A subscribed block part's `exp_drop` answer is supplied as this event's initial amount. `block_drops` sees and may change that amount afterwards; cancellation or disabled block drops suppresses it. Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). Diverges from upstream: renamed from `destroy_block`, to NeoForge's `BreakBlockEvent`. |
| `brewing_complete` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A brewing stand finished brewing at `pos`, after its bottle contents were changed. Server-side. |
| `brewing_fuel` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A brewing stand at `pos` consumed `fuel` and gained brewing fuel. Server-side. |
| `brewing_start` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A brewing stand at `pos` began brewing `ingredient`, after fuel and the timer were set. Server-side. |
| `broadcast_message` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | — | A server broadcast line is about to be logged and sent. Change `line` or set `cancel` to drop it. |
| `bucket_empty` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player empties a bucket at `pos` (`fluid`: `minecraft:water`, `minecraft:lava`, `minecraft:powder_snow`; a fish or axolotl bucket empties `minecraft:water`): where `BucketItem.use` has found the block the fluid goes into and the player may build there, right before it is placed. Set `cancel` and nothing is poured and the bucket stays full. The player's state is swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` answers them. Only for guests of a server. `fill_bucket` hears this click too (and the filling one) without the player and the fluid. Not heard: a dispenser, a cauldron, and a fluid a block places itself. |
| `build_creative_mode_tab_contents` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `BuildCreativeModeTabContentsEvent` | Edit a creative mode tab's ordered parent and search lists after its generator and before feature-flag filtering (NeoForge's `BuildCreativeModeTabContentsEvent`). Runs once for every registered tab, including Search, Hotbar and Inventory, on the logical side that requests the build. The event gives the tab id, enabled feature flags, permission profile and a build-scoped edit handle. Edits are visible to later listeners in dispatch order. |
| `calculate_player_turn` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `CalculatePlayerTurnEvent` | The mouse sensitivity and cinematic camera option a grabbed player's mouse turn reads this frame (`MouseHandler.turnPlayer`). Change either for this turn only; the options are unchanged. Client-side. This shapes how far the mouse turns the view; `ClientHost::set_local_rotation` sets the view directly, and the two meet in the same angles the tick's move packet sends. |
| `camera_distance` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `CalculateDetachedCameraDistanceEvent` | Final scaled third-person camera distance, before visual-shape collision clipping. `entity` includes its living scale/distance and optional mount scale/distance. Only finite requests in 0..=1024 blocks replace the vanilla distance. |
| `camera_pose` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeCameraAngles` | The camera this frame is drawn from, `partial` ticks past the last tick, after vanilla placed it (`Camera.setup`): its position, and its yaw and pitch in degrees. Set them to draw the world from anywhere: a cinematic or replay camera. Only the picture, the sound's listener and the billboards facing the camera follow it; the player, where they aim, the first-person hand and which chunks load stay the player's. Values that aren't finite keep vanilla's. Not called on the title screen's panorama. |
| `camera_shake` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeCameraAngles` | The camera shake native mods ask for in this frame, `partial` ticks past the last tick (0 to 1): add your pitch, yaw and roll in degrees and your offset in blocks, along the camera's right, up and back, to `shake`, so several mods' shakes add up. Read while playing only; Sunrise scales the sum by Distortion Effects and bounds it (`native_shake_matrix`). |
| `camera_submersion` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Override the client view's submersion. Camera fluid reads (`Camera.getFluidInCamera`) start with the game's fog type after `state_at_viewpoint`. The first-person water overlay instead asks at the local player's eye with `Water` if the player's tick sampled water there, or `Atmospheric` otherwise. The sample comes from death tick 20 once the local player is removed, until respawn. Setting `Water` enables the overlay; any other value suppresses it. Set `Atmospheric` to clear submersion or a fluid to force it. Later listeners see the last successful value. A panic disables only that mod's listener for the session and is reported in the log and the client error toast. |
| `camera_type_cycle` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | `key.togglePerspective` (F5) is cycling the window's camera type (`Minecraft.handleKeybinds` calling `Options.setCameraType(getCameraType().cycle())`): `previous` is the type it leaves, and `next` comes in as vanilla's next one (first person, third person behind, third person in front, then first person again); set `next` to another, to skip a type or keep one. Once a press, before the type changes; the post effect of the camera entity follows the answer as it follows vanilla's. `camera_type` and `set_camera_type` read and set the type at any other moment. Client-side. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `campfire_start` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An item was placed on a campfire at `pos` and began cooking. |
| `can_continue_sleeping` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CanContinueSleepingEvent` | `CanContinueSleepingEvent`: a sleeping living entity's check that it may go on sleeping, run each tick for a server player the dead and health checks would not already wake, and for a sleeping villager or wandering trader. `entity` is its UUID, `name` a player's name (empty for a mob) and `kind` its type (`minecraft:player`). `problem` is what vanilla found (`getProblem`): `NotPossibleHere` when the bed at `pos` is gone, `NotPossibleNow` when a player's bed rule no longer lets them sleep (daytime), none when nothing is wrong. `may_continue` starts empty, meaning `problem.is_none()` (`mayContinueSleeping`); set it `Some(true)` to keep them asleep, `Some(false)` to wake them. The player's state is not swapped in on a guest tick. |
| `can_eat` | both | replace | `&dyn Host` | — | `Player.canEat`, from `Consumable.canConsume` when eating starts and block interaction eligibility for cake and candle cake. `player` is the eater: the acting server player (including a LAN guest) or the local player whose use the client predicts. Read their effects through `host.living(player.entity())`. For food items, `stack` is the food being consumed. For cake and candle cake, `stack` is `None` regardless of the held item. Vanilla computes eligibility with always-eat false for cake, or the stack's food component for food. `vanilla_can_eat` is that answer (creative, always-eat, or hungry). Answer `Some(false)` to refuse the food, `Some(true)` to let a full player eat; `can_eat` left `None` keeps `vanilla_can_eat`. Both sides (the client starts eating as the server does), so answer from the stack and the player alone. Not asked without a resident eater or an open lifecycle epoch; vanilla's answer stands during those world/login lifecycle gaps. |
| `can_player_sleep` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CanPlayerSleepEvent` | A server player tries to sleep in the bed whose head is at `pos` (NeoForge's `CanPlayerSleepEvent`, vanilla's `ServerPlayer.startSleepInBed`). `problem` is what vanilla found, first problem only: `too_far_away`, `obstructed`, `not_possible_now` (not night or thunder, the bed rule), `not_safe` (monsters near), or `None`. A mod may set `None` to let the player sleep anyway, or one of those names (`other_problem` refuses without a message) to refuse. The spawn point is set before it fires, as in vanilla, only when vanilla found no problem. After it, `player_bed_enter` still fires and may cancel. Server-side. |
| `can_start_swimming` | both | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IEntityExtension.canStartSwimming` | Replace `IEntityExtension.canStartSwimming` for the player being simulated. `Entry` is asked of a sprinting player who isn't already swimming or flying, before excluding passengers. `ShallowWater` is asked in local sprint eligibility when the vanilla shallow-water clause is false but the body touches a swimmable fluid; a `false` answer then means shallow. The first `Some(true)` or `Some(false)` wins. If no listener answers, Sunrise uses vanilla's saved-eye and feet-cell entry checks, or the ordinary vanilla shallow-water result. This does not change the player's fluid samples, flight or existing swim. Declare `side = "both"` and install the mod on both sides. Base the answer on facts available to both sides that tick; the server remains authoritative for position. |
| `cauldron_level_changed` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | On an authoritative server, a cauldron at `pos` is about to change fill level from `from_level` to `to_level` for `reason`. Set `cancel` to leave it unchanged. For `extinguishing` (a burning entity in a water or powder snow cauldron), the game checks after the hook returns without cancelling that the block at `pos` still has the block id and properties it judged; if not, the block is not changed. |
| `chat_recipient` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A public chat line from `sender` (`sender_name`) is about to reach the placed player `recipient`: a player's chat after `server_chat` let it through, or a `/say` or `/me` (signed or not). For a non-player `/say` source such as the console or a command block, `sender` is the nil UUID (`0`) and `sender_name` is the source's display name. `message` is the text players see: what `server_chat` left, or the command's text. Set `hidden` and that one receiver is not sent the line at all, no chat index and no pending acknowledgement. The others still get it, signed as it was sent, so its signature still verifies for them. The hook is asked once for each placed receiver, the sender included, before any of them is sent the line, in the order the hub lists them. Whispers (`/msg`) are not public and are not heard here, and neither is the line the server's log keeps: hiding is per receiver. This is not a second `server_chat`: that hook is heard once for a line, only for typed chat, and cancels it for everyone; this one is per receiver, and covers `/say` and `/me`. With no listener nothing is asked and nothing is allocated. A `/say` requested while this hook is running fails rather than reentering the hook or sending an unchecked line. No player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `chat_suggestion_applied` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | `chat_suggestion_applied(original, start, end, suggestion, input)` reports a chat completion after Tab or a click. The range is in UTF-16 units of the list's original input, including when cycling; input is the resulting length-limited field value. Client-only, read-only. Bundles need "hooks": @API:chat_suggestion_applied@. |
| `chunk_data_load` | server | listen | `&dyn ChunkDiskHost` | `ChunkDataEvent.Load` | The disk record's attachments have decoded, before the chunk is published. Runs on a chunk worker with a restricted Host. Read and edit `data` through its typed methods; edits become the loaded store only if the entire job succeeds. Missing records and proto-to-full promotion do not fire this hook. Borrow the captured metadata from `host.job()`. A codec error, panic, or invalid or over-budget disk NBT refuses publication of the whole store. Later listeners still run on the last accepted envelopes. A phase refusal discards only that callback's proposal; only a panic disables a callback for this hook for the session. |
| `chunk_data_save` | server | listen | `&dyn ChunkDiskHost` | `ChunkDataEvent.Save` | A committed chunk snapshot is about to encode on the chunk worker. `data` belongs only to that snapshot: typed edits change the disk record, never the live chunk. Exactly once per captured record, for full and proto chunks alike. The restricted Host cannot resolve live chunks or mutate the world; `host.job()` borrows the captured metadata. A failed job, including invalid or over-budget disk NBT, retains its snapshot and the prior disk record. After a callback panics or hits a codec error, later listeners still run on the last accepted envelopes, but the job cannot publish. A phase refusal discards only that callback's proposal; only a panic disables a callback for this hook for the session. |
| `chunk_load` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ChunkEvent.Load` | Chunk `chunk` (`[x, z]`, in chunks) of `dimension` came into the world (NeoForge's `ChunkEvent.Load`): `new` when it was just generated, false when read from the save. A flat world's chunk that leaves before it's first lit isn't saved, so it's generated, and new, again; one generated as the players left is new when it next comes, unless the server restarts first, or the level closes for being empty, which drops its memory of what never saved. Its blocks and block entities are there, but its saved entities join afterwards and its light may not be ready: change the world from a later tick. The chunks around the spawn come before `server_started`. Not told of a chunk a client receives. The chunks leaving in the same tick were already copied for their last save: an edit made here to one of them (a neighbour's block entity, say) is not in that copy, and is dropped with the chunk. Server-side. |
| `chunk_sent` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ChunkWatchEvent.Sent` | `chunk`'s packet went to `player` (NeoForge's `ChunkWatchEvent.Sent`, at the end of `PlayerChunkSender.sendChunk`), after `chunk_watch`, so a mod can send its own data for the chunk now. Told after the server's network step for the level, with the state after that step, as `chunk_watch`. Server-side. |
| `chunk_ticket_level_updated` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ChunkTicketLevelUpdatedEvent` | `chunk`'s ticket level went from `old` to `new` (NeoForge's `ChunkTicketLevelUpdatedEvent`, from `ChunkMap.updateChunkScheduling`): a player, a forced chunk, a portal or the dragon fight's arena began or ceased to keep it alive or ticking. It is the level of the chunk's loading tickets (`DistanceManager`'s chunk tracker), not the simulation distance's. A level is a `ChunkTicketLevel`: the number `ChunkLevel` gives, 31 and lower entity ticking, 32 block ticking and 33 loaded without ticking, or `Unloaded`; none is clamped to the ticking range. Never told with `old == new`. |
| `chunk_unload` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ChunkEvent.Unload` | Chunk `chunk` of `dimension` left the world (NeoForge's `ChunkEvent.Unload`), with a level that closes for being empty too. Don't read its blocks. Its block entities are still there, but changes made in this hook aren't saved: as in vanilla, the chunk is saved before it unloads. Not told when the world closes, which `world_lifecycle` tells. Server-side. |
| `chunk_unwatch` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ChunkWatchEvent.UnWatch` | `player` stopped watching `chunk` (NeoForge's `ChunkWatchEvent.UnWatch`, `ChunkMap.dropChunk`): it left their view, or they leave the level (through a portal or a teleport, or respawning) or the server. Once for each chunk `chunk_watch` told of. After a disconnect the player handle no longer reads. Told after the server's network step for the level, with the state after that step, as `chunk_watch`. Not told when the server stops. Server-side. |
| `chunk_watch` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ChunkWatchEvent.Watch` | `player` started watching `chunk` (NeoForge's `ChunkWatchEvent.Watch`): the chunk came into their view and goes to their client, once until `chunk_unwatch`. Vanilla tells it as the chunk is queued (`ChunkMap.markChunkPendingToSend`); Sunrise picks a chunk only as it sends it, so this comes just before `chunk_sent` for the same chunk. Told after the server's network step for the level, and a handler sees the state after that step; the order per player and chunk is vanilla's. Server-side. |
| `client_attack_target` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `AttackEntityEvent` | Before a client's ordinary melee click attacks entities (not a spear jab), after the interaction-key cancellation hook. `targets` starts with the crosshair entity, if any. Replace, clear or append targets for redirected or multi-target hits. There is no batch cap. Duplicates are sent once, in first-occurrence order. The committed crosshair entry keeps its tick-pick point and client occlusion policy; the tick's selection is not re-hit. Additional targets are rechecked for the current client epoch, attackability, reach, held-item range and occlusion. Clearing an entity hit does not mine the block behind it; an untouched empty list keeps vanilla's block/miss path. Server checks and cooldowns still apply to every attack. Returning `Flow::Cancel` cancels the whole batch. NeoForge's `AttackEntityEvent` is the corresponding attack notice; this hook is client-side only. |
| `client_block_changed` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The block at `pos` in `dimension` went from `old` to `new` in this client's level (vanilla's `Level.setBlock` on a `ClientLevel`), after the write: the level already reads `new`. Told once per block whose state changed, on every route a client writes a block by: the server's block update, each block of its batched section update, the client's own predictions (a break, a placement, a use; `predicted` is true) and their reconciliation when the server acknowledges them (a refused prediction comes back, `predicted` false). A write that keeps the state is not told, and neither is a server update held under a pending prediction until the acknowledgement applies it. Whole columns arriving and leaving are `client_chunk_load` and `client_chunk_unload`. Only on a client of a server, a LAN guest included, and without a server-side mod; an integrated world's own blocks are the server's `block_changed`. A level a server opens for viewing (`sunrise:levels`) is not told. Client-side; with no listener a write costs one flag check. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `client_chat` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientChatEvent` | NeoForge's `ClientChatEvent`: a client is about to send `message`. `original_message` is immutable; change `message` or set `cancel` to prevent sending. |
| `client_chat_received` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientChatReceivedEvent, ClientChatReceivedEvent.Player, ClientChatReceivedEvent.System` | NeoForge's `ClientChatReceivedEvent`: a chat line from the server is about to show, once Chat Delay lets it through. `system` is a system message (`ClientChatReceivedEvent.System`), shown in the action bar when `overlay` is set; otherwise a player or disguised message in its chat type `chat_type` (`getBoundChatType`, its registry name; `None` for a system message or an inline chat type). `sender` is the player's UUID, 0 (`Util.NIL_UUID`) for system and disguised messages. `player_message` is the message as the player signed it (`ClientChatReceivedEvent.Player.getPlayerChatMessage`): its body, whether a signature validated, timestamp, salt, the server's unsigned replacement and the filter mask; `None` for system and disguised messages. `message` starts as the decorated line; replace it and the chat shows the new one, while the narrator still says the original. Set `cancel` and it doesn't show: a delayed message lets the next one through. Lines the client adds itself and validation errors are not told. Client-side. |
| `client_chunk_load` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ChunkEvent.Load` | Chunk `chunk` (`[x, z]`, in chunks) of `dimension` came into this client's level from the server (NeoForge's `ChunkEvent.Load` on the client, whose `isNewChunk` is always false there): once per chunk packet the client adopts, after the column and its block entities are in the level, so a listener reads its blocks through `ClientHost`. A column the server sends again while it is resident is told again. Only on a client of a server, a LAN guest included; an integrated world's own chunks have the server's `chunk_load`. Client-side. |
| `client_chunk_unload` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ChunkEvent.Unload` | Chunk `chunk` (`[x, z]`, in chunks) of `dimension` is leaving this client's level (NeoForge's `ChunkEvent.Unload` on the client): once per resident column, with its blocks still there, just before they are freed. Told on every route a column leaves by: the server's forget packet, the view centre moving away, the view radius shrinking, and the client level going (a dimension change, a respawn into another level, leaving the server). That is wider than NeoForge, which tells only the forget packet, so a listener never keeps state for a column that is gone. Only on a client of a server, a LAN guest included; an integrated world's own chunks have the server's `chunk_unload`. Client-side. |
| `client_disconnecting` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingOut` | Before an actual connection is discarded, not on a dimension change. |
| `client_identity` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientIdentityHost` | — | Choose what the client reports to the server at initial configuration (`ClientHandshakePacketListenerImpl.handleLoginFinished`, `ClientBrandRetriever.getClientModName`). Runs on the network thread once per join that reaches configuration. A transfer joins a new destination; play-to-configuration transitions and the integrated server do not fire it. |
| `client_information_updated` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ClientInformationUpdatedEvent` | NeoForge's `ClientInformationUpdatedEvent`: a server player's client sent new client information (language, view distance, chat, skin parts, main hand, filtering, listing, particles) in play. `old` and `new` are the values before and after this packet, one call per packet; they can be equal. The join's own information is reported too, once, just before `player_logged_in`, with `old` the defaults (`ClientInformation.createDefault`) and `new` what the client joined with, as NeoForge fires it when `ServerPlayer` first sets it. The player is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `client_level_load` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `LevelEvent.Load` | The client made a new level for `dimension` (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Load` from the `ClientLevel` constructor): on joining a server, single player's own included, and on changing dimension. Its chunks come after. When the dimension changes, the old level's `client_level_unload` follows this, as `Minecraft.setLevel` tells it after the new level is made. Client-side, on the window; the server's levels have `level_load`. |
| `client_level_unload` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `LevelEvent.Unload` | The client dropped its level for `dimension` (`LevelEvent.Unload` from `Minecraft.setLevel` and `disconnect`): replaced by another dimension's, or on leaving the server. Once for each `client_level_load`. Not told when the game itself quits. Client-side. |
| `client_mining_predict` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Veto a predicted client dig after the interaction mapping and vanilla eligibility checks. Auto-tool and mining overlay mods can use this instead of a mining prediction mixin. The snapshot is read-only. A veto skips the new mining operation and preserves any retained dig. Release and correction still perform ordinary cleanup. |
| `client_packet` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | A play packet from the server reached the window's game, decoded, just before the game handles it (`Connection.genericsFtw` handing it to `ClientPacketListener`): every packet of the play phase, custom payloads included, in the order the server sent them. Control packets are not heard, whether standalone or bundled: keep-alives, chunk batch starts and finishes, bundle delimiters, resource pack pushes and pops, cookies, server links, transfers, disconnects, the configuration switch, `minecraft:brand` and far terrain tiles; neither is the configuration phase. Read-only: the game handles the packet as it would without the mod. Client-side, on any server, single player's own included. Every packet exposes its protocol type id. Custom payloads also expose their channel and raw bytes; no other decoded fields cross the API. |
| `client_packet_send` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | A play packet the window's game is about to send to the server (`Connection.send`): every packet of the play phase it sends, in the order it sends them, the selected slot that goes out before an attack or a use included. Not heard: what the network thread sends itself (keep-alives, chunk batch acknowledgements, configuration pongs, cookies, the configuration phase), the answers the connection sends in whichever phase it is in (a resource pack's status, a dialog's custom click action), and far terrain requests. Cancel drops the packet: the game goes on as if it had been sent, so the server never hears of it. Client-side, on any server, single player's own included. Every packet exposes its protocol type id; custom payloads also expose their channel and raw bytes, as `client_packet` does for the packets that arrive. Play-phase pongs pass through this hook; return `Flow::Cancel` to drop a packet. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `client_pause_change_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPauseChangeEvent.Post` | NeoForge's `ClientPauseChangeEvent.Post`: the pause state changed. |
| `client_pause_change_pre` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPauseChangeEvent.Pre` | NeoForge's `ClientPauseChangeEvent.Pre`: cancel a proposed pause change. |
| `client_pick` | client | collect | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | Whenever vanilla picks: once per client tick at `partial_tick = 1.0`, after the game mode tick and before key handling (also while paused), and once per frame at the frame's game-time partial tick before extraction. Both sites overwrite the same stored result. `origin` is the world-space XYZ eye position of the camera entity used by this pick: interpolated at the frame's partial tick, current for the tick pick. `original` is the vanilla answer; block and entity candidates are supplied independently. Each listener receives the same inputs. Only `result` is shared between listeners: it starts at `original` and may be replaced or cleared in dependency order. Tick inputs, including both hands of use, read the tick's result without another pick. The highlight reads the frame's result, which can differ from the tick's. Reentrant crosshair_target reads see `original`. A replacement is validated once at writeback; invalid output becomes a miss, never a recomputed pick. Consumption does not recheck block state or entity liveness. Server checks and item-specific rays (including buckets and brushes) are unchanged. No NeoForge 26.3 counterpart. |
| `client_player_change_game_type` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPlayerChangeGameTypeEvent` | NeoForge's `ClientPlayerChangeGameTypeEvent`: observe any `PlayerInfo` game type change before the new mode is stored and after `LocalPlayer.onGameModeChanged` runs. `player_id` is `Some` when the profile has a player entity in the client level. |
| `client_player_clone` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.Clone` | After the client replaces its player on respawn or dimension change (NeoForge's `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.Clone`). Client-side. |
| `client_player_logging_in` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingIn` | The client accepted a play login and initialized its level and player identity (NeoForge's `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingIn`). `player_id` is the server's entity ID. | `client_player_logging_out` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingOut` | Before client disconnect teardown (NeoForge's `ClientPlayerNetworkEvent.LoggingOut`). The player may be absent. Client-side. |
| `client_resources_reloaded` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientReloadHost` | `ClientResourceLoadFinishedEvent` | Render-thread notification after a resource-pack reload has applied successfully. Atlas, models, renderer and client caches already refer to the new resources. Selection/F3+T and server-pack reloads share this point; initial startup does not (`client_setup` is the once-only moment after the initial load). Receives a `ClientReloadHost` for published asset/data queries; world handles and transactions are not present on this context. |
| `client_setup` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientSetupContext` | `FMLClientSetupEvent` | NeoForge's `FMLClientSetupEvent`: the client has started. It fires once per game process, on the render thread, after the first client load has applied (the atlas, models and every client reload listener's published state are final) and before the title screen's first frame. Returning to the title screen and opening another world is not a new start, so it never fires again. This is the opposite of `client_resources_reloaded`, which never runs at initial startup and runs after every later reload. The listener runs on the main thread; work a mod wants done in parallel is its own. The dedicated server has no client and never fires it (see `server_about_to_start` for its `FMLDedicatedServerSetupEvent`). Receives a `ClientSetupContext` for client setup and published resource queries without world handles or transactions. |
| `client_stopped` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientStoppedEvent` | The client has finished shutting down, after `client_stopping`. Fires once on the render thread for an orderly client exit, not for a dedicated server. NeoForge's `ClientStoppedEvent`. |
| `client_stopping` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientStoppingEvent` | The client is closing: it fires once per game process, on the render thread, when the game shuts down, after the world the player was in is left and saved, while the game still holds its settings and resources, so a listener can read them through the `ClientHost` and save its own client state. It is the closing counterpart of `client_setup`. Closing the window, a quit shortcut and the process's stop signals all reach it; a crash does not. A dedicated server has no client and never fires it (`server_stopping` is its stop). Fabric's `ClientLifecycleEvents.CLIENT_STOPPING` fires earlier, before the integrated server stops, so it is not a counterpart. NeoForge has no event for this moment. A listener is a pure function of its `ClientHost` reads and its own state. |
| `client_tick_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientTickEvent.Post` | Once every client tick, paused or not and on the title screen too, after the window's tick ran (NeoForge's `ClientTickEvent.Post`): the window's `Game` only, so not on a server's own tick (`server_tick` is that moment). Client-side. |
| `client_tick_pre` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ClientTickEvent.Pre` | Once every client tick, paused or not and on the title screen too, before the window's tick runs (NeoForge's `ClientTickEvent.Pre`): the window's `Game` only, so not on a server's own tick (`server_tick` is that moment). Client-side. |
| `command` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | A command the local player typed or clicked in chat, without its slash, before the server and Sunrise's commands see it: set `ran` when the mod ran it. Client-side: a server never asks, not for command blocks, guests or the console. |
| `component_hover` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | `GuiGraphicsExtractor.componentHoverEffect`: the hover of the text component under the cursor, about to be drawn, in the chat, a book or a dialog. `hover` is the component's hover action: its text, an item or an entity. `x` and `y` are the cursor in GUI pixels. Set `cancel` and the game draws nothing for it: no text tooltip, no item tooltip and no entity lines. A mod can draw its own with `draw`. Left alone, a text hover is wrapped at half the screen's width (at least 200) as vanilla wraps it, an item hover draws its stack's tooltip (which asks `item_tooltip` and the `render_tooltip_*` hooks as any item tooltip does), and an entity hover shows its name, type and UUID while advanced tooltips are on. Asked each frame a hovered component shows, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `component_types` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Register namespaced NBT component types before content loads. Call `ComponentTypesEvent::register` with each owned ID, such as `"mymod:charge".into()`. IDs are checked together at startup: invalid IDs, the `minecraft` namespace and duplicate IDs are refused before the component registry is frozen. |
| `compost_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player added a compostable item to the composter at `pos`. |
| `compute_fog_color` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeFogColor` | Computed world fog RGB, after ordinary effects and before upload. Title panoramas skip it. Only finite RGB components in 0..=1 are accepted; alpha and distances stay unchanged. |
| `compute_fov` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.ComputeFov` | World field of view, after normal calculation and zoom and before projection/Nausea. Called for world rendering and near-plane overlays; hand/HUD FOV uses its own path. Only finite values in 1..=179 degrees replace the computed FOV. |
| `compute_fov_modifier` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ComputeFovModifierEvent` | Player FOV scale, once per client tick with a player, after vanilla effects and before zoom and easing. Invalid values or values outside 0.01..=10 revert to base. |
| `config_loading` | both | listen | `&dyn ConfigContext` | `ModConfigEvent.Loading` | A registered config was read (`ModConfigEvent.Loading`): `config` says whose, which file and its text, which the mod parses (TOML by convention). Every mod hears every config, so check `mod_id`. Fired where the config's kind loads (`register_configs`), a `Server` one before `server_about_to_start`. Receives a `ConfigContext` with settings reads and the scoped `ConfigFiles` service. Client and Common load once on their owning side, Server once per world. |
| `config_reloading` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `ModConfigEvent.Reloading` | A loaded config's file changed on disk (`ModConfigEvent.Reloading`): its new text. The game looks at the files' modification times once a second, on the window and on the server's tick; a file saved with the same text, removed or unreadable fires nothing and keeps its last text. Receives the polling game's `HostSide`; world queries return `None` if no world is open. A panic disables only the offending mod’s reload callback; later listeners still run. Live writes outside a journal stand. |
| `config_unloading` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ModConfigEvent.Unloading` | A `Server` config is dropped as its server stops (`ModConfigEvent.Unloading`), after `server_stopping`; the next world reads it again. `Client` and `Common` configs stay until the game quits. Receives the stopping game's `ServerHost`, before the final save and teardown. A panic disables only that mod’s unload callback, not other listeners or the rest of teardown. Live writes outside a journal stand. |
| `console_line` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Observe an operator-visible log line on a dedicated or integrated server. `level` is `INFO`, `WARN` or `ERROR`, `thread` names the logging thread, and `message` is the text without its timestamp, thread or level prefix. Lines from every logging thread are delivered in order on the server thread at the start of the next server tick. The tap starts on the first tick with an enabled listener; without one, no log is captured and no queue is allocated or locked in the idle path. A listener's own log calls are not tapped. The queue keeps the oldest 4096 lines and discards later ones until it drains. `dropped` reports the number discarded on the first line of that batch and is zero on all other lines. `message` keeps its first 8192 bytes and `thread` its first 128, at a character boundary. A listener cannot change or suppress the log. |
| `consume_effect` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A custom entry of the stack's effective `consumable`, in list order, interleaved with built-in effects. `stack` is the read-only snapshot before consumption shrinks it, including components. `user` is the player or mob consuming it. An unclaimed kind does nothing. Register a custom kind's save codec and Wire value with `Registrar::register_consume_effect_type` before placing it in a stack that will be sent to a client. A registered kind's `data` holds the canonical fields returned by its save codec, with their exact NBT types. |
| `container_loot_filled` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Sunrise addition: no NeoForge counterpart. Fired once per container fill, each half of a double chest separately, on the rolled list in roll order before shuffle, splitting and slot placement. Add, replace or remove drops; empty stacks are dropped. Stacks over their item's maximum are trimmed at placement, after the hook; overflow that cannot fit is discarded. The opener is named only when a player's open starts the fill. Other accesses (including an `ItemPlace::EntitySlot` read, even during a player action) give None. The origin is the block's centre or the container entity's position. Already filled containers and block or mob drops do not fire this hook. Listeners run in dispatch order, each seeing the previous list, after loot_table_load's changes. The level Host provides reads only; only the drop list is writable. Non-negative damage within the signed integer range is accepted even above an item's maximum. Invalid component writes retain the prior list and warn. Panics follow the runtime listener isolation policy. Listeners use their own randomness. With no listener, the roll and placement random draws remain unchanged. |
| `container_opened` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player opens one of the game's own menus (`ServerPlayer.openMenu`; the job of NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent.Open`, which comes after the menu is open and cannot stop it): `kind` is the menu type (`minecraft:generic_9x3`, `minecraft:furnace`, `minecraft:crafting`, `minecraft:merchant`, `minecraft:anvil`, `minecraft:lectern`...; a horse's inventory is `minecraft:horse_inventory`) and `pos` the block it belongs to, none for a villager, a mount or a vehicle's chest. Heard after the checks that refuse the menu (a locked container, a spectator) and before the menu that was open closes. Set `cancel` and nothing opens: the open menu stays as it was and the click is spent. The player's state is swapped in for the call, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Not heard: a menu a mod opens (`native_open_menu`), the inventory screen, and the editors (sign, book, command block). Server-side, for every player of the world. |
| `crafter_craft` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A crafter at `pos` completed recipe `recipe`. |
| `create_fluid_source` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | `CreateFluidSourceEvent` | A fluid source at `pos` (`state`) is about to count towards turning the flowing fluid beside it into a source (NeoForge's `CreateFluidSourceEvent`, `FlowingFluid.getNewLiquid`): two counted sources beside a block over a solid block or a source make it one. Asked for each horizontal neighbour source, whenever the game works out a block's new fluid, spread checks included: answer fast. `can_convert` starts as the fluid's rule (`water_source_conversion` or `lava_source_conversion`). Server-side. |
| `create_skull_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.CreateSkullModels` | Skull models for a mod's own skull types (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.CreateSkullModels`): call `event.register(model)` with one `SkullModel` per type, giving its `kind`, model layer and default texture. A `minecraft:head` item model may then name the kind, so the head draws in hand, in a GUI and in an item frame. Called once at client startup. Any valid canonical `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:` for non-vanilla paths) is accepted; invalid identifiers, the seven built-in vanilla skull types (`minecraft:skeleton`, `minecraft:wither_skeleton`, `minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:creeper`, `minecraft:piglin`, `minecraft:dragon`), duplicate registrations, entries with a layer neither a mod nor vanilla has, or entries with an empty texture are logged and left out. API @API:create_skull_models@. |
| `create_spawn_position` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.CreateSpawnPosition` | Before a new server level selects its initial world spawn (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.CreateSpawnPosition`). |
| `creative_slot` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A creative-mode player sets a slot of their inventory from the creative screen (`ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.handleSetCreativeModeSlot`): `slot` is the inventory menu's slot (5 to 8 armour, 9 to 35 the inventory, 36 to 44 the hotbar, 45 the off hand; below 0 is a stack thrown out of the screen, which is dropped) and `item` (`minecraft:air` for an emptied slot) and `count` what is put there. Heard once the packet passed vanilla's checks (a creative player, a real slot, a valid stack) and before the slot changes or the stack drops. Set `cancel` and the packet is ignored. The player's state is swapped in for the call, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Not heard: stacks a creative player gets other ways (middle-click pick block, `/give`). Server-side, for every player of the world. |
| `creeper_ignite` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player ignited a creeper with an item. |
| `creeper_power` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Lightning charged a creeper. |
| `critical_hit` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CriticalHitEvent` | NeoForge's `CriticalHitEvent`: a player's melee hit on a living `target_kind` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:armor_stand`) that deals damage, where `Player.attack` decides whether it is critical. `vanilla_critical` is vanilla's answer; `critical` starts as it and may be forced or denied (as in vanilla a critical hit prevents the sweep, so a forced crit loses it; a denied crit loses the crit sound and particles); `damage_multiplier` starts at 1.5 for vanilla's critical and 1.0 otherwise, and scales the damage only while `critical` ends true. Not fired for a target that is no living entity (a dragon part) or when the hit does no damage. Fires on the Game that runs the attack. |
| `crop_grow_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `CropGrowEvent.Post` | A crop-like block grew (NeoForge's `CropGrowEvent.Post`). Diverges from NeoForge: original_state is always the pre-growth state. `state` is what is at `pos` now. Sugar cane reports the new cane above it, and kelp and vines the new head, while `original_state` names the growing plant before growth. A full-age stem fires even without fruit; sugar cane fires only when adding a cane, not when only aging. Cactus and chorus report the plant's position. Server-side. |
| `crop_grow_pre` | server | listen | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `CropGrowEvent.Pre` | A crop-like block at `pos` is about to grow on a random tick (NeoForge's `CropGrowEvent.Pre`): crops, stems, nether wart, cocoa, sweet berries and bamboo at their own position; cactus and chorus flowers at the block above; sugar cane at its own position; kelp, weeping and twisting vines and cave vines at the block they grow into. `state` is the growing block. Leave `result` at `Default` for vanilla's own chance, or set `True` to grow now or `False` to stop it (`DO_NOT_GROW`). Vanilla's random roll is drawn either way, in vanilla's order. Server-side. |
| `custom_click_action` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CustomClickActionEvent` | NeoForge's `CustomClickActionEvent`: a server player's client sent a `custom` click action (a dialog button or a text component's click event): its id and optional payload. Vanilla only logs it; set `cancel` to skip that. Only play-phase clicks are reported: one sent during configuration (no player yet) is not. The player is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `custom_spawner_tick` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ModifyCustomSpawnersEvent` | `CustomSpawner.tick` for a spawner a mod added with `modify_custom_spawners`: the level `dimension` ticks the spawner `key`, once per tick in the list's order, with `spawn_enemies` as `ServerLevel.isSpawningMonsters` (the `spawn_monsters` rule, and not peaceful). Every mod gets the call, and the mod that owns `key` spawns through `host` (its spawns go through `finalize_spawn` and the spawn checks like any other); the rest ignore it. It is called only while the `spawn_mobs` rule is on, as vanilla's custom spawners are, and only for a key in the list, so a level with no mod spawner pays one check per tick. Beyond NeoForge: a key and this hook stand in for the `CustomSpawner` object. Server-side. |
| `customize_boss_event_progress` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.BossEventProgress` | One boss bar is about to be drawn (NeoForge's `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.BossEventProgress`, in `BossHealthOverlay`): `bar` is the bar, `x` and `y` come in as its top-left corner in GUI pixels (91 left of the screen's centre; 12 for the first bar), and `increment` as the spacing to the next bar's `y` (19). Move `x` and `y` to draw this bar, and its title, there; set `increment` to space the next bar, which comes in that far below where this one came in (moving `y` does not move the bars after it). Set `cancel` and this bar is not drawn; the increment is still applied, leaving room for what a mod draws in its place. Asked once a frame per bar, in the order the server added them, until the next bar would start a third of the way down the screen; not under F1. Client-side. A bar the API can't carry (a server's name text the API refuses) isn't heard and is drawn as vanilla draws it. |
| `customize_chat_overlay` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.Chat` | The in-game chat is about to be drawn (NeoForge's `CustomizeGuiOverlayEvent.Chat`, in `Gui.renderChat`): `x` and `y` come in as the chat's bottom-left corner in GUI pixels (0 and the GUI height minus 40, vanilla's place); move them to draw the chat elsewhere. `focused` is whether the chat screen is open, and `gui_width`/`gui_height` the scaled GUI size. Asked once a frame the chat layer draws, focused or not; not while the chat is hidden (F1 with the chat closed). Only the drawing moves: the chat screen's input box, and clicks and hovers on messages, stay where vanilla puts them, as with NeoForge. Client-side. |
| `data_map_types` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterDataMapTypesEvent` | The server is assembling its data tables, as for `data_pack_files` (a world's first open and every `/reload`): push the data map types your mod registers (NeoForge's `RegisterDataMapTypesEvent`). A type is a namespaced `id` (`namespace:path`) for a `registry`: any the game names. Its values come from the world's data packs' `data/<namespace>/data_maps/<registry folder>/<path>.json` (the registry's path for a `minecraft` registry, `<namespace>/<path>` otherwise), and from files you push through `native_data_pack_files` under the key `data_maps/<namespace>:<registry folder>/<path>`. Each file has a `values` object mapping entry IDs and `#tags` to JSON, with an optional boolean `replace` and a `remove` list. Entry conditions (`neoforge:conditions`) are evaluated before the value, including on direct objects. A malformed layer is logged and skipped as a whole; otherwise newer values override older ones. Push a typed map's `DataMap::ty` and use the key's `data` (`ItemKey::data`, `EntityTypeKey::data`...) for an owned decoded value. By name, `data_map_value` borrows a raw tag and `data_map_entries` borrows the whole slice in entry ID order. Both borrow from the host; copy values only if you need them after the borrow. Server-side, not synced to clients. |
| `data_maps_updated` | both | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `DataMapsUpdatedEvent` | The data maps of a registry were reloaded on the server or synced to the client (NeoForge's `DataMapsUpdatedEvent`): the place to build caches from the new values or post-process them. `registry` is the registry's id (`minecraft:item`, `minecraft:block`) and `cause` says how they arrived. A listener cannot cancel or change anything. |
| `data_pack_files` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AddPackFindersEvent` | The server is assembling its data tables from the world's data packs (`ReloadableServerResources`): a world is opening, or `/reload` was run, and the recipes, tags, loot and advancements are built after this. Push `(path, bytes)` pack files and they join this load. `path` is the overlay's key, `<kind>/<namespace>:<path>` without `.json`, over the kinds the tables read: `recipe/mymod:thing`, `tags/mymod:block/thing`, `loot_table/…`, `advancement/…`, `trade_set/…`, `villager_trade/…`, `dialog/…`. A file that isn't JSON is skipped with a warning. Asked once per load on both paths, so this is where a mod recomputes them. Contributed files sit below the world's own data packs: a pack's file for the same path still overrides a mod's. Server-side (the single player's integrated server included). Listener applies run first. A later reload refusal preserves the old engine generation, but does not undo callback effects. Contributions share one list, so a failed file cannot be attributed to a particular mod. |
| `datapack_sync` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `OnDatapackSyncEvent` | Collect recipe types once the joining player exists, after admission and before that player's recipes are sent. On reload, asked once per online player, at most once per player per reload, before their recipes are re-sent. With nobody online this hook is not asked. `player` always identifies the recipient. Configuration tags precede admission. Only this player's Sunrise client receives the selected recipes; vanilla clients receive nothing new. Recipes using a vanilla serializer carry the server's resolved item candidates, not the source's ingredient definitions or bound predicates. A mod serializer still writes its own recipe content. An empty answer retains the client's own recipes, or restores them after an earlier nonempty answer. |
| `default_components_bound` | both | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `DefaultDataComponentsBoundEvent` | Items' default components are bound and readable (NeoForge's `DefaultDataComponentsBoundEvent`); `cause` says how, as `tags_updated`'s does. The place to build or rebuild caches derived from item defaults (stack sizes, food, tool rules): from now on the default-component reads return the published defaults, `modify_default_components`' edits included. |
| `difficulty_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `DifficultyChangeEvent` | The server's difficulty changed, from `from` to `to` (`peaceful`, `easy`, `normal`, `hard`; NeoForge's `DifficultyChangeEvent`). It is vanilla's `MinecraftServer.setDifficulty`, which `/difficulty`, a dedicated server's management and a test environment's difficulty all go through, and it is told only when the difficulty is another one. It only hears: the change has already happened. Server-side. |
| `dragon_egg_form` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | The Ender Dragon fight is about to place its first dragon egg at `pos`. Set `cancel` to leave the block unchanged. Server-side. |
| `effect_particle_modification` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `EffectParticleModificationEvent` | NeoForge's `EffectParticleModificationEvent`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.updateSynchronizedMobEffectParticles`: asked for each active effect `effect` of the living entity `entity` (a `LivingRef`, a mob or a player), the ones vanilla hides included, as the server is about to send their swirls in its synched data (`DATA_EFFECT_PARTICLES`, `DATA_EFFECT_AMBIENCE_ID`). `original_particle` is vanilla's for the effect: an `entity_effect` in its colour (alpha 38 of 255 when ambient), the omens' own particles. `particle` starts as it: replace it, or set it to `None` to go back to the original. `visible` starts as the effect's own: set it to show a hidden effect's swirl or hide a visible one. The visible effects' particles are sent in the order the effects are kept; particles that need a block state, and item, trail and vibration particles, can't be sent and are left out. A mod's own particle (`ParticleOptions::simple` with its registered `ParticleKey`) goes only to Sunrise clients: the others get the list without it. Whether the swirls are ambient (drawn fainter and rarer) follows from the answers as vanilla's `areAllEffectsAmbient` does: a visible effect that is not ambient makes it false, an effect a mod made visible counts as visible and one it hid doesn't, and it is true when none is visible. Asked when the entity's effects change (one is added, removed, or changes its amplifier, visibility or ambience), as vanilla recomputes them only then, and only while a mod listens; the answers are sent until the next change. The window's own player draws its swirls from its own effects, not from these answers. Server-side. |
| `emissive` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | At most 32 pairs of already-loaded base and transparent overlay sprites. Per-pixel alpha in the overlay marks full-bright material, without illuminating the surrounding block or changing gameplay light. |
| `enchant_effect` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterEvent` | A `minecraft:post_attack` effect of a mod's own kind, on a mob a player's weapon hit: the entry says `enchanted` attacker and `affected` victim, its `requirements` passed, and its `effect` names a `type` in a namespace outside `minecraft:`. Told the enchantment id, the kind, the `effect` payload as a borrowed `Tag`, the level and the mob's id (it may have just died). The acting player is the one whose weapon carries the enchantment. Each such entry is told once a hit, and once for each mob a sweep lands on. A hit on a player, and an entry inside `minecraft:all_of`, aren't told yet. An unclaimed kind does nothing. |
| `enchant_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player applied an enchantment at `pos`; `item` is the finished item and `levels` the levels charged. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `enchanted_block_loot` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | `EnchantedBlockLootEvent` | NeoForge's `EnchantedBlockLootEvent`: a block's loot table is reading the level of `enchantment` on `tool` to decide what `state` at `pos` drops, in `ApplyBonusCount.run` or `BonusLevelTableCondition.test`. `level` starts as the tool's level, after `get_enchantment_level`; what is left in it is what that one count or condition uses, and the tool keeps its own.
