A `u32` can't be negative, the case `setEnchantmentLevel` refuses; one above 1024 counts as 1024, as vanilla's levels stop at 255 and a level sizes a formula's loop. `tool` is none for an empty hand or a break with no tool, and the read is still asked, at level 0. `entity` is `LootContextParams.THIS_ENTITY`: the player mining, the mob breaking the block, the blast's direct source (primed TNT, TNT cart, exploding mob, fireball, wither skull, end crystal), the enderman whose carried block drops, the source of `/loot ... mine`, the projectile that broke a chorus flower or a decorated pot, the villager harvesting, the ravager walking through a crop, or none for a break with no entity (a neighbour update, a piston, water, a trident through a speleothem). A boat through a lily pad also gives none, though vanilla names it. Asked once per read, so a table may ask several times per break, or not at all, and the table's draws stay in vanilla's order. It runs inside the roll, so it gets the read-only level host: no writes, no draws, and `entity` is a handle a listener can keep and compare but not read until it has a `Host`. Authoritative servers only. Use `get_enchantment_level` when the block doesn't matter. Tick-time effect: one `listened` test per roll when no mod listens. |
| `enchanted_entity_loot` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EnchantedEntityLootEvent` | An entity's loot table reads an enchantment's level (NeoForge's `EnchantedEntityLootEvent`): asked in the middle of the roll, each time an `enchanted_count_increase` function or a `random_chance_with_enchanted_bonus` condition is evaluated, for a death and for `/loot ... kill`. `entity` is the living entity dropping the loot, `source` the killing blow (`minecraft:magic` for the command) and `attacker` the attacking entity (`getAttacker`): the one that caused the blow, or the command's source, even when `source` names nobody. It is None without one, and it need not be living: such a read is asked too, from a level of 0. `level` comes in as the living attacker's level of `enchantment`; what a mod leaves in it is what the table uses, so a level raised from 0 counts even with no attacker. A level can't be negative, and one above 1024 counts as 1024: vanilla's levels stop at 255, and a table's count grows with the level. Mods that don't need the entities prefer `get_enchantment_level`; a mob's equipment drop chance isn't this hook's (`get_equipment_drop_chance`). Not asked for a `/loot ... kill` of an entity that isn't living, nor for a table rolled any other way. The draws a mod makes from the level's random stay in order with the roll's. Server-side. |
| `enchantment_level_set` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EnchantmentLevelSetEvent` | An enchanting table at `pos` drew the level of one offer (`EnchantmentMenu.slotsChanged`, after `getEnchantmentCost`): `offer` holds the server player whose table it is, the row (0 to 2), the power (bookshelves counted), the item and the level drawn. Change `level` to change the offer; a level below `row + 1` offers nothing, as vanilla's. Asked for each row in order, before `prepare_item_enchant`. Server-side. |
| `ender_entity_teleport` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | `EntityTeleportEvent.EnderEntity` | An enderman or a shulker is about to teleport on its own (`EntityTeleportEvent.EnderEntity`). A mod may move `target` or cancel. It is given the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity (its registries, settings and mod list); it fires inside the tick's own AI step, before the teleport's own random draws, so it takes no `Host`. |
| `ender_pearl_teleport` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.EnderPearl` | A server player's or mob's ender pearl is about to teleport its `owner` (`EntityTeleportEvent.EnderPearl`, `ThrownEnderpearl.onHit`). `owner` is the live entity handle, `pearl` its projectile id, `from` the owner's feet and `target` the pearl's previous position. A mod may move the target; non-finite changes keep the original spot. Canceling leaves the owner in place with no teleport sound, damage or endermite roll; the pearl is used up. Player owners keep their acting-player context and mutable fall `damage` (5; nonpositive causes no health loss, but zero remains a hit attempt that asks the invulnerability predicate). Mob owners take no fall damage and make no endermite draw; their `damage` starts at zero and is player-only. An owner moved, removed, killed or put to sleep by a listener is not moved again using the old decision. The impact hook still hears the hit. What the pearl hit (`getHitResult`), in `projectile_impact`'s shape: the entity `hit_entity` (a dragon part is the dragon), or, with it None, the block `hit_block` on its `hit_face` (the face's normal); `hit_at` is the hit point. For an entity, `hit_block` is the cell of `hit_at` and `hit_face` is `[0, 0, 0]`. |
| `enderman_anger` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `EndermanAngerEvent` | `player` stares at `enderman` (NeoForge's `EndermanAngerEvent`, at the end of `Enderman.isBeingStaredBy`): the look is within the enderman's cone, in line of sight, and no carved pumpkin hides the face. Asked each time the enderman's goals check the stare: to get angry, to freeze while looked at and to teleport away up close. Set `cancel` and that stare doesn't count. Server-side. |
| `entities_load` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Entities from `chunk` finished loading into the server world. `count` is the number of saved entity records read. |
| `entities_unload` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Entities in `chunk` left the active server world and were queued for saving. `count` is the number of entity records. |
| `entity_break_door` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob broke the door at `pos`. |
| `entity_breed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Two mobs completed a breeding action. `child` is absent when vanilla lays an egg instead of creating a baby. |
| `entity_change_block` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob changed a block at `pos`, after the new state was applied. |
| `entity_change_block_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob or a player is about to change the block at `pos`: a landing's trampling (farmland to dirt, a turtle egg breaking), before its neighbour updates and events. `player` is the player whose landing it was, `entity` and `kind` the mob for a mob's landing (`None` and the player's type here). `from` and `to` are resolved block states. Set `cancel` and the block stays as it was and nothing the change would have caused happens, as Bukkit's `EntityChangeBlockEvent` (@API:entity_change_block_v2@). The fork names this pre-change veto `entity_change_block_pre`; `entity_change_block` is the separate post-change notice. |
| `entity_constructing` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `EntityEvent.EntityConstructing` | A new entity is being made (NeoForge's `EntityEvent.EntityConstructing`, vanilla's `Entity.<init>`): `kind` is its type (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player` for a player) and `holder` holds its attachments. Set attachments here and the entity starts with them: this runs before any mod reads them, and an entity read from disk or from a summon's NBT reads its saved attachments over what you set. The entity may not be in the level yet, so read nothing else of it. Nothing can be cancelled. |
| `entity_drop_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob is about to drop `count` of `item` at `pos` (Bukkit's `EntityDropItemEvent`). |
| `entity_enter_block` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A bee entered the hive at `pos` and was stored there. |
| `entity_enter_love_mode` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob entered love mode after player interaction. `player` is the feeder when known. |
| `entity_entering_section` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `EntityEvent.EnteringSection` | `entity` moved from the 16x16x16 section `old_section` into `new_section` (NeoForge's `EntityEvent.EnteringSection`, vanilla's `EntitySectionStorage` callbacks on `Entity.setPosRaw`). Both are section coordinates (`SectionPos`: each block coordinate shifted right by 4), and `chunk_changed` is NeoForge's `didChunkChange`: the x or z section differs, so the entity is in another chunk column. `host` is the side that saw the move. Nothing can be cancelled: the entity is already in its new section. |
| `entity_interact` | both | replace | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.EntityInteract` | A player right-clicks the entity `entity` (`Entity.getId`) of type `kind` (`minecraft:cow`) with `hand` (`Hand::Main` or `Hand::Off`), holding `stack` (none in an empty hand): where `Player.interactOn` starts, before the entity's own `interact` and the item's `interactLivingEntity`. Both logical sides answer independently, once per hand, main hand before off hand. A consuming client result ends the loop; only Success swings there. SuccessServer and Consume consume without a client swing. Fail, Pass and TryWithEmptyHand fall through on this entity path. A server-only answer leaves the client's off-hand prediction unchanged. ClientHost permits reads and local effects, not authoritative writes. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `entity_interact_specific` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.EntityInteractSpecific` | a right-click on an entity with the point clicked: `entity_interact_specific` hears the entity, its type, the held stack and the point clicked, measured from the entity's position, before `native_interact_entity` (NeoForge's `PlayerInteractEvent.EntityInteractSpecific` counterpart); a claim swings the hand and ends the click. Server-side. Bundles need "hooks": @API:entity_interact_specific@. |
| `entity_invulnerability_check` | both | listen | `LevelSide` | `EntityInvulnerabilityCheckEvent` | Decide whether an entity is invulnerable to this specific damage source (`Entity.isInvulnerableTo`). `original_invulnerability` is the vanilla answer and `invulnerable` starts with that value. Listeners may change it before the hit is applied. This fires once per player or mob hit that enters the invulnerability predicate, including creative/PvP, death, fire-resistance, shield and cooldown refusals. Nested player predicates and equipment continuations reuse the same base decision; a new attempt asks again. Ghast and warden overrides are observed too, including reflected fireballs and digging. No amount is supplied: vanilla's predicate takes a source, not an amount. Amount mutation remains on `living_incoming_damage`, before cooldown. Zero-damage projectile collisions and player pearl teleport damage still ask before the zero-damage refusal. Skull impact and its explosion are separate attempts. Fireworks directly hurt their attached entity; their splash query excludes spectators, not dead or unloaded residents. |
| `entity_join_level` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityJoinLevelEvent` | An entity joins the server level `dimension` (NeoForge's `EntityJoinLevelEvent`): vanilla's `PersistentEntitySectionManager.addEntity`. `loaded_from_disk` is true for an entity coming back with its chunk, false for one added fresh (`addFreshEntity`: a drop, an orb, `/summon`, an arrival through a portal). `entity` is its ID and `kind` its type (`minecraft:item`). It is already in the level's lists, so helpers that take an entity ID find it. Set `canceled` to keep it out: it is taken back out as if never added, and a `/summon` of it fails. Tells every kind in the game's entity lists (items, orbs, mobs, boats, minecarts, armor stands, primed TNT, falling blocks, projectiles, rockets, tridents, end crystals, the dragon, cushions, paintings, item frames, block/item/text displays, markers and interactions), once each, in the order they were created; check `kind`. A drop, an orb, a mob, a `/summon` and a chunk's entities are told at once, any other spawn between the tick's phases (it is checked after the blocks, the vehicles, the projectiles, the items and the mobs, and at the tick's end). A mod that cancels one of the others sees it removed before it ticks again. Inside a transaction (`Host::transaction`) opened in the entity's own level the join is told at that transaction's root commit, not at the spawn call (an entity spawned in another level, as by a command run `in` it, is told at once): before the commit the entity exists and `entity_constructing` has run, but no one is told it joined, and a listener reads the entity when the commit runs. An aborted transaction tells nothing, and the entity it spawned is gone. A join cancelled at the commit leaves the spawn's handle stale. Joins at one commit come in the order the entities were added, before the removals the transaction asked for take effect. Join notifications in the same level requested while a commit is announcing its joins (from a direct spawn or a committed transaction in that level) are delivered after that announcing batch, in request order. Live fallback-store entities also notify: displays, markers, interactions, and summoned or chunk-restored eyes of ender and explicit leash knots. Gameplay-thrown eyes and naturally tied leash knots live in separate lists and do not notify yet. `loaded_from_disk` is set when a chunk restores an entity. Saved data kept for a missing mod is not an entity in the level and does not notify. Server-side. |
| `entity_leave_level` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityLeaveLevelEvent` | An entity leaves the server level `dimension` (NeoForge's `EntityLeaveLevelEvent`): vanilla's `onTrackingEnd`, when it is removed for any reason (picked up, merged, despawned, burnt, blown up, killed, taken by a hopper or a mob, gone through a portal) or its chunk unloads, a level closing with nobody in it included. Not for the entities still loaded when the server stops, as in vanilla. `entity` is its ID and `kind` its type (`minecraft:item`); it is no longer in the level's lists. It only hears. It tells every kind `entity_join_level` does (`kind` is the type), each once, when the game next notices it gone (the tick or action that removed it). Inside a transaction (`Host::transaction`) opened in the entity's own level an entity's removal takes effect, and this hook fires, at that transaction's root commit, not at the removal call (an entity in another level, as by a command run `in` it, leaves at once): from the call on its handle and selectors no longer find it. An aborted transaction removes nothing and tells nothing. An entity the transaction spawned and removed is never told to join, so it never leaves. Check `kind`. Server-side. |
| `entity_mob_griefing` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `EntityMobGriefingEvent` | May `entity` change the world? (NeoForge's `EntityMobGriefingEvent`, `EventHooks.canEntityGrief`.) `can_grief` starts as the `mob_griefing` game rule; set it either way. Asked for a mob explosion (`ExplosionInteraction.MOB`, for the creeper, wither, ghast fireball or wither skull), a mob picking up items, an enderman taking or placing a block, a sheep eating grass, a mob trampling farmland or turtle eggs, a ravager breaking leaves or crops, a wither breaking blocks or leaving a wither rose, a silverfish leaving or entering stone, a snow golem's snow, a fox's berries, a rabbit's carrots, a villager's farming, an evoker's sheep, a zombie trampling eggs, an allay's pickup, and a mob's arrow or small fireball changing a block (asked for its owner), a zombie breaking a door, the dragon breaking blocks, a ravager breaking pitcher crops, a burning mob melting powder snow, a weaving mob's cobwebs, and decorations and items in explosions (asked for the exploding entity, and for the mob that causes the blast). Asked often: answer fast. Server-side. |
| `entity_mount` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityMountEvent` | An entity mounts or dismounts a vehicle (NeoForge's `EntityMountEvent`, posted from `Entity.startRiding` and `removeVehicle`): `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`) in the server level `dimension`, getting on (`mounting`) or off the `vehicle` of type `vehicle_kind` (`minecraft:oak_boat`, `minecraft:minecart`, `minecraft:horse`, `minecraft:cushion`). Set `canceled` and it does not: a player stays where they were, a mob does not board, a `/ride` fails, and a rider whose dismount is canceled keeps riding. Players hear boarding and dismounts by input, `/ride`, hits, bucking, teleport or spectator mode; mobs hear boarding and dismounts (jockeys, ravager raiders, `/ride`, hits), including removal cleanup. The departing rider's own dismount is asked first, then its direct passengers in passenger-list order. The removed vehicle is no longer a valid handle or query result during these callbacks. A Mob's canceled dismount keeps the relationship with a removed boat, cart or mob; each later dismount asks again. A resident Mob retries immediately before its next eligible entity tick: its chunk must be entity-ticking and its tick must not have been canceled. A server player retries on its next vehicle tick. Either can be later in the same world tick. A rejected, nonresident passenger does not autonomously tick or retry. A mob riding a player is compulsorily ejected on save, logout, death or dimension change: the dismount notifies this hook, but cancellation cannot keep the mob aboard. Loading a save does not notify. A mob that boards a boat by touching it is checked again after the hook returns without canceling: the boat is found again by id (gone: the contact pass for that boat ends), its passengers are counted again, and the mob must still exist, not be removed and not already ride; a full boat or an unfit mob skips that mob. A player who sits on a cushion has the cushion found again by id after the board and dismount checks: gone, the click is spent and the player is not seated. Server-side. |
| `entity_move` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | One physical `Entity.move` invocation (#2414, design #2308, the maintainer ruling of the same issue): the entity `entity` of `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`) is about to move by `delta` (blocks this tick) for `cause`'s sake - itself, the player behind a position packet, a piston, a shulker box lid, a shulker mob's opening lid, a moving collider carrying or shoving the body. It fires before the move's own handling, no-physics and the stuck multiplier included, in the one gate every kind's physics resolves through, so a forgotten family cannot move around it. Change `delta` to replace the movement (still through every ordinary restriction) or set `event.cancelled` or return `Flow::Cancel` to skip it: no position, velocity, collision-flag or fall change, and no displacement notice. Replacement components must be finite; there is no additional per-axis limit. A non-finite replacement rejects that listener's delta and veto together and logs the mod and field. An unchanged game delta is not revalidated. An earlier accepted veto cannot be cleared. If a listener panics or incurs a phase refusal, its edits and cancel flag are discarded; earlier results stand and later eligible listeners still run. Only a panic disables this mod's callback for the session. It takes no Host - the maintainer ruling, like the enderman's teleport: the listener only decides the delta, and its `context` is the loop `context` of the side that ticks the body. The hook fires only on the server. The client predicts ordinary collider displacement for its own player, not a listener's replacement. The server remains authoritative. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. |
| `entity_outline` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Whether an entity the client draws has the glowing outline, and in which colour (`Entity.isCurrentlyGlowing` and `getTeamColor`, what Fabric's and NeoForge's glowing and team colour overrides change). `outline` comes in as vanilla's answer: `Some(0xRRGGBB)`, the team's colour or white, when the entity glows, None when it doesn't. Set `Some` to make any entity glow in that colour, None to take its outline away. It changes only what this client draws: the entity's `Glowing` flag, what the server and other players see, stays. Asked each frame for every mob, player (yourself in third person when you hold the camera), dropped item and projectile drawn. In the ordered body pass, these queries for mobs, players and dropped items follow level join order, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions. Projectile queries run in a later pass. General entities, the dragon and end crystals add no outline queries. API @API:entity_outline@. |
| `entity_place` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BlockEvent.EntityPlaceEvent, BlockEvent.EntityMultiPlaceEvent` | `BlockEvent.EntityPlaceEvent` (and `EntityMultiPlaceEvent`, which extends it): `placer` set the block `state` at `pos` against `placed_against`, before it is written. `placer` is the player for `BlockItem.place` (before the stack shrinks), or the mob for an enderman putting its carried block down and a snow golem's trail; `None` is reserved for a placement with no entity. `placement` is `Single`, or `Multi` with every block the placement writes (a bed's foot and head, a door's halves, a tall plant), where `state` and `pos` stay the primary block (the foot, the lower half). For players, `placed_against` is the state adjacent to the primary placement position opposite the clicked face, including when the clicked block is replaceable. For mobs it is the state below the placement. Cancel stops the whole placement: no block is written, the stack is kept and an enderman keeps carrying its block. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: replaces the listen-only `place_block`; a mod that stopped placements by claiming `native_use_on` cancels here. |
| `entity_portal` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server entity finished its portal wait and is beginning to leave `dimension` through `pos`. The destination follows the portal rules. |
| `entity_pose_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest player's synchronized pose changed from `from` to `to`. |
| `entity_shoot_bow` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player bow or crossbow launched an arrow with `force`, after the projectile entered the level. |
| `entity_size` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `EntityEvent.Size` | An entity's dimensions are being resolved for `pose` (NeoForge's `EntityEvent.Size`, fired from vanilla's `Entity.refreshDimensions` and from the entity's constructor): it is new, its pose changed to `pose`, it grew up, its `minecraft:scale` changed, or another value its vanilla size follows changed; or as a player's pre-motion candidate (version 2, below). `size` (width, height and eye height, in blocks) starts as vanilla's new size and `old_size` is the size it had. Change `size` and the entity's box and eyes take it; later mods see earlier mods' edits. The answer holds while the entity keeps that pose and vanilla size; the next change asks again, starting from vanilla's. Changing the width or height leaves the eye height as it is: set it too. Zero-size answers are valid. An answer is ignored if any component is non-finite, negative or greater than 32 blocks. This bounds collision and pathfinding work while allowing sizes larger than naturally occurring vanilla entities. `host` reads the side that asks; the entity may be new, so read only what you need of it. |
| `entity_struck_by_lightning` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityStruckByLightningEvent` | A lightning bolt is about to hit `entity` (NeoForge's `EntityStruckByLightningEvent`, before `Entity.thunderHit`): once per bolt strike for each entity whose box meets the strike box (3 blocks around, 9 up), players included. `bolt` is the lightning bolt. Set `cancel` to spare the entity: no fire or damage, and no conversion (pig, villager, mooshroom, creeper, copper golem). It still counts as struck for Channeling's advancement. Not asked for a visual-only bolt, which hits nothing. Server-side. |
| `entity_tame` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob became tamed after its owner and persistent state were set. |
| `entity_teleport` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server entity was moved by a same-level teleport command, after its position changed. `entity` is its network id; `from` and `to` are coordinates in `dimension`. This hook reports command teleports that use Sunrise's same-level target path; it does not change vanilla's movement. |
| `entity_tick_listened` | both | collect | `HostSide` | — | Whether a mod listens to `entity_tick_pre` and `entity_tick_post`: a mod that hooks them hooks this one too and sets `listened` (HEAD, `*listened = true`). Without it the game tells no entity's tick, and keeps no list of them. Asked once for each family each tick, on the server and on a client (`host` says which side). Contract version 2: version 1 was asked on the server only. |
| `entity_tick_post` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `EntityTickEvent.Post` | The entities `entities` ticked in the level `dimension`, and `kinds` holds the type of each, in the same order (NeoForge's `EntityTickEvent.Post`): those `entity_tick_pre` was told and did not cancel, also one that died or was removed in its tick. An entity `entity_tick_pre` was told alone is told alone here too, right after its own tick; a family is told once, after its last tick. Told where `entity_tick_pre` is, on the server and on a client (`host` says which side). Version 3 observes additional families without changing the event signature. |
| `entity_tick_pre` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `EntityTickEvent.Pre` | The entities `entities` are about to tick in the level `dimension`, and `kinds` holds the type of each (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:item`), in the same order (NeoForge's `EntityTickEvent.Pre`, posted right before each entity's tick in `tickNonPassenger`). Told on the server for the mobs, players, item entities, minecarts, boats, general entity rows and projectiles that tick in an entity-ticking chunk, and the entities the server keeps without simulating them (displays, markers, interactions), whose tick is only told (not a player's own boat or cart, which ticks with the player); and on a client for the mobs, other players, projectiles, tridents and displays it ticks; `host` says which side. Minecarts, boats and general rows are each told alone. Eligible types named at registration with `entity_tick_each` (or requested through `entity_tick_each_entity`) are also told alone. Other observed entities are told once per family. On the server, player Pre runs before the observed parts of `ServerPlayer.tick`, not the connection body's `player_tick_pre`. The local client player is not yet observed here. Push an ID from `entities` onto `canceled` to skip that entity's observed tick work and suppress its Post. IDs outside this call are ignored; repeated IDs count once. A mod using these hooks also opts in through `entity_tick_listened`. Removing a general entity during its own Pre skips its tick and Post and dismounts its passengers unless dismount is vetoed. An eligible former passenger can still tick as a root in this pass if it has not ticked and its join-order slot is still ahead. An already-passed slot is not revisited. Version 3 expands the version 2 coverage without changing the event signature. |
| `entity_toggle_glide` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's fall flying state changed on the server. The change has already been applied. |
| `entity_travel_to_dimension` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTravelToDimensionEvent` | `entity` is about to travel to the level `dimension` (NeoForge's `EntityTravelToDimensionEvent`), before anything of it moves: the entity is still where it is, in its own level. `cause` is why: `nether_portal`, `end_portal` or `end_gateway` for a portal, or `teleport` for a teleport that changes level (`/tp` and `/teleport`, a spectator's teleport to a player in another level). Set `cancel` to keep it where it is: a portal's wait is forgotten, a teleport is dropped and `/tp` still reports success. An End gateway leads to the level the entity is already in, so its `dimension` is the entity's own. |
| `entity_unleash` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A leashed mob's leash was removed, before any lead drop is handled (Bukkit's `EntityUnleashEvent`). |
| `exp_bottle` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An experience bottle broke and its experience was awarded. |
| `experience_orb_can_merge` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Whether the experience orb `into` takes the orb `from` in (vanilla's `ExperienceOrb.canMerge`): the same value, and ids a multiple of 40 apart. Asked for each pair whose boxes meet, when an orb spawns (`tryMergeToExisting`: `from` is 0, the new orb isn't an entity yet, and only `from_value` is known) and every 20 ticks as an orb scans for neighbours (`scanForMerges`). `can_merge` holds vanilla's answer; change it either way. A merged orb counts as `count` orbs of `into`'s value, so forcing orbs of different values together drops points unless the mod raises `into`'s value with `native_set_experience_orb` in `experience_orb_merge`. Server-side. |
| `experience_orb_merge` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The experience orb `into` (`into_value` points, `into_count` orbs) is about to take `from` (`from_value` points, `from_count` orbs) in, after `experience_orb_can_merge` said yes (`from` is 0 and `from_count` 1 for a new orb that is spawning). Set `canceled` to stop it: both orbs stay, and a spawning orb tries the next orb in reach, then spawns on its own. Left alone, `into` adds `from`'s count, as vanilla does. A mod that writes `into`'s value or count with `set_experience_orb` here makes that write the merged orb: `from` is taken in without adding its count, so `into_value = 11, into_count = 1` pays 11 once. The merge is refused, and `into` put back as it was (value, count and age), when `from` has changed or left reach by the time the merge applies. A merge whose count would pass `i32::MAX` (what a save holds) is refused the same way. A write the listener made to `from` itself is not undone. Server-side. |
| `explosion_detonate` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ExplosionEvent.Detonate` | An explosion at `center` in `dimension` (`minecraft:overworld`) is about to break `blocks` and reach `entities`: `ServerExplosion.explode` with its `toBlow`, before any block or entity is hit. Take out the positions to spare; they keep their block and get no fire. `entities` is `getEntities(source, box)`: every entity in the box the centre ± (2 × radius + 1), in walk order, without the exploding source, those that will ignore the blast included — the entity-list walk, then the item frames, paintings, cushions and end crystals it misses. Take one out and it is neither hurt nor pushed, and `explosion_knockback` is not asked for it; one a mod adds is walked as any other, and the list the listeners leave controls membership, subject to the usual distance, exposure and per-kind immunity checks. Order controls the ordinary-storage walk only, not global effect order: crystals/dragon and players run in separate earlier passes, and decorations run last in storage order. Block positions are shuffled before destruction, so their list order is not destruction order either. Server-side. |
| `explosion_knockback` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ExplosionKnockbackEvent` | An explosion at `center` in `dimension`, about to break `blocks`, pushes `entity` (None for a player, whom `host.players().acting_player()` names): `ServerExplosion.hurtEntities`, after the knockback is calculated. Change `knockback_velocity` to change what is added to the entity's velocity; a player's client is sent the same. Wind bursts too, except dragons and end crystals. End crystals are also omitted from ordinary blasts: Sunrise removes them without a velocity to change. Server-side. |
| `explosion_prime` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server explosion is about to begin at `center`, with `radius` (Bukkit's `ExplosionPrimeEvent`). This is listen-only: the explosion proceeds as usual. Server-side, including the single-player server. |
| `explosion_start` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ExplosionEvent.Start` | A server explosion is about to happen (`ExplosionEvent.Start`, in `ServerLevel.explode` before `ServerExplosion.explode`): every one, TNT, creepers, beds and anchors, fireballs, withers, end crystals and wind bursts. Set `cancel` and nothing of it happens: no blocks, no damage or knockback, no sound, particles or game event. Before `explosion_prime`, which a cancel skips. Server-side. |
| `extend_poi_types` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `ExtendPoiTypesEvent` | Add block states to an existing POI type (NeoForge `ExtendPoiTypesEvent`): a mod's bed to `minecraft:home`, its workstation to `minecraft:armorer`. Asked once the block registry is resolved, before any world's POI tables are built, and again on a resource reload. Build each predicate on `event.extensions.metadata()` and hand it to `event.extensions.extend(poi_type, states)`; a block predicate with no constraints means all of the block's states. The engine resolves the additions and refuses, one logged warning each naming both types, an unknown POI type and a state vanilla or an earlier addition already gives to a different type; the rest of a mod's additions still apply. A state added to the type it already has is a no-op. A tag predicate is refused: additions name blocks. A callback that panics or incurs a phase refusal contributes no predicates. |
| `extract_block_screen_effect` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ExtractBlockScreenEffectEvent` | Before an overlay from `ScreenEffectRenderer` is drawn, once a frame per eligible overlay: `Block` with the view-blocking `state` at `pos` the camera is inside, `Water` with water's default `state` at the eye's block `pos`, and `Fire` with fire's default `state` at the player's block `pos`. Never for a spectator; `Water` and `Fire` only in first person. Water eligibility comes from the player's eye fluid, sampled through death tick 20. While the death screen stays open after removal, extraction keeps that last sample. `camera_submersion` can still override it at the eye. Cancel to leave the vanilla overlay out, except that a selected fire renderer still decides whether its vanilla quads follow it. Use `ExtractBlockScreenEffectEvent::set_custom_renderer` to prepend or replace the quads. A failed listener leaves the previous choice and cancellation intact so later eligible listeners still run. A panic disables that mod's listener for the session and is reported in the log and the client error toast. `partial_tick` is world interpolation; `player_partial_tick` is local-player interpolation, which continues when the level is frozen. Client-side. |
| `farmland_trample` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BlockEvent.FarmlandTrampleEvent` | A player or mob that fell onto farmland is about to turn it to its base block (`BlockEvent.FarmlandTrampleEvent`, from `FarmlandBlock.fallOn`). `entity` is the landing living entity: read its pose, sneak state and type through the handle. It fires after vanilla's own chance and checks (the draw, the mob-griefing rule or spawn protection, and the size test), so a listener only ever sees a trample that was going to happen. Cancel and the farmland and its crop stay as they are; the entity takes its fall damage either way. |
| `fill_bucket` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player fills (`stack` an empty bucket) or empties a bucket at `pos`: `BucketItem.use`, where it asks `Level.mayInteract`. Set `canceled` and the bucket does nothing. Server-side, for every player of the world (`host.players().acting_player()` says who). |
| `finalize_spawn` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `FinalizeSpawnEvent` | NeoForge's `FinalizeSpawnEvent`: the new mob of type `kind` is at `pos` and `Mob.finalizeSpawn` (equipment, attributes, variants, the class's own setup) is about to run for `reason`. `spawner` is the spawner block for a spawner or trial spawner spawn, none otherwise (a minecart spawner too). `difficulty` is the local difficulty finalization reads: change it to change what the mob gets. There are two cancels. Return `Flow::Cancel` to skip finalization: no finalizeSpawn runs, and the mob still spawns uninitialised (no equipment, attributes or class setup). Set `spawn_cancelled` to stop the spawn: no mob is added and finalization does not run. It draws nothing itself; skipped finalization skips its draws, and with no listener or an untouched event the draws are vanilla's. Fired only where vanilla 26.3 calls `finalizeSpawn` on a mob the game makes, with vanilla's `reason` at that call: natural spawning, chunk generation's animals, spawners, trial spawners, patrols, raids and sieges, zombie reinforcements, a cat spawner's cat, phantoms, jockeys, `/summon` and `/raid`, spawn eggs and dispensers, the nether portal's piglin (`Structure`), a skeleton trap's horse and riders, an evoker's vexes and wandering traders. A conversion fires with `Conversion`, and a cancel there leaves the converted mob as it is. Not fired where vanilla creates the mob without `finalizeSpawn`: breeding and a baby from a spawn egg, an infested block's or effect's silverfish, a thrown egg's chick, a thrown pearl's endermite, a hatching egg's turtle, sniffer or tadpole, a copper golem built from blocks, a slime from the oozing effect and a lightning trap's horse. Nor for entities loaded from disk or structure mobs placed by world generation. `mob` is the mob itself, not yet in a level: read it through the host (`mob.kind(host)`, `mob.equipment(host, slot)`, `mob.attribute_base(host, id)`) and change it through `edits` (`edits.set_equipment(mob, slot, item)`, `edits.set_attribute_base(mob, id, base)`, tags and the custom name); the handle and the editor reach only this mob, and after the hook returns the handle answers `Expired`. Listeners run before finalization: finalization's own rolls (equipment, attribute bonuses, variants) still run after them and overwrite what a listener put in the same slot or attribute; `Flow::Cancel` keeps every edit, since no finalization follows. A mob finalization itself makes (a chicken jockey's chicken, a zombie horse's rider) is lent with its own handle. `group` is the pack's spawn group data (`SpawnGroupData`, `SpawnGroup`): what the listener leaves there is what finalization reads for this mob. Every site this hook fires at hands it `None`: the game threads no group data through a live pack; chunk generation's animals share it only inside the generator, out of the hook's reach. The host is the read-only one, and `edits` changes nothing but this mob: listeners run on replayed ticks, so they must be pure functions of their inputs and edit only `mob` and `group`. Server-side. |
| `firework_explode` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A firework rocket completed its explosion effects. |
| `fishing_medium` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | A fishing bobber `bobber` at `position` in `level` is flying or floating outside water (`FishingHook.tick`'s fluid test found none), and a mod may say it floats and fishes in a medium there: lava, the void under an island, a cloud. `owner` is the player who cast it and `owner_position` where they stand (their feet); a client may know neither for another player's bobber. Answer a `FishingMedium` to make the spot a medium, or None to leave vanilla's (the bobber falls). The first mod to answer, in dependency order, wins. |
| `flip_frame` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `FlipFrameEvent` | Once for each frame presented, in a world or not, after that frame's `render_frame_post` (NeoForge's `FlipFrameEvent`): the place to rotate per-frame GPU data. Not cancellable. A frame that was not presented does not fire it. Client-side. |
| `fluid_flow` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The current of the fluid block at `pos` (`FlowingFluid.getFlow`, the vector that pushes an entity standing in it) is about to push a player, mob, item, experience orb, armor stand or arrow. `fluid` is the fluid's id (`minecraft:water`, `minecraft:flowing_lava`...); a mod may change `flow`, so it can rotate, strengthen or still a current. It runs for each fluid block the entity touches, so keep it short. The vector the renderer reads to turn a fluid's texture is not changed. Fires on both sides, on the client's replayed ticks too, with the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity (its registries, settings and mod list): register the mod on both, and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs so prediction and the server agree. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `fluid_level_change` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | A flowing fluid at `pos` is about to change level. `fluid` is `water` or `lava`; the levels are 0 through 8. Set `cancel` to keep the old level. Server-side. |
| `fluid_place_block` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `BlockEvent.FluidPlaceBlockEvent` | Liquid at `liquid_pos` is about to place `state` at `pos`. Set `cancel` to prevent placement or change `new_state` to replace it. Server-side. |
| `fluid_tick` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | The fluid at `pos` is about to run its scheduled tick (`FlowingFluid.tick`). `fluid` is `water`, `lava` or a mod fluid's id. Set `cancel` and the tick does nothing at all: no level change, no spreading and no new tick. For a mod that simulates a fluid itself; `fluid_level_change` and `block_spread` still ask about each change of a tick that runs. A cancelled tick schedules nothing; a mod taking over must re-schedule itself. It's asked once per tick, so keep it cheap. Server-side. |
| `fluid_tooltip` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `FluidTooltipEvent` | `FluidTooltipEvent`: the tooltip of `stack`, a fluid the game draws a slot of (the recipe viewer's fluid wells), once its name, its amount in millibuckets and, with F3+H, its id are in `lines`, each with a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own). Add, change, reorder or remove lines, and an empty list draws nothing. It fires before the tooltip is laid out or drawn, with no item and no `item_tooltip` involved: a tank's or a fluid slot's tooltip has neither. Asked each frame the tooltip shows, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `food_level_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The food level of the server player `player` (`name`) is about to change from `from` to `to` (Bukkit's `FoodLevelChangeEvent`): eating, hunger and saturation effects, the exhaustion that costs a point, starving. Change `to` (kept between 0 and 20; saturation never exceeds it) or set `cancel` to leave the level at `from`, saturation included. Heard once for the change a player's turn made as a whole, so two changes in one tick arrive as one. Not heard: the full food bar a respawn gives, the food a player joins with and the host of a LAN world. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. On a server. |
| `framerate_limit` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The frame rate this window is about to be held to: Max Framerate after the inactivity throttle and Battery Mode (260 is unlimited). `focused` is whether the window has focus, `minimized` whether it is minimised and `in_world` whether a world is open. A mod may lower `limit`, as Dynamic FPS does for a window in the background: Sunrise keeps the lower of its own limit and the mod's, and ignores values below 1. |
| `furnace_burn` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A furnace at `pos` consumed fuel and began or resumed burning it. `fuel` is the item consumed from its fuel slot. Server-side. |
| `furnace_extract` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player took an output from a furnace at `pos`; `item` is the output item id and `count` is how many were taken. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `furnace_smelt` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A furnace finished smelting `item` into `result` at `pos`. Server-side. |
| `furnace_start_smelt` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A furnace at `pos` began cooking `item` (its cooking timer advanced from zero). Server-side. |
| `game_rule_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `GameRuleChangedEvent` | A server game rule `rule` (`minecraft:keep_inventory`) was set to `value` (`GameRuleChangedEvent`, from `GameRules.set` with a server: `/gamerule`, the game rules screen, the management protocol, a test environment, `server.properties`). `actor` is present when a player caused the change. `level.game_rules(host)` of any of the server's levels reads every rule, the new value included. Server-side. |
| `game_shutting_down` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `GameShuttingDownEvent` | The game process is shutting down, after the client or dedicated server has stopped. Fires once on the process's game thread on an orderly exit, with the host for that side. NeoForge's `GameShuttingDownEvent`. |
| `gather_effect_screen_tooltips` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `GatherEffectScreenTooltipsEvent` | `GatherEffectScreenTooltipsEvent`: the tooltip of the effect panel the cursor is over beside the survival or creative inventory. `tooltip` starts empty when the panel's information is fully visible, or holds the full name then duration when compact or clipped. Each line has a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own); add, change, reorder or remove lines, and an empty list draws nothing. `effect` is the effect the panel shows, `screen` the inventory. Asked each frame a rendered panel is hovered, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `gather_skipped_attribute_tooltips` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `GatherSkippedAttributeTooltipsEvent` | `GatherSkippedAttributeTooltipsEvent`: the attribute modifiers a stack's tooltip leaves out (`ItemStack.addAttributeTooltips`), asked once before its attribute lines are built. Set `skip_all` to hide every one, push modifier ids (`minecraft:base_attack_damage`, a bare name is `minecraft:`'s) to `skipped_ids`, or `EquipmentSlotGroup` names (`mainhand`, `armor`, `any`) to `skipped_groups`; a slot group with nothing left shows no heading. Display and resource-query builds skip it when `minecraft:tooltip_display` hides the attribute modifiers or the entire tooltip. `player` and `player_entity_id` are the local player's, or `None` for recipe book search and a stack nested in another's tooltip (a crossbow's loaded projectile). `advanced` (F3+H) and `creative` are the tooltip's `TooltipFlag`. Asked when lines are built for display, recipe book search or `ItemResource::tooltip`, except during reentrant line queries. Client-side, only while a mod listens. |
| `get_arm_pose` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IClientItemExtensions.getArmPose` | NeoForge's `IClientItemExtensions.getArmPose`: the pose of the arm that holds `stack` in `hand`, as a humanoid model draws it (local and remote players and humanoid mobs). `humanoid` borrows both held stacks, the main arm, use state and selected poses before listeners answer; item declarations are included in those poses. Set `pose` to a vanilla or registered custom pose and it replaces what the item would give, whatever the use animation, a loaded crossbow or the item's own `arm_pose` declaration say; leave it unset to keep those. Asked once a tick per humanoid model and non-empty hand, never for an empty hand. A two-handed pose (`bow_and_arrow`, `crossbow_hold`, `crossbow_charge`) still poses the other hand as vanilla's does. Vanilla's `spear` pose poses an arm only for an item with a kinetic weapon. Register a custom pose with `ArmPosesRegistrar`, return `ArmPose::Custom` here, and write its angles in `arm_pose_transform`. Unknown registration slots are ignored. Mob-specific animations still run after the humanoid base pose, as in vanilla. Client-side, with a `ClientHost`. |
| `get_enchantment_level` | both | listen | — | `GetEnchantmentLevelEvent` | `GetEnchantmentLevelEvent`: the enchantments gameplay sees on `stack`, starting from its own `minecraft:enchantments`. `target` is the one enchantment a read asks for (`ItemStack.getEnchantmentLevel`), and then only its level counts; `None` when every one is read (`ItemStack.getAllEnchantments`). Set, upgrade or remove levels in `enchantments`: what is left is what fights, mining, fishing, tridents, crossbows, armor, loot and every other enchantment effect use. Tooltips, the anvil, the grindstone, the enchanting table, saves and the network keep the stack's own component. Asked on every such read, often many times a tick, on the server and on a client (its tooltip modifiers). A client without this mod keeps its vanilla tooltip; server gameplay uses the modded levels. |
| `get_equipment_drop_chance` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `GetEquipmentDropChanceEvent` | A dying mob `entity` of `kind` is about to roll its drop chance for the item `stack` in `slot` (`Mob.dropCustomDeathLoot`, NeoForge's `GetEquipmentDropChanceEvent`). `original_chance` is `DropChances.byEquipment`; `chance` starts as the chance after the enchantments of the attacker and the victim (`getOriginalChance` and `getDropChance`); change `chance` (1.0 or more always drops, 0 or less never does). `preserved` is `DropChances.isPreserved`: the slot drops without a player kill, whatever the mod chooses. Each slot is asked at its own point of the loop, after the mob's loot table was rolled and just before the slot's roll, only when it holds an item and its recorded chance isn't 0, in `EquipmentSlot` order. The roll is drawn whatever the chance is, so the mob's random follows vanilla's order. `source` and `recently_hit` are as in `native_living_drops`. Server-side. |
| `grindstone_place` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `GrindstoneEvent.OnPlaceItem` | A grindstone at `pos` made its result after its inputs changed, for the server player `player` (UUID) and `player_entity_id` (`GrindstoneEvent.OnPlaceItem`, matching NeoForge `CommonHooks.onGrindstoneChange` in `GrindstoneMenu.createResult`), including the three input notifications of an accepted take: `inputs` lends the input stacks and vanilla result. Runs inline without a decided-action transaction or rollback on cancel. Cancellation is checked before converting `edits.output` and clears slot 2 without rolling back listener writes. Otherwise leave `edits.output` at `None` to keep vanilla's result and experience, or set a replacement stack and its `edits.xp` (any negative value falls through to vanilla's experience roll; nonnegative values set the award). An invalid replacement stack is logged and ignored, keeping vanilla's result. A panicking listener is caught and leaves vanilla's result. Server-side. |
| `grindstone_take` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `GrindstoneEvent.OnTakeItem` | The server player `player` (UUID) and `player_entity_id` took a grindstone's result at `pos` (`GrindstoneEvent.OnTakeItem`, matching NeoForge `CommonHooks.onGrindstoneTake` in `GrindstoneMenu.java.patch`): `inputs` lends the pre-take stacks. Runs inline after the click has taken the result, without a decided-action transaction or click rollback. `edits.xp` starts at the resolved experience (`>= 0`, from `grindstone_place` or vanilla's pre-rolled award); non-positive values (`<= 0`) spawn 0 orbs while still firing level event 1042 and replacing the inputs. Replacing each slot and finally notifying the input container each run `grindstone_place`. This completes before the click drops its taken stack or checks for another result. Use `edits.new_top` and `edits.new_bottom` to leave items in the input slots (invalid stacks are logged and ignored as empty slots). Set `cancelled` to skip experience, level event 1042 and input replacement; cancel is checked before converting remainders and does not undo the click or listener writes. A panicking listener is caught and completes the take with the initial experience and cleared inputs. Server-side. |
| `gui_layer_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiLayerEvent.Post` | After the complete body of a vanilla or mod HUD layer (`RenderGuiLayerEvent.Post`). Fires once with the ID from `gui_layer_pre`, even if F1 or another condition hid the vanilla drawing, or if a wrapper omitted its previous body. Does not fire when `gui_layer_pre` cancels the layer. If the body schedules deferred subtitle paint, Post still fires in the HUD walk, not when that paint runs. Use `event.draw` to paint after the body, or `gui_render_post` for a foreground pass independent of any layer. |
| `gui_layer_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiLayerEvent.Pre` | Before a vanilla or mod HUD layer (`RenderGuiLayerEvent.Pre`). Draw below the layer through `event.draw`, or return `Flow::Cancel` to skip its entire body, including side effects like the air-bubble sound, and `gui_layer_post`. Fires even when the layer's vanilla drawing is hidden. A wrapped or replaced layer fires this once, with the slot's original ID. To change a layer's body rather than veto the whole slot, wrap or replace it at startup in `register_gui_layers`. |
| `gui_render_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiEvent.Post` | After the complete normal GUI batch, including screens, prompts, toasts, debug overlays, the pad cursor and IME. Fires even with F1 or a menu open; file/world loading and resource-reload overlays do not fire it. The batch is in GUI pixels. `native_camera` is this frame's world camera (None on the panorama). `gui_hidden` is the F1 setting; `focused` is window focus. A mod decides whether its foreground indicator respects those flags. Client only; the host is read-only. A panicking listener's drawing is discarded. API @API:gui_render_post@. |
| `hanging_break` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An item frame (`minecraft:item_frame`, `minecraft:glow_item_frame`) or a painting (`minecraft:painting`), `kind` its entity type and `pos` the block it hangs in, is about to break: a server player's hit (`player`, their state swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` answers them), a blast, or the wall it hangs on gone (`player` None for both). Where `BlockAttachedEntity.hurtServer` and its survival check would kill it, before anything drops or sounds. Set `cancel` and it stays, as it was. A hit that only takes the item out of a frame does not break the frame and is not asked. The game's own player's hit is not asked, nor is `/kill`. The listener may change the world: straight after it returns without cancelling, the game finds the frame or painting again by its entity id; if it is gone, or an empty frame being broken now holds an item, nothing breaks and nothing is removed. |
| `hanging_place` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player places a hanging entity: an item frame (`minecraft:item_frame`, `minecraft:glow_item_frame`), a painting (`minecraft:painting`) or a leash knot (`minecraft:leash_knot`, when a lead is tied to a fence that has none yet), `kind` its entity type and `pos` the block it takes (the fence's for a knot). Where `HangingEntityItem.useOn` and `LeadItem.useOn` create it, once it is known to fit and before anything is placed or used up. Set `cancel` and nothing is placed: the item stays in the player's hand. The player's state is swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` answers them. Only for guests of a server; `/summon`, a dispenser and the game's own player are not heard, nor is a lead tied to a mob or a boat. |
| `hears_projectile_impacts` | server | collect | `&dyn Host` | — | Whether a mod hears projectiles' impacts (`native_projectile_impact`): set `hears`. Asked once a server tick before the projectiles fly; only then does the tick keep the copy of each flying projectile that a canceled impact flies on from, so a game without such a mod pays nothing. Server-side. |
| `hopper_inventory_search` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A hopper selected a container while searching for items to push or pull. Exactly one of `container` and `entity` identifies the target. |
| `hopper_pickup_item` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | A hopper at `pos` is about to pick up `count` of `item`. Set `cancel` to leave the dropped stack in the world. Server-side. |
| `hotbar_attack_indicator_progress` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Edit the stock hotbar's attack indicator for this draw only (`Hud.extractItemHotbar`). After item extraction, `event.progress` starts at the local player's attack strength at partial tick zero, even if the camera follows another player. This runs at full charge too: `1.0` hides the indicator, while a lower value makes it visible. |
| `humanoid_setup_anim` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Once per actual `HumanoidModel.setupAnim` invocation, after head/walk/riding setup and before arm priority/posing. `event.parts` lends the live `crate::HumanoidPose` for this callback, not a snapshot to replace or keep. `get` reads a present base part's local position and rotation; `set_position` and `set_rotation` write all three components immediately on any present base part, matching `HumanoidModel.setupAnim` with no finiteness gate. Later listeners see edits from earlier ones. Later vanilla ITEM, attack, crouch, bob and swim animation still apply. Armor stands then replace rotations with their saved pose, preserving position edits. `humanoid` contains this invocation's held stacks and use state. Time is captured for its view; `game_time` is absent outside a level, never wall time. The Host is read-only. No frame deduplication: layers that actually animate again get another invocation; copying parent poses does not dispatch. |
| `ingredient` | both | replace | `&dyn DataLoadContext` | `ICustomIngredient` | Define one occurrence of a custom ingredient named by a non-`minecraft:` recipe entry. Return `None` for a kind you do not handle, or an `IngredientDefinition` with the ordered candidate IDs and a predicate that can capture the parsed metadata. The first enabled mod to answer, in dispatch order, owns the predicate. Returning `None` lets the next listener answer. If nobody answers, the recipe is refused with its file and kind. |
| `input_key` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.Key` | A key event after handling, the window's or the control socket's `key` (one press, then one release for a tap), including repeats and consumed shortcuts (`InputEvent.Key`). `key` is vanilla's name for the physical key (`key.keyboard.a`, `key.keyboard.unknown` for one it doesn't name; `getKey`), `layout_key` its name for the key the keyboard layout produced (`getKeycode`: AZERTY's key at QWERTY's W gives `key.keyboard.z`; a named key such as Enter keeps the physical name; `None` for a character other than one ASCII letter or digit, a dead key's accent included since it arrives as its character, and for a key vanilla doesn't name) and `raw_code` a code telling physical keys apart, named or not (`getScanCode`: the platform scan code, stable within a platform only). `action` is what it did, and the modifier bits the screen hooks' GLFW encoding. Not cancellable. |
| `interaction_key_mapping_triggered` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.InteractionKeyMappingTriggered` | An interaction mapping is about to act on the tick's crosshair target. `attempt` distinguishes a queued press from held use or mining. Use asks for each hand tried, main first. Cancelling the main hand ends the click. Attack and use honour `swing_hand` independently: it governs only the acting player's own screen. False suppresses natural local swings; true preserves natural no-swing outcomes. A cancelled use with true requests a local swing only: neither a use packet nor a swing packet is sent, so observers see no swing. An uncancelled use sends no extra client swing packet: observers see the server's success swing, regardless of this flag. Attack and mining swing packets also honour the item swing veto. Pick only vetoes its packet and never swings. Fires on the window client. |
| `inventory_block_start` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player is about to open an inventory supplied by a block at `pos`. Set `cancel` to leave the inventory closed. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `inventory_move_item` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | — | A hopper is about to move one item between containers, a composter's bone meal included. Set `cancel` to leave it in its source slot. A chest boat or minecart is reported at the block it is in. |
| `item_attribute_modifier` | both | listen | — | `ItemAttributeModifierEvent` | `ItemAttributeModifierEvent`: the attribute modifiers `stack` grants (`ItemStack.getAttributeModifiers`), starting from its own `minecraft:attribute_modifiers` or its item's. Add, replace or remove entries in `modifiers`; what is left is what every reader of the stack's modifiers uses: the equipment of players, mobs, armor stands and the dragon when it changes, mobs' gear knockback and pickup choices, the attribute sync and the tooltip. A worn stack is asked again only when it changes (`ItemStack.matches`), so the answer must be the same for the same stack. Asked on the server and on a client (its tooltips). A client without this mod keeps vanilla modifiers in its tooltips; the server applies the mod's answer to attribute sync. |
| `item_consumption_teleport` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.ItemConsumption` | A server player or fox that ate chorus fruit (or another item with the `teleport_randomly` consume effect) is about to try a random spot (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent.ItemConsumption`, vanilla's `TeleportRandomlyConsumeEffect.apply`): once per attempt (up to 16), after they stopped riding. `from` is their feet, `target` the spot drawn; a mod may move it (its y kept inside the level's height, a value that is not a number ignored), and the game still looks down from it for ground it may land on. Canceling ends the effect: no teleport and no more attempts. `consumer` names the living actor through its handle. `consumed` is the stack captured before consumption, including its count and components; it is not reread from the hand after listener writes. A player's state is swapped in (`host.players().acting_player()`). A fox's callback has the full server host before its living AI/movement step and before the next mob ticks. Listener writes survive; changing the consumer's item, position or eligibility refuses the captured effect instead of overwriting that write. Cancellation still finishes consumption. A consumed stack the API can't carry (a saved item whose id isn't registered) isn't heard: the listeners aren't called for that consumption and the teleport goes on as vanilla's. |
| `item_crafted` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.ItemCraftedEvent` | A player took a crafting grid's result (`ResultSlot.onTake`, NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.ItemCraftedEvent`): `player` is the player's uuid, `recipe` the recipe the grid matched (`minecraft:oak_planks`) and `stack` the result it took, with its components. `inputs` is the grid row by row as it was before this take used it (4 cells for an inventory's grid, 9 for a crafting table's; `None` for an empty cell): a shift-click that crafts several times is heard once per craft, each with the grid before that craft. It only hears: the take has already happened. It fires for the crafting grid of an inventory or a crafting table, on the integrated server and a dedicated server, guests included, and not for a stonecutter's, a smithing table's or a furnace's result. Server-side. |
| `item_despawn` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A dropped item entity reached its 6000-tick lifetime and is about to be removed (Bukkit's `ItemDespawnEvent`). |
| `item_expire` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemExpireEvent` | A dropped item reaches its lifetime on the server (`ItemEntity.tick`, NeoForge's `ItemExpireEvent`). Every mod may set or add to `extra_life`, in ticks, which starts at zero; a positive total keeps the item that much longer, zero or less lets it expire. Extensions are capped at 32766 ticks. |
| `item_fished` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemFishedEvent` | A server player `player` (UUID) and `player_entity_id` reels in a catch: `ItemFishedEvent` in `FishingHook.retrieve`, after fishing loot and its modifiers roll. `fished` holds the bobber's id and position; listeners may change `drops` and set absolute non-negative `rod_damage` (`damageRodBy`): vanilla uses 1, or 2 with the bobber on the ground. After `player_fish`'s reel-in, before rewards. Server-side. The final list reaches `fishing_rod_hooked`, then each stack spawns its item, one experience orb, and its fish statistic if it belongs to `#minecraft:fishes`. Empty stacks are removed. A cancel removes the bobber and skips the trigger, items, experience and statistic; the rod still takes `rod_damage` last. An emptied list without cancel gives no items, experience or statistic; the trigger still fires with an empty list and the rod is still damaged. An invalid `drops` write (a stack the game refuses, such as a count over the item's maximum) discards the whole event's write-back, every listener's `cancel` and `rod_damage` included, and logs a warning naming the hook and the conversion error. Diverges: Sunrise lets a listener change the drop list itself; the reference lets a listener change the stacks but not the list. |
| `item_model_property` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RegisterConditionalItemModelPropertyEvent` | Client-side evaluation of a mod's namespaced conditional item model property. Set value only for a property your mod owns; unhandled properties stay false. The owned stack snapshot is the one being resolved, including nested bundle items. `bundle_selected` is the runtime bundle selection index; -1 means none. |
| `item_picked_up` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemEntityPickupEvent.Pre` | `player` touches the ground item entity `item` (`ItemEntity.playerTouch`), before its pickup delay and target are checked: it is asked on every tick the two touch, even while the item can't be taken. Leave `can_pickup` at `Default` for vanilla's rule, set `True` to take it despite its delay or another player's target (a full inventory still leaves it), or `False` to leave it there: the fork sets a vanilla eligibility delay of at least 10 ticks. The hook still runs on each touch, and a later `True` overrides that delay. The last mod to set it decides. The handle reads the whole stack on the ground; for the window's own player of a world it ticks itself, whose pickup pass lifts the items out of the level, it reads as unavailable. Server-side. |
| `item_picked_up_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemEntityPickupEvent.Post` | `player` picked up all or part of the stack of the ground item entity `item` (`ItemEntity.playerTouch`, right after `Inventory.add` took some of it), the entity `item_picked_up` named. `original` is a copy of the stack as it lay before the pickup: changing it does nothing. `remaining` is what the item entity still holds: `None` (empty) after a whole pickup, the part that did not fit after a partial one. Set a stack there to leave the entity holding it, or `None` to empty it: the pickup then counts `original`'s count less what is left. The amount taken is `original`'s count less `remaining`'s. A mod that removes the item entity meanwhile keeps it removed: no take animation, the stat and criteria still run. Not called when `item_picked_up` refused the pickup or the inventory took nothing; a forced `True` from `item_picked_up` changes none of this. A creative player always takes the whole stack. Server-side. |
| `item_smelted` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.ItemSmeltedEvent` | `FurnaceResultSlot.checkTakeAchievements` ran for a server player at a furnace, blast furnace or smoker's result slot: `player` is their UUID and `player_entity_id` their network id; `stack` and `amount_removed` are what vanilla passes there (`getSmelting`, `getAmountRemoved`). A click or a throw passes the taken stack and its count; a shift-click passes the slot's stack as it was with the count moved, then what stayed in the slot (`None` once it is empty) with 0; a hotbar swap passes the taken stack with 0. Fires before `furnace_extract`. Server-side. |
| `item_spawn` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A dropped item entity was successfully added to the server level. |
| `item_stacked_on_other` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` | NeoForge's `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` and vanilla's `Item.overrideStackedOnOther` / `Item.overrideOtherStackedOnMe`, fired inside a resolved pickup click (a direct left/right `Pickup` when no drag is active, or a one-slot left/right `QuickCraft` drag release; never on multi-slot or middle-button drags, active-drag resets, or creative catalogue slots), with the cursor and both stacks frozen in `event`. |
| `item_tooltip` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ItemTooltipEvent` | `ItemTooltipEvent`: the whole tooltip of `stack` (`ItemStack.getTooltipLines`), once its name, the item's own lines, the components' lines, the advanced lines and the operator warning are in `lines`, each with a colour as `rgba` packs it (`u32::MAX` keeps the text's own). Add, change, reorder or remove lines. `player` and `player_entity_id` are the local player's, `stack` borrows the stack lazily (`ItemStackView::project` copies components on demand), `display` borrows the stack's `minecraft:tooltip_display`, `advanced` (F3+H) and `creative` (a creative tab's own slot) are its `TooltipFlag`, and `lines` mutates the live tooltip directly with per-edit `TextFormError` validation so one invalid edit leaves all other lines intact. Asked when item tooltip lines are built, for the game's display or `ItemResource::tooltip`. Never asked for a hidden tooltip, a crossbow's loaded projectile or a query made during item tooltip line construction. Queries outside line construction can dispatch this event, but never gather images or draw. Client-side. This global event is distinct from `ItemPart::tooltip`, which runs at the earlier `Item.appendHoverText` position, before component lines. Parts' lines therefore come first; this event sees the finished list including them, for any item, even one with no attached parts. Replacements are used in memory; they are not subject to serialized component limits. |
| `item_toss` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ItemTossEvent` | `ItemTossEvent`: `player` tosses the item entity `item` (`Player.drop`: the drop key, a menu's throw, what no longer fits in the inventory), not what scatters when they die. The entity is made, with its thrower, but has not joined the level yet: its handle reads and changes the whole stack, the pickup delay and the target, and those changes stay. Set `canceled` and it never joins the level; the items are already out of the inventory and are lost. A canceled toss awards no drop statistic, as vanilla awards one only when an item entity came of it. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: replaces the listen-only `item_dropped`, which fired for the drop key and menu throws only. |
| `items` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Declare items in code once at startup on each side, before resources load. Pass each `mod_api::Item` to `event.register`. The declaration belongs to the submitting package — in its own namespaces or in a content package it serves (`served_by`) — and works like `data/<id>/sunrise/item/<path>.json` in a content mod. A block's item is declared with its block, not here. `crafting_remainder` may reference items across the host and its served packages; duplicates across layers and declarations into a `side = "server"` package's namespace are refused. `register` returns a typed `DeclarationError` for invalid IDs or fields. The game records the refusal and stops startup even if a returning listener ignores it. A panic discards this listener's declarations and diagnostics without rejecting startup. |
| `key_mapping_pre` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.InteractionKeyMappingTriggered, InputEvent.Key` | A physical key or mouse button bound to the gameplay mapping `mapping` (`key.jump`, `key.attack`...) changes state while playing, before the game acts on it. `pressed` is true for a press (a key's repeat counts as one, as the game takes it) and false for a release. Cancel a press to drop it for every mapping bound to its input: the game holds nothing and acts on nothing. A release cannot be canceled, so what the game holds is always let go. Told for the keys and mouse buttons the window takes (agent control's included), after the window's own shortcuts (fullscreen, screenshot, debug keys), and only with no screen open; not for a controller, whose presses go straight to the mappings, nor for a mod's `crate::VanillaKeyMappingRef::set_down` or `crate::VanillaKeyMappingRef::click`, which do not change physical input. | `left_click_block` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.LeftClickBlock` | A player left-clicks the block `state` at `pos`, `face` the face hit (`[0, 1, 0]` is the top): where `ServerPlayerGameMode.handleBlockBreakAction` takes a dig's start, after its reach, spawn protection and world border checks, before `Block.attack` (a note block plays) or a creative break. | `left_click_empty` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.LeftClickEmpty` | The local player left-clicks empty space, with any main-hand stack. Client-side only; the server does not receive this event. |
| `level_load` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.Load` | `dimension`'s level opened on the server (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Load`, posted in `MinecraftServer.createLevels`): before any of its chunks (`chunk_load`), so a mod sets up what it keeps for the level here. Before `server_started`, this fires in Overworld, Nether, End order, with the level in place, regardless of the saved player's dimension. The next built-in is not available until after the current notification. Built-in levels stay open until server shutdown; custom levels that close while idle fire this again when they reopen. Explicitly opened runtime levels can fire this while parked: `event.dimension` names the opened level, not the Host's current level. Server-side. |
| `level_save` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.Save` | The end of `dimension`'s level save pass (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Save` in `ServerLevel.save`), for autosave, `/save-all`, pause and shutdown. Individual record failures are reported and do not suppress this hook; a failed awaited storage flush does. Server-side. |
| `level_tick_post` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LevelTickEvent.Post` | After a successful level tick pass on either logical side; never after a failed tick. `host.server_has_time()` says whether optional work still fits in the tick (`LevelTickEvent.hasTime`); always true on a client. |
| `level_tick_pre` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LevelTickEvent.Pre` | Before a level's tick pass on either logical side. `level` identifies the level. `host.server_has_time()` says whether optional work still fits in the tick (`LevelTickEvent.hasTime`); always true on a client. |
| `level_unload` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LevelEvent.Unload` | `dimension`'s level closed (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.Unload`, posted after `level.close()` in `MinecraftServer.stopServer`), after its last save: drop what the mod kept for it. Every level still open closes when the server stops, before `WorldCloseFinal`, and when its open or its loop fails, before `WorldOpenFailed` or `WorldCloseFailed`. Once for each `level_load`. Don't read its blocks. Server-side. |
| `lightning_strike` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server lightning bolt is about to be created. `cause` is `weather`, `trident`, `command` or `mod` (a native mod's `strike_lightning`); `player` is the responsible player when known. Set `cancel` to prevent the bolt and its effects. |
| `living_breathe` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingBreatheEvent` | An entity's breathing this tick (`LivingBreatheEvent`, posted from `LivingEntity.baseTick` every tick for a living entity with the generic air logic): `entity` is the player or mob. `can_breathe`, `consume` and `refill` come in as Sunrise does it (a mod-free game changes nothing); set them to change this tick. With `can_breathe` the air rises by `refill`, up to its maximum (`entity_max_air_supply`: 300 for every entity except dolphins, which have 4800); a dolphin's vanilla `refill` is its maximum minus its current air. Without breath, air falls by `consume`, and at -20 it is set to 0 and the entity takes 2 drowning damage. Negative amounts count as 0. Asked on servers for players and mobs. A water animal, axolotl or nautilus comes in with its own air logic's answer: where it breathes (water, or rain for an axolotl) `can_breathe` and a `refill` to its full air; elsewhere no breath and `consume` 1. | `living_change_target` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `LivingChangeTargetEvent` | A mob is about to change its target. `original_target` is the target requested by the game, or none when it is clearing the target. `new_target` starts as that value; a listener can redirect or clear it. During the lifted mob tick pass, the scoped view reads the ticking mob directly and peers from the tick snapshot: an already-ticked peer exposes post-turn state, and a not-yet-ticked peer pre-turn state. A mob spawned during that pass does not resolve in the scoped view until the next pass. Removed mobs do not resolve; dead-but-present mobs are distinct from removed mobs. Outside this scoped tick path, dispatch reads the entities available in the game host rather than this snapshot. A stale or invalid replacement, including a fake player, is refused. | `living_conversion_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingConversionEvent.Post` | A mob converted into another type (NeoForge's `LivingConversionEvent.Post`): `converted` replaces `entity`.
