| `living_conversion_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingConversionEvent.Pre` | A mob is about to convert into another type (NeoForge's `LivingConversionEvent.Pre`, vanilla's `Mob.convertTo`): a zombie drowning, a piglin or hoglin in the Overworld, a tadpole growing, a mooshroom sheared by a dispenser, a pig or villager struck by lightning. `outcome` is the new type. Set `cancel` to keep the mob as it is; a drowning zombie starts its timers again. Not told: a mooshroom a player shears, a zombie villager's cure, a villager turned by a zombie. |
| `living_damage_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingDamageEvent.Post` | NeoForge's `LivingDamageEvent.Post`: entity `entity` of type `kind` has been hurt by the hit `container` (a read-only live `DamageContainerView`) describes, with its final values (health damage, the reductions, the post-attack invulnerability ticks). It fires once for every hit that passed `living_incoming_damage` and the invulnerability cooldown, at the end of `hurtServer`: after the hurt sounds and after that hit's death processing (even if vetoed) or totem protection, before the hurt caller resumes. The next hazard, poison application or rider's hit sees the preceding hit already completed. It is not delayed to the next tick or the end of a player's turn. A queued mob effect's hurt finishes before the next action; a dolphin's dry-out hurt finishes before the land hop. A bee's drowning hurt finishes before it checks stinger expiry; an expiry hurt finishes before nectar and anger bookkeeping and movement controls. A server player's landing hurt finishes before the landing's `HIT_GROUND` and fall reset, and before the next accepted move. A mob landing in its own tick finishes its hurt before the fall reset, movement restitution, travel's collision hurt or a later goal. This includes a `BreathAir` move from either selector and a dolphin following a rider. A pufferfish sting on a mob finishes the hit before applying poison or starting the next sting, and before the fish's own `entity_tick_post`. A projectile hit finishes before its enchantment effects, levitation or a follow-up explosion. Each mount or passenger's hurt finishes before the fall routine continues to the next hit: a horse is hurt before its riders. `container.inflicted()` is the amount before absorption; `container.health_damage()` is the amount applied to health. A hit that took no health (a shield, absorption, armor or a `living_damage_pre` listener took it all) fires it too, with `container.health_damage()` 0. `container.should_cause_side_effects()` carries `hurtServer`'s fresh-hit decision (`shouldCauseSideEffects`): false for a stronger hit inside the cooldown unless its source bypasses it, independent of the final damage or modded invulnerability ticks. | `living_damage_pre` | server | listen | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingDamageEvent.Pre` | NeoForge's `LivingDamageEvent.Pre`: `LivingEntity.actuallyHurt` of entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`) by `source` (named as `native_living_incoming_damage` names it) has taken armor and magic, and the absorption hearts are next: `container` is a live mutable borrow of the hit's damage container; it holds the damage it started with, what it is now (`new_damage`, which may be 0) and each reduction so far (armor, enchantments, mob effects). Change its `new_damage` to change what is inflicted before absorption, not what `lastHurt` remembers for the cooldown. Set its post-attack invulnerability ticks to change the cooldown itself, or use `add_reduction_modifier` for subsequent stages (such as `Reduction::Absorption`). The setters refuse negative damage, NaN damage and negative ticks without changing the hit. Pre fires for players, mobs and the ender dragon; a wolf whose armor takes the hit and armor stands do not fire it. The host is the hit's (`HitHost`): its blocks, random and own attachments, level sounds, and the own random of the entities the hit lends; the hurt entity is in the middle of its hit. |
| `living_death` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDeathEvent` | A mob or server player is about to die (NeoForge's `LivingDeathEvent`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.die`): its health has run out, and the loot, experience and death message have not run. `entity` is its id and `kind` its type (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player`). `source` and `weapon` are the complete killing blow, as `living_incoming_damage` saw it, including the causing player, direct projectile and weapon components. A server player's lethal fall is heard on the landing body before its fall distance is reset. A lethal pufferfish sting is heard before the fish applies that sting's poison or stings the next mob. A projectile's killing blow is heard before on-hit follow-ups; a mount or passenger's killing blow is heard before falling on to the next rider. | `living_death_message` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `living_death_message` is asked for the death message a tamed mob sends its owner (`TamableAnimal.die`), once the message is built and only when it would be sent (owner online, `show_death_messages` on). `message` holds the text: replace it, or set it to `None` to tell the owner nothing. | `living_death_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A mob or server player's death has finished (Fabric's `ServerLivingEntityEvents.AFTER_DEATH`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.die` at its `broadcastEntityEvent`): the loot, equipment and experience dropped and the death message went out. `entity` is its id; `damage_type` names the killing blow (`minecraft:player_attack`) and `attacker` is the mob that dealt it, 0 for none or a player. It only hears. The tail of `living_death`, a separate moment, and not fired when that hook cancelled the death. On a server player's lethal landing this is still inside the hurt, before `HIT_GROUND` and the fall reset. On a pufferfish sting it is before that sting's poison and the next sting. Projectile and mounted-fall deaths likewise finish before their on-hit or next-rider follow-ups. Server-side, once per death, for every ordinary living entity. The ender dragon uses a separate death sequence and fires neither living death hook. Armor stands, boats and other non-mob entities are not told. |
| `living_destroy_block` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDestroyBlockEvent` | A mob is about to destroy a block (NeoForge's `LivingDestroyBlockEvent`): a wither breaking blocks around it, a ravager breaking leaves, the ender dragon's wall check, an enderman picking a block up, a silverfish waking its friends (the infested blocks break), a zombie breaking a door or a turtle egg. `entity` is its entity id, `kind` its type (`minecraft:wither`), `pos` the block and `state` its state. Canceling keeps the block (an enderman then holds nothing). Asked once per block, only while a mod is loaded. Server-side. |
| `living_drops` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDropsEvent` | Not `loot_drops`: that is the loot-modifier step, which edits the stacks a table rolled (blocks, shearing, a mob's table). Each `ItemDrop` also sets the item entity's `thrower`, `target` (the only player who may pick it up), `no_gravity` and starting `age` (`ItemDrop::UNLIMITED_LIFETIME` never despawns); a drop left as it was spawns as before. | `living_drown` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | `LivingDrownEvent` | An entity that can't breathe and has no air (`LivingDrownEvent`, posted from `LivingEntity.baseTick`), with vanilla's answers as the defaults: it drowns at -20 air, for 2 damage and 8 bubbles. | `living_entity_use_item_finish` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Finish` | NeoForge's `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Finish`: `entity`'s use of `stack` in `hand` ran its time (`LivingEntity.completeUsingItem`), `duration` the ticks left, always 0. `entity` is as in `living_entity_use_item_start`. `result` arrives with what `finishUsingItem` left in the hand (the bowl of a stew, none for the last apple; a mod's own item is kept) and may be replaced. | `living_entity_use_item_start` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Start` | NeoForge's `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Start`: `entity` starts using `stack` in `hand` (`LivingEntity.startUsingItem`), for `duration` ticks (`use_duration`'s answer). `entity` is a mob's `Entity.getId`, None for a player (`host.players().acting_player()` says who); a mob fires it only for `LivingRef::start_using_item`. | `living_entity_use_item_stop` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Stop` | NeoForge's `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Stop`: `entity` lets go of `stack` in `hand` before its use ran its time (`LivingEntity.releaseUsingItem`: the use button let go, a screen opened), `duration` the ticks left. | `living_entity_use_item_tick` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Tick` | Each living tick that `entity` may still use `stack` in `hand`. `entity` is as in `living_entity_use_item_start`; `duration` replaces the remaining-ticks counter, not the original use duration. | `living_experience_drop` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingExperienceDropEvent` | A dying mob or player `entity` of `kind` is about to drop `original` points of experience; the vanilla conditions have been checked. `attacking_player` is the player who last hurt it, if any. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `living_knockback` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `LivingKnockBackEvent` | NeoForge's `LivingKnockBackEvent`: `LivingEntity.knockback` is about to push entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:player`) `strength` away from the direction (`x`, `z`), on the raw strength, before the entity's knockback resistance takes its share and its motion changes. Vanilla's direction points at the source of a hit (the attacker's side), and the entity is pushed the other way. `source` is the push's `DamageSource`, named as `native_living_incoming_damage` names a hit's. `attacker` is the attacking entity's network id, falling back to the victim's last hurt-by mob, or 0 when unknown; `attacker_main` and `attacker_off` are what it holds (a player's selected and off-hand stacks, a mob's main hand), none when unknown. `LivingRef::knockback` has an unattributed `minecraft:generic` source: this same attacker fallback and the victim's existing kill credit still apply. The call deals no damage and creates no credit. `entry` is `Short` when the push came in through `knockback(power, x, z)`, which only delegates to the full entry, and `Full` when the full entry was called directly. The hook fires once per call of the full entry either way, so a listener that wants its change applied twice for a short-entry push, as a mixin on both entries would, applies it twice when `entry` is `Short`. Change `strength`, `x` or `z`, or set `canceled`, and the push follows them: canceled, the entity's motion is left alone. A `strength` of 0 or less after the hook is no push, and a direction too short to tell draws a random one after it, as in vanilla. A value a mod leaves that is not a finite number is put back as it was. It fires for `LivingRef::knockback` and for vanilla living knockbacks: a hit's own push, a sprinting or Knockback hit, a sweep, a spear, a shield's push on its holder, a goat's ram and a mob's melee push. It does not fire for a sulfur cube with a block that a hit with a causing entity knocks back (`SulfurCube.knockback` does not call `super` there), for the pushes vanilla doesn't make with `LivingEntity.knockback` (the ender dragon's wings, Punch arrows, explosions, wind charges, a mace smash's area, a hoglin's throw). The level is the victim's: its blocks, random and own attachments. `kill_credit` is the pushed living entity's kill credit at this pre-motion moment: a remembered player takes precedence over its last hurting mob, even when that player cannot resolve (then 0, without falling back). `kill_credit_main` is that entity's current main-hand stack, or none. This is not the immediate damage-source attacker. Accepted-hit memories, including a tame wolf's owner, are committed before this read. Both are additions: NeoForge's `LivingKnockBackEvent` doesn't carry them, and mods there read the pushed entity's kill credit and its holder's main hand themselves. |
| `living_shield_block` | server | listen | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingShieldBlockEvent` | NeoForge's `LivingShieldBlockEvent`: entity `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`, `minecraft:zombie`) is hit for `damage` from `source` (its type and entities, as `native_living_incoming_damage` names them), and `LivingEntity.applyItemBlocking` is about to resolve what a raised `blocks_attacks` item stops. Every hit with damage asks, raised item or not: `original_blocked` is whether vanilla blocks (only a player raises one). Change `block`: `blocked` makes the entity block or not (a block forced on a hit vanilla lets through stops all of it), `blocked_damage` what is stopped (kept within 0 and `damage`) and `shield_damage` the durability a raised item loses (below 0: vanilla's `itemDamage` of what is stopped). Set `blocked` to false and nothing is blocked, as NeoForge's `setBlocked(false)`; the event has no cancel. The host is the hit's (`HitHost`): its blocks, random and own attachments, level sounds, and the own random of the entities the hit lends. |
| `living_speed` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | A living entity's movement speed (`LivingEntity.getSpeed`) is about to drive its travel on the ground and through fluids. `entity` is its id and `kind` its type; a mod may change `speed`. Fires on both sides, on the client's replayed ticks too, with the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity (its registries, settings and mod list): register the mod on both, and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs so prediction and the server agree. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `living_travel` | server | cancel | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | Before the server's `Player.travel` branch (after swim steering) or a mob's `LivingEntity.travel` branch. `entity`, `kind` and `mode` identify the body and the branch it is about to run, including species overrides. A body that doesn't travel, such as a shulker, fires no hook. Neither does a replayed player tick. A branch that calls `entity_move` can still meet a move veto there. |
| `living_use_totem` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn HitHost` | `LivingUseTotemEvent` | NeoForge's `LivingUseTotemEvent`: a killing blow on entity `entity` of type `kind` from `source` (named as `native_living_incoming_damage` names it) would be taken by the `death_protection` item `item` in its `hand` (main or off; `LivingEntity.checkTotemDeathProtection`). Set `cancel` and the entity gets no protection: it dies and the item is kept. A player or a mob holding a totem fires it; a source that bypasses invulnerability never reaches it. `player_totem` still hears a server player's totem that did save them, after this. The host is the hit's (`HitHost`): its blocks, random and own attachments, level sounds, and the own random of the entities the hit lends. |
| `living_visibility` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `LivingEvent.LivingVisibilityEvent` | How visible `entity` is to `looking` (NeoForge's `LivingVisibilityEvent`, last in `LivingEntity.getVisibilityPercent`): the range a mob's targeting check sees it from is scaled by `visibility`. It comes in as vanilla's percent (sneaking 0.8, invisibility by armour cover, a worn head's `mob_visibility`: 0.5 to its own mob type); multiply it to scale. Values below 0 count as 0, and the result is clamped to 10. Asked for any living target, by the mobs whose targeting checks scale by visibility (hostile targeting, sensors, the happy ghast's tempt). A mob target comes in without the sneaking factor, which vanilla reads through `isDiscrete`. Server-side. |
| `loot_drops` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDropsEvent, BlockDropsEvent` | A block broke or a mob died, and `drops` are the items about to drop, in the order they drop: what the loot tables rolled (after `loot_table_load`'s changes), and for a mob what it wore, held or carried (a fox's mouth item, an allay's, piglin's or horse's inventory), but not an enderman's carried block or a copper golem's preserved equipment, which drop after. A charged creeper's head and a fox's mouth item come before the mob's own table. A block's stacks get its block entity's data (a shulker box's items) after this. Add, change or take out stacks; empty ones don't drop (NeoForge's global loot modifiers, Fabric's `LootTableEvents.MODIFY_DROPS`); `context` says what dropped them and how, and it is called even when `drops` is empty. A player's use drops through it too (`LootDrops::used`: shears, a brush, an item frame), with that player named. Lead drops from untying or cutting a leash, and from a mob a player killed while leashed, pass through here too, with that player named. It hears blocks that a player, an explosion or a mob breaks, not other breaks (leaves decaying, a torch losing its wall, a piston, `/setblock ... destroy`), nor a creative break or a tool that can't harvest the block, nor experience. For deaths, `context.damage_source` carries the death invocation's damage type and causing/direct IDs (not remembered player credit), and `context.tool` is None (the entity context has no TOOL). Resolve the causing ID through `host.entity` and `host.living` to read its main hand live with `living.with_hand_item(host, Hand::Main, \|stack\| stack.item())`, including earlier listeners' changes. Use `stack.project()` inside the callback if you need components or an owned hand stack; projection may refuse. `context.weapon_item(host)` returns an owned snapshot of the recorded projectile weapon or the live melee weapon, including a player's active auto-spin stack. Block and use drops retain their tool meaning. A changed drop keeps any non-negative signed-int damage, even above its maximum durability. Server-side, for every player. |
| `loot_table_load` | server | collect | `&dyn DataLoadContext` | `LootTableLoadEvent` | A native mod's hook point (docs/modding/src/how-to/native-mods.md), declared in `hooks/*.rs` with the game's: the loot table `id` (`minecraft:chests/simple_dungeon`, `minecraft:blocks/stone`, `minecraft:entities/zombie`) as it loads, its JSON after the data packs', before anything rolls it (NeoForge's `LootTableLoadEvent`, Fabric's `LootTableEvents.MODIFY`). Add pools to `table["pools"]` or entries to a pool's `entries`, change or replace it (no pools: it drops nothing; keep its `random_sequence` when replacing it, as a mob's table without one drops nothing). Called once per table in id order when the server loads or reloads its data, with the `DataLoadContext` of that owning load (`origin()` is always `ServerDataLoad`: the client never loads loot tables, so the hook never fires there). |
| `map_decoration_types` | both | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | The map decoration types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterEvent` for `Registries.MAP_DECORATION_TYPE`): call `event.register(decoration)` with a `MapDecorationType` per type. Asked once at startup on every side. |
| `map_decorations` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `MapDecorationType` | Icons a mod puts on map `map` each time it is drawn: held, in an item frame or on the cartography table's screen (a map's pixels are its own; nothing is saved in the map). Push `MapDecoration`s (an atlas sprite, a position in half map pixels, a rotation, and whether it shows in a frame and on the screen like vanilla's markers do); they are drawn after the vanilla ones with the same pose, and a sprite the atlas lacks is left out. `map.center`, `map.scale` and `map.dimension` say which blocks the map shows, and `MapDecoration::x` has the formula. Vanilla's decorations are not passed in. Asked every frame a map is drawn, so keep it cheap. The client half of `MapDecorationType` and `addDecoration`: a type registered on the server and saved in the map's data is not here. |
| `menu_background` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The normal menu blur request, before finite/range validation. None disables it. |
| `menu_closed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerContainerEvent.Close` | A menu a mod opened closed: the player closed it, the server or a mod opened another, or the player left. `opening_hand` is the hand the opener supplied, if any. `owned_slots` contains the transient container's final values in slot order, or an empty slice when there was no such container. The window is already detached and those stacks are not returned or dropped as entities by the game. A listener can use the Host to return them or update the opening item if the player is still available. |
| `menu_extra_data` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IPlayerExtension.openMenu` | Block `block` at `pos` opens its mod menu type `menu` for server player `player`: set `data` to hand the menu's screen bytes of your own (a machine's tier, mode or recipes), up to 1,048,555 bytes (vanilla's 1 MiB clientbound custom payload limit minus the 21-byte envelope header), which the client reads only from `MenuScreenOpen::extra` during construction (NeoForge's `openMenu` with an extra-data writer). Oversize data is logged and refuses the menu open. For live values use the menu's data slots (`MenuScreenFrame::data` on the client). The first mod to set `data` wins, so answer only for your blocks. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `menu_links` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Containers exposed by linked slots of server player `player`'s open mod menu `menu` (`namespace:path`) at `pos`. The player's state is not swapped in. Push container positions to `links`, in order. The engine resolves containers and refreshes their linked slots in the same tick. Asked each server tick only for open mod menu types declaring links, and dispatched only to subscribers. With no subscribers the linked-container tick does no work and allocates nothing. Silently skips `pos`, duplicate positions, missing or non-lendable containers, and positions after 64 accepted containers (`MAX_MENU_LINKS`). First accepted occurrence wins, preserving answer order. |
| `mob_despawn` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `MobDespawnEvent` | NeoForge's `MobDespawnEvent`: the mob `mob` (of type `kind`, at `pos`) is about to run `Mob.checkDespawn`, which fires once per tick for every mob on the entity tick list (its chunk is entity-ticking, which player tickets give across the whole view distance), persistent ones and peaceful-mode ones included. A listed mob beyond the simulation distance is checked without being ticked; a mob in a loaded chunk that is not entity-ticking is neither checked nor told. The hook is told while the game has lent its mob list to the tick, so `mob` resolves through the tick's own read view (#795): the ticking mob itself, and the other present mobs in its tick snapshot, are readable through the host. The ticking mob is read directly; an already-ticked peer exposes its post-turn state and a not-yet-ticked peer its pre-turn state. A mob spawned during the pass does not resolve in this scoped view until the next pass. Removed mobs do not resolve; a dead-but-present mob is not a removed mob. This is the scoped read-host first step for #795, not an in-place ownership redesign. `kind` and `pos` stay as plain data for listeners that only need them. Set `result`: `Allow` discards the mob now, `Deny` keeps it this tick, and `Default` (the starting value) leaves vanilla's rules to decide. Anything but `Default` skips vanilla's checks, including the `nextInt(800)` draw of the idle-despawn roll, so the hook fires before that draw: a mob a mod denies does not consume it. A listener is a pure function of its inputs, because the hook fires on replayed ticks. The wither and the ender dragon override `checkDespawn` without `super`, so the event is not fired for them. Server-side. Tick-time effect: one `listened` branch per mob per tick with no listeners. |
| `mob_effect_added` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `MobEffectEvent.Added` | NeoForge's `MobEffectEvent.Added`, vanilla's `MobEffect.onEffectStarted`: every `LivingEntity.addEffect` of the content mod's effect `effect`, new or not, changed or not, after `on_effect_added`. Server-side. |
| `mob_effect_applicable` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `MobEffectEvent.Applicable` | NeoForge's `MobEffectEvent.Applicable`, vanilla's `LivingEntity.canBeAffected`: asked wherever the game checks whether the resolved `EffectKey` `effect` (`minecraft:poison`) can land on the living entity `entity` (a `LivingRef`, the player's own included), before `addEffect` accepts it. Unlike the effect hooks above this fires for every effect, vanilla's included, and only when an add is in flight. The first mod to answer decides: `Allow` or `Deny` overrides the vanilla tag check; an unanswered hook falls back to it. Server-side. |
| `mob_effect_expired` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `MobEffectEvent.Expired` | NeoForge's `MobEffectEvent.Expired`, vanilla `LivingEntity.tickEffects`: the resolved `EffectKey` `effect` (`minecraft:speed`, amplifier `amplifier`) on the living entity `entity` (a `LivingRef`, the player's own included) counted down to nothing and is about to leave. Cancel and the effect stays, kept at no ticks and asked again next tick; unanswered, the expiry runs. Fires only on the tick an effect actually expires, never per tick. Server-side. |
| `mob_effect_remove` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `MobEffectEvent.Remove` | NeoForge's `MobEffectEvent.Remove`, vanilla `LivingEntity.removeEffect`: the resolved `EffectKey` `effect` (`minecraft:speed`, amplifier `amplifier`) on the living entity `entity` (a `LivingRef`, the player's own included) is about to be removed by any path that walks the vanilla funnel: a command or milk for the player, a cure for a zombie villager, a mod's removal helper. Cancel and the effect stays. Server-side. |
| `mob_killed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDeathEvent` | `player`'s melee blow killed the mob `entity` of `kind` (other deaths aren't told yet). It only hears, after the blow landed. Server-side. |
| `mob_spawn_placement_check` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `MobSpawnEvent.SpawnPlacementCheck` | NeoForge's `MobSpawnEvent.SpawnPlacementCheck`: may the entity type `kind` spawn at `pos` in `level` for `reason`? This server hook runs after `SpawnPlacements.checkSpawnRules` evaluates the type's spawn predicate. `default_result` is the effective predicate result, including predicates composed or replaced through `Registrar::register_spawn_placement`. It can differ from vanilla even for a vanilla entity type. `result` starts as `Default`, which keeps that answer. Set `Succeed` or `Fail` to override it for this check. The predicates have already drawn from `event.random`, the caller's live stream (NeoForge's `SpawnPlacementCheck.getRandom()`). Each listener borrows that same stream; replacing its local reference cannot redirect later listeners. Draws advance the stream even if the result stays `Default` or the spawn is later refused. |
| `mob_spawn_position_check` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `MobSpawnEvent.PositionCheck` | NeoForge's `MobSpawnEvent.PositionCheck`: may the freshly created mob of type `kind` stand at `pos` (its position, a mob's centre) in `level` for `reason`? Vanilla's two checks are `Mob.checkSpawnRules` (the type's own rules) and `Mob.checkSpawnObstruction` (inside blocks or fluids, an ocelot's grass and sea level). `result` starts as `Default`, which runs them; `Succeed` or `Fail` answers instead and they are skipped. It runs on natural spawning, chunk generation's creature packs and spawner blocks (where a spawner with custom spawn rules skips `Mob.checkSpawnRules` on `Default`, as vanilla does).  A trial spawner runs only `checkSpawnObstruction`, so only that is skipped. `spawner` is the block that asked (`Spawner::Block` or `Spawner::Trial`), `None` for natural spawning, chunk generation and a spawner minecart. `mob` is the mob itself, in no level yet: read it through the host (`mob.kind(host)`, `mob.position(host)`, `mob.equipment(host, slot)`); after the hook returns the handle answers `Expired`. It is the mob that spawns if the check passes, handed on rather than created again, and dropped without trace if it fails. Where the game does not create the mob fresh (chunk generation's packs, a spawner whose spawn data carries entity data) the handle answers its kind and position and `Unsupported` for the rest. Server-side. With no listener or `Default` the checks and their random draws are vanilla's (creating the mob for the listener draws nothing from the level's random); `Succeed` and `Fail` skip the checks and so their draws, and the level random's order changes only then; a listener should be a pure function of its inputs, because ticks are replayed. |
| `mob_spawned` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityJoinLevelEvent, FinalizeSpawnEvent` | A new mob of `kind` joined `dimension` at `pos`: `natural` when the world's spawning made it, false when something else did (a breed, a raid, a spawner, a conversion, `/summon`, and the second mob of a jockey the world spawned). One call a mob: a `/summon` with passengers tells each. Not told of a mob loaded with its chunk, leaving a hive or changing level. It only hears; the mob is already among the game's mobs. Server-side. |
| `mob_split` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `MobSplitEvent` | A slime, magma cube or sulfur cube is dying and about to split into smaller ones (NeoForge's `MobSplitEvent`, vanilla's `AbstractCubeMob.remove`): `dimension` is the level it dies in (a `PendingMobSpec` for a new child names it), `parent` its entity id, `kind` its type, `size` its size and `child_size` the size of the children (half of it). The children vanilla made (2 to 4 for a slime or magma cube, 2 for a sulfur cube; a primed one makes none and isn't asked) are already constructed, in order, and wait in an engine-owned list that `edits.children()` names and `edits` changes: read it with `edits.len` and `edits.child`, then edit a child (`edits.set_position`, `edits.set_yaw`, `edits.set_baby`, `edits.set_size`), replace one (`edits.replace`), remove one (`edits.remove`) or append a new one (`edits.construct`, then `edits.append`; at most 64 children). Each child is a complete mob that is in no level yet: it has no entity number, no join and nothing a player or another mod can see, and every handle (`edits.children()` and the children `edits.child` gave) is borrowed for one listener only; kept past it, or used by a later listener, it answers `Expired`. Set `cancel` (or leave the list empty) to spawn no children: the children's construction draws stay spent and the parent still dies. A panicking callback is reported and disabled for the session, and this split is cancelled. A refusal (including an explicit veto) rolls back journaled listener writes in the split's level. The accepted children are inserted once, in order, before deferred join callbacks run. If the listener invalidates the split, or the list ends up invalid, nothing spawns. Only for a parent above size 1, once its children are made. With no listener nothing is allocated for the list. On the server. |
| `mod_container_viewers` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The number of players with the mod container at `pos` of `dimension` open went from `previous` to `viewers` (vanilla's `ContainerOpenersCounter`, as a barrel keeps it): 0 to more than 0 is `onOpen`, back to 0 is `onClose`, any change `openerCountChanged`, so a block can show itself open while `viewers` is not 0. Incremented synchronously when a non-spectator opens the menu, decremented when it closes (including disconnect), and rechecked every five ticks while open, including copper golems in range. Rechecks also report unchanged counts. Zero crossings post container_open/container_close game events: the opening or closing player is the source, none on a recheck. Server-side; open/close runs with the player's state in place. |
| `mod_mismatch` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `ModMismatchEvent` | A saved mod list (code and content mods) was read while opening a world. Fires when a nonempty list exists, changed or unchanged, never when the lists are absent or empty (an empty saved list counts as absent), once per open. Versions use the loader's comparison; minecraft is excluded. Newly added mods are not differences. Push a mod id converted with `.into()` into `resolved`. Dispatch assigns the first resolving listener and preserves earlier markers. When a saved mod is no longer loaded, it fires before the world opens, on the side that asks (the client in singleplayer, the dedicated server), and a resolved id is taken off the question; otherwise it fires on the opening server before world-state restoration, after preparation. |
| `model_baking_completed` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ModelEvent.BakingCompleted` | Every model of a resource load is baked and published (`ModelEvent.BakingCompleted`): the block and item models and the standalone ones mods asked for (`standalone_models`), in the store `model` now reads. Fires once per successful model load, after both `texture_atlas_stitched` calls, at the model manager's apply step on first load and reload. Listeners ordered after the model manager apply after this hook. It precedes `client_setup` on startup and `client_resources_reloaded` on later reloads. A refused prepare fires nothing, and the old handles stay valid. Resolve models once through `render_assets().model` and keep the `ModelRef`s until the next `model_baking_completed`, when the old ones answer `ModelError::StaleModel`. Use `host.render_assets().baked_model(key)` to borrow published geometry during this callback; it does not edit the store. Never look them up by name every frame. Client-side. A panic rolls back the callback's journaled writes; the mod is reported and its callback skipped for the session without suppressing other mods' notifications. |
| `modify_custom_spawners` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `ModifyCustomSpawnersEvent` | NeoForge's `ModifyCustomSpawnersEvent`: the level `dimension` is about to tick its custom spawners for the first time. `spawners` is the list `ServerLevel` ticks, in order, and it starts with vanilla's (`WanderingTrader`, `Patrol`, `Cat`, `VillageSiege` and `Phantom`, in the order Sunrise ticks them). Take a vanilla spawner out with `remove` and it never ticks, so its random draws are gone with it; add a spawner of the mod's own with `add` and a `SpawnerKey` the mod owns, and `custom_spawner_tick` runs for it every tick, after the ones before it in the list. With no listener, or one that leaves the list alone, the level ticks exactly vanilla's spawners. The phantom spawner runs after the level's mobs have ticked, as the game runs it, wherever it is in the list. It fires once per level, on the server, and listeners run on replayed ticks, so they must be pure functions of their inputs. Not cancellable. |
| `modify_default_components` | both | collect | `&dyn DataLoadContext` | `ModifyDefaultComponentsEvent` | NeoForge 26.3's ModifyDefaultComponentsEvent: set or remove entries in `components`, the default component map for `item`, including vanilla items. Receives a read-only DataLoadContext with the actual ServerDataLoad or ClientPreload origin, frozen registry names, settings captured once for loaded packages at this load boundary, and the shared LoadedMods view. Readers and datapack reloads do not replay this event. No runtime Host, world handles or transaction access. |
| `moisture_change` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Farmland at `pos` is about to change moisture from `from` to `to`. Set `cancel` to keep its old moisture. Server-side. |
| `mouse_button_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.MouseButton.Post` | An accepted raw button event finished dispatch, including a screen handler's early return. |
| `mouse_button_pre` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.MouseButton.Pre` | Raw mouse button input on any screen, before dispatch; cancel skips dispatch and Post. Button/modifiers use the same GLFW encoding as screen hooks. Synthetic clicks skip it. |
| `mouse_scrolling` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `InputEvent.MouseScrollingEvent` | Gameplay scrolling after accumulation, before hotbar/spectator handling. `scroll` is scaled x/y, `accumulated` whole x/y steps, `buttons` left/middle/right, and `cursor` the GUI position. Cancel skips gameplay scrolling. |
| `movement_input_update` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `MovementInputUpdateEvent` | Lends the local player's sampled `MovementInput` after sampling, before auto-jump and sprint decisions. Every listener sees the preceding listeners' edits. |
| `music` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `SelectMusicEvent` | Choose this client's music, once a client tick: `music` and `volume` hold what vanilla chose (`Minecraft.getSituationalMusic` and `getMusicVolume`: the biome's track, the creative or underwater one, a boss's, the menu's; None for silence). Set `music` to a track of your own, a sound event of your content mod's `sounds.json` such as `Music::new("mymod:music.spring", 1200, 6000, false)` (the delays in ticks between songs, and whether it cuts off a different song playing), None to keep vanilla's quiet, or change `volume` (0 to 1) to fade it. |
| `music_layers` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The tracks to play over (or instead of) the music on this client, asked once a client tick: push a `MusicLayer` (a sound event, looped, and its volume, 0 to 1) for each. A layer eases to a new volume over up to two seconds, and one left out fades out and stops; layers that first appear in the same tick start together, so list every stem of a piece from the start, the silent ones at volume 0, and raise them (a fuller arrangement as a village grows).
