| `neighbor_notify` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `BlockEvent.NeighborNotifyEvent` | The block at `pos` (`state`) is about to tell its neighbours it changed (NeoForge's `BlockEvent.NeighborNotifyEvent`): at `ServerLevel.updateNeighborsAt`, which notifies all six sides (`skipped_side` is `None`), and `updateNeighborsAtExceptFromFacing`, which notifies all but `skipped_side`. Set `cancel` and no neighbour hears, on both paths. Asked for every such update, redstone included: answer fast. Server-side. |
| `note_block_change` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `NoteBlockEvent.Change` | A player is tuning a note block on the authoritative server, not in client prediction. `context` gives its dimension, position, instrument and old note; `new_note` starts as vanilla's cycled note. Set it to 0 through 24; an out-of-range value keeps vanilla's. Cancel to leave the note unchanged and prevent playback. |
| `note_block_play` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `NoteBlockEvent.Play` | The note block at `pos` is about to play (NeoForge's `NoteBlockEvent.Play`, in `NoteBlock.playNote`), once a block above no longer mutes it. `player` and `player_entity_id` name the player who tuned or punched it, and are `None` for redstone. `instrument` is its instrument and `note` its note, 0 to 24; change either to play another (a note out of range keeps the block's own). Set `cancel` and it doesn't play: no sound, no particle, no `note_block_play` game event. Server-side. |
| `on_effect_added` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `MobEffect.onEffectAdded`: `LivingEntity.addEffect` gave `entity` the content mod's effect `effect`, which it didn't have. Server-side. |
| `on_equip` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's equipment slot `slot` (`head`, `chest`, `legs`, `feet`, `offhand`, and `mainhand` on a swap of the hands or `/item`) took a different item, `new` (none when it was emptied): `LivingEntity.onEquipItem`, before the equip sound. It only hears. Never for a spectator. Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). |
| `panel_event` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | `event` happened on mod `mod_id`'s panel (`native_panel_items`): the player's click, key or edit, or (`Search`, since version 2) the owner's own `panel_search` reaching its box, on the first frame that draws it, with no player edit. Since version 3 a left click on an item with nothing carried comes as `Clicked` when the button comes up without a drag, and a drag off the list ends as `Dropped`. Act on it when it is yours. |
| `permissions_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PermissionsChangedEvent` | NeoForge's `PermissionsChangedEvent`: the operator list is about to give player `player` (`name`) the permission level `new` in place of `old`, through `PlayerList.op` (`/op`, the console's `op`, the management API's `operators/add` and `operators/set`) or `PlayerList.deop` (`/deop` and the same on the console and the management API; `new` is then `All`). Set `cancel` and the list keeps the old level. The player need not be online: `player_entity_id` is their server entity id when they are. Clearing the list (`operators/clear`) is not a call to either, as in vanilla. Dedicated servers only; a single-player world has no operator list. Server-side. |
| `pickup_xp` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerXpEvent.PickupXp` | Before `player` picks up orb `orb` in `dimension`. Mutations to `value` and `count` take effect only when not canceled; value is clamped to 1..=i32::MAX and count to at least one. Canceling leaves the orb and pickup delay unchanged. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: renamed from `experience_orb_pickup`, to NeoForge's `PlayerXpEvent.PickupXp`. |
| `piston_post` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | `PistonEvent.Post` | On an authoritative server, the piston at `piston` pushed the blocks at `push` one step `direction` (`[0, 1, 0]` is up; pulling when it retracts, `extending` false) and destroyed those at `destroy` (NeoForge's `PistonEvent.Post`, at the end of `PistonBaseBlock.moveBlocks`, after the neighbour updates). It only hears; `piston_pre` is the veto. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: `piston_move` is split into `piston_pre` and `piston_post`. |
| `piston_pre` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `PistonEvent.Pre` | On an authoritative server, the piston at `piston` is about to push the blocks at `push` one step `direction` (`[0, 1, 0]` is up; pulling when it retracts, `extending` false) and destroy those at `destroy` (NeoForge's `PistonEvent.Pre`, in `PistonBaseBlock.moveBlocks`, once its structure resolved). Set `canceled` and nothing moves, as when the structure can't; a sticky piston then retracts without its block, and clients are sent the blocks rather than the retraction to replay. Diverges from upstream: `piston_move` is renamed to `piston_pre`, and `piston_post` is new. |
| `play_level_sound_at_entity` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtEntity` | A sound bound to `entity` is about to play (NeoForge's `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtEntity`, in `Level.playSeededSound`'s entity overload, `playSound(except, sourceEntity, ...)`): a goat horn, a frog's tongue, eating and spawn, an animal eating (frog, goat, sniffer), a long jump, a goat's ram, a breeze's charge and deflection, shearing (sheep, mooshroom, snow golem, bogged, copper golem), dye on a sheep, an axe on a copper golem, an allay's item and duplication, a ghastling hatching, a trident thrown (the trident) or riptide (the player), and a mod's sound bound to an entity. Queued server sounds are asked as they go out. For a transactional server request, the hook runs synchronously at the call; only the resolved effect is deferred. Sounds emitted by a level-sound listener are not re-dispatched to level-sound listeners. The fields and their rules are `play_level_sound_at_position`'s. Client entity-sound packets ask before playback and keep following their emitter; an absent emitter plays nothing. `LocalPlayer.playSound` also asks this hook, including steps, falls, hurt, bundle clicks and consumption; these remain positional sounds, not following sounds. Both sides, distinguished by `HostSide`. |
| `play_level_sound_at_position` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtPosition` | A sound is about to play at `position` in `level` (NeoForge's `PlayLevelSoundEvent.AtPosition`, in `Level.playSeededSound`): every server sound not bound to an entity, mobs' own sounds included (`Entity.playSound` plays at the entity's position). A mod may change `sound`, `source`, `volume` and `pitch`, or set `cancel` and no player hears it; `original_volume` and `original_pitch` are as asked. A sound left unplayable (a volume or pitch that isn't finite and at least 0) is dropped. Sounds the server's level plays during the tick are asked as they go out, at the end of the tick, with the state then. `/playsound` sends its packet directly and isn't asked, as in vanilla on the server. On the client, incoming sound packets ask before playback, including `/playsound`; client-predicted and game-event level requests ask here too. Canceling a sound does not cancel its action or prediction. Server and client requests are separate: `HostSide` identifies which one is being edited. UI and sound-engine-only playback do not ask this hook. For a transactional server request, the hook runs synchronously at the call; only the resolved effect is deferred. Sounds emitted by a level-sound listener are not re-dispatched to level-sound listeners. Both sides. |
| `play_sound` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlaySoundEvent` | `starting` is true for starts and loop replays, false for a live channel refresh. A ticking sound's live refresh applies volume, pitch and `effects` transiently; a refresh for a category volume change applies volume and pitch. `None` stops the channel. Invalid edited numeric values refuse that refresh, `effects` by `SoundEffects::valid`. A sound is about to play (`PlaySoundEvent`, vanilla's `SoundEngine.play`): `original` is the sound as asked for, `sound` starts as a copy of it. Edit `sound` (its volume, pitch, source, position, identifier, looping, delay, attenuation, relativeness or `effects`) to change what plays, or set it to `None` to play nothing. `effects` (`SoundEffects`) is the mixer's DSP for this one sound: its gain, a low-pass and a high-pass on the direct sound and sends to the shared `room`, `hall` and `cavern` reverbs, each in a fixed range (`SoundEffects::valid`: a value out of range, NaN or an infinity refuses the play, never clamped). A sound that doesn't tick keeps the effects it started with. It is asked before the engine's own checks (output open, sound resolved, volume, channel pool), for every sound the client plays, so answer fast; later mods see earlier mods' edits in `sound` and the sound as asked for in `original`. A new `identifier` is resolved again, with its own weighted pick. `context` is the client's `LoopContext`: no game is at hand inside the sound engine. Client-side only; a server has no sound. `instance` is an engine-local sound instance id, unchanged across its live updates. |
| `play_sound_source` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlaySoundSourceEvent` | A sound that is not streamed has started on `channel` (`PlaySoundSourceEvent`), after the engine set its pitch, volume, attenuation, looping and position: `sound` is what is playing, after `play_sound`'s edits. A notice: the channel is an id only and gives no control. Streamed sounds (music, records) fire `play_streaming_source` instead. Client-side only. |
| `play_streaming_source` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlayStreamingSourceEvent` | A streamed sound (music, a record) has started on `channel` (`PlayStreamingSourceEvent`): `sound` is what is playing, after `play_sound`'s edits. A notice: the channel is an id only and gives no control. Other sounds fire `play_sound_source` instead. Client-side only. |
| `player_advancement_done` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AdvancementEvent.AdvancementEarnEvent` | A server player completed an advancement, after progress was recorded. |
| `player_advancement_progress` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `AdvancementEvent.AdvancementProgressEvent` | A criterion of a server player's advancement was granted or revoked, after its progress changed and before any completion rewards. A grant or revoke that changes nothing is not heard. `progress` is an owned snapshot of every criterion (including its stored grant date), the requirement groups and their completion at this exact mutation. It belongs to the mutated progress, including first-tick and reload grants queued by the server hub. During a nested player turn, a suspended player's hub record may contain displaced progress. A listener may retain a clone; later mutations do not change the snapshot. |
| `player_arm_swing` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's arm swing was handled by the server. `hand` is `mainhand`. Server-side. |
| `player_attack_cooldown_reset` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player reset their attack strength by swinging (`handlePunch`). The swing and reset have already happened. The player's state is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_bed_enter` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player is about to enter a bed after the sleep checks pass. Set `cancel` to keep them out. The player's state is swapped in. |
| `player_bed_leave` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerWakeUpEvent` | `PlayerWakeUpEvent`: a server player is getting out of the bed at `pos` (`Player.stopSleepInBed`), heard first, while they still read as sleeping there. `wake_immediately` is vanilla's flag for skipping the wake-up fade: generic stop-sleeping, death and bed-missing wakes pass true; morning, explicit leave and bed-rule refusal pass false. `update_level` is false for morning, true for those individual wakes. |
| `player_break_speed` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayerEvent.BreakSpeed` | A player's destroy speed for the block at `pos` may be changed or canceled. A client without this mod predicts vanilla destroy speed; the server remains authoritative and corrects the resulting block-breaking progress. |
| `player_brewed_potion` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerBrewedPotionEvent` | A server player took `stack` from a brewing stand bottle slot (`BrewingStandMenu.PotionSlot.onTake`): `player` is their UUID and `player_entity_id` their network id. |
| `player_bucket_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player caught a mob in a bucket, after it was removed and the filled bucket was given. Server-side. |
| `player_bundle_item_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected a bundle item in `slot` at `index`. The guest's state is not swapped in during packet handling. |
| `player_change_beacon_effect` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player changed a beacon's selected effects, after they were stored. Each selected effect is an `Option<EffectKey>`; None means no effect. Server-side. |
| `player_changed_dimension` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerChangedDimensionEvent` | `player` (`name`) changed dimension (NeoForge's `PlayerChangedDimensionEvent`): `from` and `to` are dimension ids (`minecraft:overworld`, `minecraft:the_nether`, `minecraft:the_end`). Fires once a cross-dimension teleport lands: a Nether or End portal (including travel on a vehicle and later End portal exits), or a command such as `/execute in ... run tp`. The destination level is in place and the player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()` and `PlayerRef::tell` reach them. Runs on the logical server, not the client. Does not fire for a move within a level, a player who leaves before arrival, or a replacement created by respawn, even in another level. Use `player_respawned` for respawns after death or after the End credits. |
| `player_client_loaded_world` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player finished loading the world around them after joining, respawning or changing dimension. The guest's state is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_clone` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.Clone (not cancellable)` | A player's data was copied to their new player (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.Clone`, from `ServerPlayer.restoreFrom`): `was_death` is true after a death, false when they left the End after the credits (`respawn` with `keepAllPlayerData`). `original` is a direct, callback-scoped loan (`OriginalPlayer`) of the retained original player, enforced at compile time so it cannot outlive the hook. It supports attachment reads on the old player without default insertion. Saved values decode once, and cache writes, explicit writes and removals are journaled to `host.transaction` without touching the replacement. It also provides `with_inventory` over the 43-slot vanilla inventory, `persistent_data`, health, experience, active effects, position, source dimension and source level. `player` is the replacement's UUID, already assigned its final level, including any `player_respawn_position` redirect. Heard after removal of the old player's menus and after the data copy, before placement or bulk attachment publication. Old bulk tokens are stale. Not cancellable. |
| `player_command` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `CommandEvent` | A command a player typed (NeoForge's `CommandEvent`, before `Commands.performCommand` runs it), without its slash: any command, vanilla's and the mods' own, also one the player may not run. Set `canceled` to stop it: nothing runs and nothing is answered but the lines pushed to `replies`, which go to the player as system chat. No player's state is swapped in and the server's player list is busy with this one, so `PlayerRef::tell` and the other player helpers reach nobody here: answer through `replies`. Once for each command a player sends, on a server, and for each line of a quiet command batch (`sunrise:quiet_commands`, whose replies are dropped). A command the server refuses before reading it (unsigned chat arguments, the chat rate limit) never reaches it. |
| `player_commands_sent` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The command tree a server sends a player (NeoForge's and Paper's "commands sent to a player" stage): when they join and when their level changes (`/op`, `/deop`, the LAN settings). `names` are the root commands their client would get, vanilla's they may use and the mods'; take a name out and that client gets the tree without it (and without what it leads to), so it neither completes nor highlights it. This hides the command from the client only: the server still runs a typed one, `player_command` stops it. For a player who is already on, their state is swapped in and the helpers (`PlayerRef::tell`...) work; for one who is joining it is not, and their client has no world to tell yet. The tree the hub sends again by itself (after a respawn or a change of level) keeps the last answer and does not ask again; the integrated server's owner is not asked when the world's cheats are switched. |
| `player_container_close` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerContainerEvent.Close` | `player` (`name`) closed the `menu_type` that `player_container_open` told of (NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent.Close`, posted in `ServerPlayer.doCloseContainer` after the menu's `removed`): they closed it, walked out of reach, died, changed level, or another menu opened. A player who leaves closes it before `player_logged_out` (vanilla posts nothing for a disconnect; Sunrise does, so each open has its close). Once for each open; not told when the server stops. The menu's items are where it left them: the block's, and the cursor stack back in the inventory. State swapped in, when the player has a body. Server-side. |
| `player_container_open` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerContainerEvent.Open` | `player` (`name`) opened a menu (NeoForge's `PlayerContainerEvent.Open`, posted when `ServerPlayer.openMenu` sets the new `containerMenu`): any menu, vanilla or a mod's. `menu_type` is the menu type's name (`minecraft:generic_9x3`, `minecraft:furnace`, `minecraft:merchant`, `minecraft:crafting`, `minecraft:lectern`, a mod's own), `pos` the block it is of in `dimension` (none for a trade, a vehicle's, a mod's native menu or a mount's inventory, which has no menu type in vanilla and is told as `sunrise:mount_inventory`). The player's own inventory is not one, nor is failed construction. Heard synchronously after construction, the open packet is queued and full contents sent, with the new menu already current. Replacing a menu follows its close; even inside one tick, open precedes `player_container_close`. `menu` is the current menu handle, valid during this call, as returned by `PlayerRef::container_menu`. It is `None` for unsupported menus such as crafting tables, merchants, workstations, lecterns and mount inventories. A supported menu keeps its handle when backing indices are permanently excluded; its slot methods still refuse those indices. The player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_deep_sleep` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player has slept for 100 ticks in a bed and now counts as deeply asleep for the night-skip check. |
| `player_destroy_item` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerDestroyItemEvent` | The player's item was used up or broke. `original` is the complete stack before the use or durability loss, including count, damage and components. `hand` is the hand it was in, or None for worn equipment and ammunition consumed from the inventory. `player` and `player_entity_id` identify the acting player, including the local host. Listen only, after consumption or the break has applied and before a use remainder is installed. A mining break notifies after block drops. Both sides hear their own actions; the client does not report a break inferred from an equipment status packet. Partial consumption, refused actions and creative use that preserves the stack do not fire. |
| `player_destroyed_block` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's break removed the block `state` from `pos`: the `Block.destroy(level, pos, state)` call `ServerPlayerGameMode.destroyBlock` makes only after `Level.removeBlock` succeeded, before a creative player's return and `playerDestroy`'s drops. `state` is the block the removal took out, read just before it: what `playerWillDestroy` left there (a cracked decorated pot; a disarmed tripwire, where vanilla passes `Block.destroy` the armed one) or what a `break_block` listener put there. `block_entity` is the saved data of the block entity that stood at `pos` (`BlockEntity.saveWithoutMetadata`, read with `state`), none for a block without one; the entity itself is gone from the level by now, so a live handle would be stale. The level is writable. Server-side, for every player of the world: the helpers read the acting one. Not heard for a break `break_block` canceled, nor for a break the level makes (a piston, water, an explosion). |
| `player_died` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingDeathEvent` | `player` died: where `ServerPlayer.die` starts, with the death message's key when the game knows it (`death.attack.lava`; none for a message it builds from the combat). A lethal landing is still in progress when this fires. It only hears. Server-side, for every player of the world, whether death messages are shown or not. |
| `player_edit_book` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's book edit or signing packet is about to be applied. `pages` is its page count; `signing` means the packet has a title. Set `cancel` to drop the edit. |
| `player_egg_throw` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player threw an egg, after its projectile was launched and the use was applied. Server-side. |
| `player_elytra_boost` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player used a firework rocket to boost while gliding, after the rocket was launched and consumed. Server-side. |
| `player_enchant_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEnchantItemEvent` | A server player enchanted `item` at an enchanting table (`EnchantmentMenu.clickMenuButton`): `enchantments` are the ones it was given, in order, as (id, level). `item` is the enchanted stack (an enchanted book for a book). Fires after the levels are paid, before `enchant_item`. Server-side. |
| `player_exp_cooldown_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's XP orb pickup cooldown changed to `ticks`. It is reported after the value changes. Server-side. |
| `player_fish` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's fishing rod, with `state`: 0 the cast (`FishingRodItem.use` throwing the bobber), 1 a fish biting (`FishingHook.catchingFish`), 2 the reel-in of a bite, which gives the catch, 3 the reel-in with nothing on the line, 4 the reel-in of a bobber hooked to an entity or item (like Bukkit's `PlayerFishEvent`; NeoForge's `ItemFishedEvent` is later, in `FishingHook.retrieve`). Set `cancel` on the cast and nothing is thrown; on a bite the fish gets away and the wait starts over; on a reel-in (2 to 4) nothing happens, the bobber stays out and the rod takes no wear. The bobber ending by itself (too far, the rod put away, the owner dying), nor the host of a LAN world's rod. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Once for each cast, bite and reel-in, on a server. |
| `player_flower_pot_manipulate` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player put a plant into a flower pot, after the potted block state was stored and the item consumed. `plant` is the item id and `pos` the pot position. Server-side. |
| `player_flyable_fall` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerFlyableFallEvent` | NeoForge's `PlayerFlyableFallEvent`: server player `player` (`name`) landed after falling `distance` blocks at damage multiplier `multiplier` while allowed to fly (a creative or spectator player walking), so `Player.causeFallDamage` returned early and nothing was hurt. Listen only. Once for each landing, after the fall's block reaction. The player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_game_mode_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerChangeGameModeEvent` | A server player's game mode is about to change (NeoForge's `PlayerChangeGameModeEvent`): from `from` to `to`, both the shared `GameMode` used by commands and `PlayerRef`. `player` is the UUID and `name` the name. Heard for the `/gamemode` command, the F3+F4 switcher's packet and a hardcore world's respawn into spectator, once for each change to a different mode; not for the world's default mode a player joins with. Set `to` to send the player to another mode instead: listeners run in order and each sees the mode the one before left. Return `Flow::Cancel`, or leave `to` at the player's current mode, and the player keeps their mode (nothing is sent, the command counts it as no change); the command's message names the mode it asked for, as vanilla's does. For a change the game makes inside a player's turn their state is swapped in; for the hardcore respawn it is not. |
| `player_handshake` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A login or allowed transfer reached the server, after the transfer and network protocol checks and before the login hello, authentication, or admission. Runs once on the server thread. There is no player or profile yet. |
| `player_harvest_check` | both | collect | `HostSide` | `PlayerEvent.HarvestCheck` | A player's ability to harvest the block at `pos` may be changed. |
| `player_heart_type` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlayerHeartTypeEvent` | Which hearts the local player's health bar draws this frame (NeoForge's `PlayerHeartTypeEvent`, from `Hud.HeartType.forPlayer`): `kind` comes in as vanilla's pick (poisoned, else withered, else frozen when fully frozen, else normal); set it to draw another kind. This runs inside the vanilla `minecraft:player_health` body, after `gui_layer_pre`. A canceled or replaced layer does not fire this hook, and a wrapper fires it only by drawing the vanilla body. Only the heart sprites change, not the layout or the separate armour, food, vehicle-health or air layers. Client-side. |
| `player_input` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest sent the server's current `PlayerInput` flags, after they were applied to the hub. The guest's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_insert_lectern_book` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player inserted a book into a lectern, after the block entity and inventory changed. `book` is the item id. Server-side. |
| `player_inventory_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `player_inventory_changed(player, stacks)`: a server player's inventory changed, at the moment vanilla's `InventoryChangeTrigger` is checked (the per-tick check found another item or count in a slot), heard before any criterion and whether or not the tree has `inventory_changed` advancements. `stacks` are the 41 slots in `Inventory` numbering: 0..36 the main inventory (0..9 the hotbar), 36..40 the armour (feet, legs, chest, head), 40 the off hand. It also fires at a player's first check after joining, so a mod sees what they log in with. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers read that player. Single player, LAN guests and a dedicated server's players; never on a client of a remote server. Quest, collection and 'obtain an item' mods need it instead of polling every inventory. Bundles need "hooks": @API:player_inventory_changed@. |
| `player_item_cooldown` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | An item cooldown is about to start for a server player. `item` is the item id and `ticks` its duration. The player's state is not swapped in. Set `cancel` to skip starting the cooldown. Server-side. |
| `player_item_frame_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player changed an item frame after its contents or rotation changed. `action` is `put`, `rotate`, `remove_item` or `break`; `item` is empty when no item remains, and `rotation` is the resulting step. Server-side. |
| `player_item_mend` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Mending repaired a server player's item with experience from an orb. `repair` is durability restored and `experience` is the orb's value. Server-side. |
| `player_item_smithed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.ItemSmithedEvent` | `PlayerEvent.ItemSmithedEvent`: a server player took `result` from a smithing table (`SmithingMenu.onTake`, or `onQuickCraft` for a shift-click): `player` is their UUID and `player_entity_id` their network id, `inputs` the template, base and addition as they were before the take used them (`None` for an empty slot). It comes once the click is done, so the menu already holds the used inputs, but before the table sounds. Server-side. |
| `player_jump` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's accepted movement made the server jump them from the ground. Server-side. |
| `player_kicked` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A network or fake guest was kicked, after the hub recorded the rendered `reason`. The guest's state is swapped in unless they were already removed in the same poll. |
| `player_launch_projectile` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player launched a projectile item, after it was created. `item` is the projectile's held item. |
| `player_lectern_page_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player changed the page of a lectern, after its page and redstone pulse changed. `page` is the zero-based new page. Server-side. |
| `player_level_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's experience level changed, after the new level was applied. |
| `player_load_from_file` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.LoadFromFile (not cancellable)` | A player was loaded from the world's save (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.LoadFromFile`, `PlayerList.loadPlayerData`): their `.dat` has been read and applied, and they are not placed in a level yet. `directory` is the world's player directory (`players/data`): a mod reads its own file there, named `<uuid>.<suffix>` as `getPlayerFile(suffix)` names it. Heard once for each join, for the owner of an integrated server as for anyone else, on a server, with no player's state swapped in. Not cancellable. |
| `player_logged_in` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerLoggedInEvent` | `player` (`name`) joined the game (NeoForge's `PlayerLoggedInEvent`): placed in the world and listed in `host.players().server_players()`, so `PlayerRef::send_payload` reaches them. Once a join, with no player's state swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_logged_out` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerLoggedOutEvent` | `player` (`name`) left the game (NeoForge's `PlayerLoggedOutEvent`), by any path: a lost connection, a kick, the server stopping or failing. Once for each player `player_logged_in` told of, and also for a kicked fake player without a preceding login. Silent `remove_fake` sends no logout. The player is still in `host.players().server_players()` when the hook fires. A listener's edits persist only on leaves this server saves. A completed proxy hand-over or a crash writes no logout save. Only when the server stops can a player still get a last message. A kicked fake has already left the list and is not saved. Server-side. |
| `player_login` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player whose login the server has accepted (NeoForge's `PlayerNegotiationEvent` stage): the name, whitelist, ban and server-full checks have passed and the player holds a place, but their saved state is not loaded and they are not placed in a level yet. Set `cancel` to refuse them: they are disconnected with `reason` (a plain "Disconnected" if it is left unset) and nothing else of the join (the join message, `player_logged_in`) happens. Once for each player who joins, on a server. The player is not placed yet and their client has no world to show a message in, so no player's state is swapped in and `PlayerRef::tell` reaches nobody: the refusal `reason` is the answer. The checks that need no mod (name, whitelist, bans, a full server, the proxy's forwarding) refuse earlier, on the connection, and this hook does not hear those players. |
| `player_login_profile` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The properties of an admitted login's profile, before `LoginFinished` sends them (`ServerLoginPacketListenerImpl.finishLoginAndWaitForClient`): the name, ban, whitelist, capacity and duplicate-player checks have passed; configuration and `player_login` come after. `properties` starts with the login's own, signed `textures` included when there are some, and none for an ordinary offline login; each mod gets the list the mods before it left, and the final list goes to login success and to the player's profile in the player list. The UUID and name are read-only, the login can't be refused here, and the hook can't wait for a profile lookup. |
| `player_loom_pattern_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected a loom pattern, after the loom menu accepted the button. `pattern` is the zero-based button index. Server-side. |
| `player_map_filled` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player filled an empty map, after the filled map and its saved data were created. Server-side. |
| `player_move` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's movement report is about to be applied: they would move from `from` to `to`, or turn from `from_rotation` to `to_rotation` (`[yaw, pitch]`). Heard once for each validated report whose position or rotation differs from the player's last reported location, after the server's own validation and before the report is committed. That location is kept on the player's connection, apart from the accepted position: it is the position given to the listener before it runs, and the destination of every server-side teleport (a join, a teleport in the level, a level change, a respawn, the resend of an unconfirmed teleport, and the correction after a veto). `from` is that location. Set `cancel` for a real veto and not a rollback: the report is not applied and its bookkeeping is skipped, and the client is corrected to the accepted position and rotation through the existing teleport correction. The report was already seen by the server's own checks before the hook, and what a violation of those would have done still happens. The values are the exact ones: there is no once-per-tick accumulation and no threshold in the engine, so the mod filters (`if (to - from).length() < 1.0 / 16.0 { return; }` skips anything under 1/16 of a block). Their state is swapped in (`host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell`). |
| `player_name_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player named a mob with a name tag, after its name and persistence changed and the tag was consumed. Server-side. |
| `player_name_format` | both | collect | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlayerEvent.NameFormat` | A player's display name was formatted. Replace `displayname` to change it. Both sides: `context.side()` says which. It runs wherever vanilla asks `Player.getDisplayName()`: when a chat line, a join or leave line, a command's feedback, a death message or an advancement message is built, and for the name tag over a player (every frame it shows, so it is not asked when no mod listens). Answer from the arguments and `context` alone, and fast: `context` holds the side, the registries and the settings of the resource generation, and a `/reload` builds the next one. A name the API can't carry (a component tree deeper than the text limit) skips the listeners for that call; a `displayname` the engine can't take back is dropped, and the name stays as it was. |
| `player_open_sign` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player opened a sign for editing, after the editor state was made. `front` selects the front or back side. Server-side. |
| `player_passage_cross` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `player`'s client crossed `passage` (#3775), one of the player's level's, from `from` (its feet before), and the server is about to follow: the move to `from` passed the move checks and both ends are tracked for the player, and the server has not yet worked out where the player comes out. Set `cancel` and the server refuses the crossing (`PassageCrossRefusal::Vetoed`) and puts the player back at `from`, as a portal Immersive Portals' `canTeleportEntity` turns down does. A listener that removes the passage or teleports the player refuses it too. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `player_pick_item` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player picked an item from a block or entity, before it enters the hotbar. `pos` is zero for an entity; `entity_id` is zero for a block. The player's state is swapped in. Set `cancel` to leave the inventory and client unchanged. |
| `player_pickup_arrow` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player picked up an arrow after it was removed and its pickup was applied. `item` is the arrow's pickup item id. Server-side. |
| `player_pong` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A connected `player` sent a play-phase pong (`ServerCommonPacketListenerImpl.handlePong`). Runs on the server thread at the packet's turn in that connection's inbox. `name` is the player's profile name; `id` is the signed 32-bit value the client sent. Unsolicited and repeated IDs are reported as-is: an ID does not prove the client answered a particular `PlayerRef::send_ping`. This notice is not cancellable and does not affect keep-alive latency. Configuration and status traffic do not fire it. Each listener gets its own event. |
| `player_ready_arrow` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player released a drawn bow with `arrow` selected, by the use-item key or a mod's `LivingRef::release_using_item`. The player's state is swapped in. |
| `player_recipe_book_click` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's recipe book requested a known recipe be placed in their crafting grid. `recipe` is the recipe id; `all` is the shift click form. Server-side. |
| `player_recipe_book_settings_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's authoritative recipe book pair changed. `book` is a typed RecipeBookIdentity, never a custom vanilla ordinal. Rejected and unchanged custom requests do not notify. The guest is not swapped in. |
| `player_recipe_discovered` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A recipe is about to be unlocked for a server player. Set `cancel` to keep it locked. The player's state is not swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_respawn_position` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerRespawnPositionEvent` | `PlayerRespawnPositionEvent`: where `player` respawns, once the server has found it: the bed or anchor they stand up at (facing it), or the world spawn's free column. `original_level` and `original_position` are that point; `missing_respawn_block` says the bed or anchor was gone, so the player came back at the world spawn. Change `level`, `position`, `yaw` and `pitch` to choose their final destination; no preliminary placement is made. `copy_original_spawn_position` (default: the block wasn't missing) says whether they keep their respawn point. Runs on the removed original player, before `player_clone` and placement. To change the point copied for future respawns, call `event.player.set_respawn_config(host, ...)` here, even on death, and set `copy_original_spawn_position` to true. This does not change the current destination. `from_end_fight` is true for the respawn after the End credits, false for death. An ordinary End portal teleport does not run this hook. Server-side. |
| `player_respawned` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.PlayerRespawnEvent` | `player` (`name`) respawned (NeoForge's `PlayerRespawnEvent`): at the end of `PlayerList.respawn`, after placement and initial synchronization, with the replacement in scope. `end_conquered` is true after the End credits (the player keeps health, food and experience), false after death. An ordinary End portal teleport does not fire this hook. Server-side. |
| `player_riptide` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player launched with a riptide trident, after the velocity, spin attack and grounded lift have been applied. The player's state is swapped in. No reply is needed. |
| `player_save_to_file` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.SaveToFile (not cancellable)` | A player was saved to the world's store (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.SaveToFile`, `PlayerList.save`): when they leave or the world closes, when a dedicated or integrated server saves its guests, and for an integrated server's owner when the world saves them. Once for each save. `directory` is the world's player directory (`players/data`): write your own file there, named `<uuid>.<suffix>`, never the player's `.dat`. The player may be leaving, so no player's state is swapped in. Not cancellable. Autosave snapshots are written asynchronously: this notification runs on a later server tick after a successful write, possibly after WorldSaved. Live state may have advanced beyond the saved snapshot; a process exit before completions are drained can prevent notification delivery. |
| `player_set_spawn` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSetSpawnEvent` | NeoForge's `PlayerSetSpawnEvent`: a server player's respawn point is about to be set (`ServerPlayer.setRespawnPosition`), once for each call vanilla makes: every sleep in a bed (the same bed too, which refreshes its angle), an anchor that is a new point, `/spawnpoint`, and the copy to the replacement after `player_clone` when `player_respawn_position` allows copying (by default unless the saved block was missing). `pos` is the block position and `dimension` its level; `pos` is none when no point is set. `forced` is `/spawnpoint`'s flag. Cancel to keep the current point, including a point already set on the replacement by a clone listener. `player_entity_id` is the server's entity id for the player. Server-side. |
| `player_shear_entity` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player shearing a mob is about to do so. Set `cancel` to leave the mob and shears unchanged. The guest's state is swapped in. The listener may change the world: straight after it returns without cancelling, the game finds the mob again by its entity id and checks that it still exists, is not removed, and that the held stack keeps its item and count; if not, the click is spent and nothing is sheared. |
| `player_shield_disable` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's shield was disabled by a blocked melee hit. The shield cooldown is set immediately after this call. Server-side. |
| `player_spawn_phantoms` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSpawnPhantomsEvent` | `PlayerSpawnPhantomsEvent`: the phantom spawner is about to decide whether to try a spawn near a non-spectator server player, before its difficulty and rest rolls. `result` starts empty, meaning `Default` (skylight and sea level check); `Allow` skips that check and `Deny` stops the attempt. `phantoms` starts with a randomly drawn group size; set it to override the count. Without mods the original draw order is kept. |
| `player_sprint` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's sprint started or stopped: their client sent `START_SPRINTING` or `STOP_SPRINTING` (the sprint key, a double tap forward, or letting go) and the server's player is now `sprinting` (vanilla's `setSprinting` in `handlePlayerCommand`). Heard only when it changes. It cannot be stopped, and it hears what the client asks, not what the server does: a sprint that ends by itself (a hit's knockback, hunger, a wall) is not reported. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers and `PlayerRef::tell` work. Once for each change, on a server. |
| `player_start_spectating_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player started spectating `entity_id`, after their camera moved to it (`ServerPlayer.setCamera` with a new target: a spectator's click or `/spectate`) and their client was told. Heard once per change of target, not again while the camera follows it. The mirror of `player_stop_spectating_entity`. |
| `player_start_tracking` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.StartTracking` | A client started tracking an entity (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.StartTracking`, posted at the end of `ServerEntity.addPairing`): `player` (`name`) has just been sent the spawn bundle of `entity` (its ID, `uuid` its UUID, `kind` its type, `minecraft:zombie`) and counts as tracking it. It came into range, its client's chunks reached it, or it was put in the level; another player is an entity too, and a player never tracks their own. The entity is still in the level. What a listener sends the player (`PlayerRef::send_payload`) reaches it right after that bundle, before the entity's next changes. Heard in the tick's tracking pass, after the pass has built every player's packets: the player already counts as tracking the other entities the same pass paired. The engine is still walking its tracker: read the game and send payloads, but don't spawn, remove or teleport entities from here. No player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_stat_award` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `StatAwardEvent` | A statistic is about to be awarded to a server player, before its count and scoreboard criteria change. `stat_type` and `stat` are ids such as `minecraft:mined` and `minecraft:stone`, and `value` the amount. All three may be rewritten; ids must use their canonical namespaced spelling. Unknown types or entries, noncanonical ids and negative amounts are refused together, warning once and keeping the entire original award. Set `cancel` to award nothing. Server-side. Ordinary interactions fire at vanilla's award point; inside a transaction, the award and this hook wait until the outermost successful commit, after tentative world and inventory writes. |
| `player_stays_on_ground_surface` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The player's crouch edge backoff (`Player.maybeBackOffFromEdge`) asks whether the player is staying on the ground surface (`Player.isStayingOnGroundSurface`, vanilla: the shift key is down). `entity` is the player's id; a mod may change `staying`, so a mod can make the player hold to an edge without crouching, or walk off while crouching. Fires on both sides, on the client's replayed ticks too, with the loop `context` of the side that ticks the player (its registries, settings and mod list): register the mod on both, and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs so prediction and the server agree. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `player_stonecutter_recipe_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected a stonecutter recipe, after the menu accepted its button. `recipe` is the zero-based button index. Server-side. |
| `player_stop_spectating_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player stopped spectating `entity_id`, after their camera returned to the player. |
| `player_stop_tracking` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerEvent.StopTracking` | A client stopped tracking an entity (NeoForge's `PlayerEvent.StopTracking`, posted at the end of `ServerEntity.removePairing`): `entity` went out of range, left the level or died, or `player` (`name`) left, changed level or respawned, which stops everything they tracked. `entity`, `uuid` and `kind` are as `player_start_tracking` told, once for each start; the player no longer counts as tracking it. A stop of the tracking pass is heard right after the removal packet, and what a listener sends the player reaches it after that removal. A leaving player's stops come after `player_logged_out`; those of a respawn or a level change, which send their own removals, at the next tracking pass. The entity is not necessarily still there, and a player who left is gone. As in `player_start_tracking`, the engine is walking its tracker: don't spawn, remove or teleport entities from here. No player's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_stop_using_item` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player released the use-item key, or a mod's `LivingRef::release_using_item` did, before the item finished being used. The player's state is swapped in. |
| `player_swap_hands` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LivingSwapItemsEvent.Hands` | Heard before a non-spectator player's swap-hands action. The two mutable stacks start as the off-hand and main-hand stacks; the hands receive the listener's outputs unless it cancels. slots. Set `cancel` to keep the items where they are: the player's client is sent both slots again. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Once for each press, on a server: a LAN or dedicated server's players, the single-player owner and a LAN host. Not heard: the same swap made in an open inventory (hovering a slot and pressing the key, a menu click). |
| `player_swap_with_equipment_slot` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player swapped their main and off hands, after both inventory slots changed. Server-side. |
| `player_switch_hotbar_slot_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Post` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Post`: a server player switched from hotbar slot `old_slot` to `new_slot` (0 to 8), after the selected slot changed and a main-hand item use stopped. Fired only when the slot differs and `player_switch_hotbar_slot_pre` didn't cancel it. The player's state is not swapped in. |
| `player_switch_hotbar_slot_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Pre` | `PlayerSwitchHotbarSlotEvent.Pre`: a server player is switching from hotbar slot `old_slot` to `new_slot` (0 to 8), after the slot range check and before the selected slot changes. Fired only when the slot differs. The player's state is not swapped in. Set `cancel` to keep the old slot; the client is sent that slot again, and `player_switch_hotbar_slot_post` doesn't fire. |
| `player_tab_list_name_format` | server | collect | `&dyn LoopContext` | `PlayerEvent.TabListNameFormat` | ServerPlayer.getTabListDisplayName: the name the Tab list shows for `player`, None to let the client choose it. Replace `display_name` to change it. It runs while the Tab list is built. Answer from the arguments and `context` alone, and fast: `context` holds the side, the registries and the settings of the resource generation, and a `/reload` builds the next one. A name the API can't carry skips the listeners for that call; a `display_name` the engine can't take back is dropped, and the name stays as it was. |
| `player_take_lectern_book` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player took the book from a lectern, after the menu and block entity changed. `book` is the removed item id. Server-side. |
| `player_teleport_end_gateway` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player is about to travel through an End gateway at `gateway`. Set `cancel` to leave them at the entrance; the gateway is not consumed and its cooldown does not start. Server-side. |
| `player_teleported` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player a command teleported (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent`, for a player): `/tp` and `/teleport`, inside their level or into another one. `from` and `to` are their feet before and after, `dimension` the level they are in now. Heard after the move has reached the player's state and their client (an inter-level teleport: when the new level is entered, before it has loaded around them), so it cannot stop it. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers that read the acting player and `PlayerRef::tell` work. Once for each teleported player, on a server. Ender pearls and chorus fruit have their own hooks; a spectator's teleport menu is not heard yet; portals and respawns are not teleports here. |
| `player_tick_post` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerTickEvent.Post` | After a player's tick on both logical sides (NeoForge 26.3 `PlayerTickEvent.Post`). Non-cancellable. On the client, fires through death tick 20, but not after the local player is removed. `player` is the same identity as in `player_tick_pre`; on the server their state is acting. Runs after body work and server-side item, arrow and XP pickups, so reads see the completed tick. Client reads see only the state available to that client, not future server updates. On the server, neither player tick hook fires for a spectator whose bounds touch an unloaded chunk; that player's body tick is skipped. |
| `player_tick_pre` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `PlayerTickEvent.Pre` | Before a player's tick on both logical sides (NeoForge 26.3 `PlayerTickEvent.Pre`). Non-cancellable. On the client, fires through death tick 20, but not after the local player is removed. `player` identifies the ticked player; on the server their state is acting. Fires before sleep, cooldowns and body work. Players still tick under `/tick freeze`, but not while the level is paused. On the server, neither player tick hook fires for a spectator whose bounds touch an unloaded chunk; that player's body tick is skipped. |
| `player_toggle_entity_age_lock` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player toggled an ageable mob's age lock with a golden dandelion, after the lock and item use changed. |
| `player_toggle_flight` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's client started (`flying`) or stopped flying (vanilla's `handlePlayerAbilities`, which honours it only for a player who may fly: creative or spectator), heard once for each change, on a server, with the player's state swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` and the other helpers reach them. The player is already flying or not as asked, as vanilla leaves them; set `cancel` to refuse it: they keep the state they had and the server sends their abilities back, as vanilla does for what it refuses, so the client lands or flies again. Only what a client asks for is heard: not the flight a game-mode change starts or ends (`/gamemode`), and not a LAN host's own player. |
| `player_toggle_sneak` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest's client changed sneaking in its player input packet, after the hub applied the new state. The guest's state is swapped in. Server-side. |
| `player_totem` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A totem of undying saved the server player `player` (`name`): their hurt would have killed them and the totem held in either hand fired (`LivingEntity.checkTotemDeathProtection`; Bukkit's `EntityResurrectEvent`), heard just before it is used up and its effects apply. Listen only: whether the totem saves is decided inside the health state machine, before a mod is asked, so a mod cannot veto it from here. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. When a fall triggered the totem, the same landing is still in progress. Once for each totem, on a server; the host of a LAN world's totem is not heard. |
| `player_trade` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player completed a villager or wandering trader trade. `entity_id` is the merchant network id. Server-side. |
| `player_unleash_entity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player removed an entity's leash, after it was cleared. |
| `player_velocity` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server applied `velocity` to a player, when syncing changed motion to that player's client. |
| `player_xp_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerXpEvent.XpChange` | A server player's experience points are about to change through `Player.giveExperiencePoints` (NeoForge's `PlayerXpEvent.XpChange`): an orb pickup after mending, an advancement reward, or `/xp add ... points`. Change the signed `amount` (in points) or set `cancel` to give nothing. Experience bottles and furnaces make orbs, so they come through the orb path. Server-side. |
| `player_xp_level_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PlayerXpEvent.LevelChange` | A server player's level is about to change through `Player.giveExperienceLevels`: once per level gained or lost by points, and for the anvil, `/experience add ... levels` and `PlayerRef::give_levels`. Change `levels`, or set `cancel` to leave the level unchanged. The player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them. Server-side. |
| `portal_create` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A Nether portal was built for a dimension travel, or an End platform was made for an arrival. `pos` is the lower corner of the Nether portal rectangle or the End platform origin. The completed blocks are already in the world. |
| `portal_spawn` | server | cancel | `&dyn LevelHost` | `BlockEvent.PortalSpawnEvent` | A fire placed at `pos` (`state`) found an empty obsidian frame, `shape`, and is about to fill it with a nether portal (`BlockEvent.PortalSpawnEvent`, in `BaseFireBlock.onPlace`), in the Overworld or the Nether. Set `cancel` and no portal forms: the fire stays where it can survive, as without a frame. Server-side. |
| `potential_spawns` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `LevelEvent.PotentialSpawns` | Possible natural spawns at a server position (NeoForge's `LevelEvent.PotentialSpawns`, `NaturalSpawner.mobsAt`). Mods may add or remove entries, or cancel to make the list empty. Mods can read the world, but cannot write it during the spawn pass. |
| `potion_brew_post` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PotionBrewEvent.Post` | A brewing stand at `pos` brewed (`PotionBrewEvent.Post`, at the end of `BrewingStandBlockEntity.doBrew`), or `potion_brew_pre` was cancelled: `stacks` holds its five slots after the brew (or as the cancelling mods left them), and what the mods leave there is the stand's. Fires last, as `doBrew` ends: after `brewing_complete`, the brew sound and the ingredient's remainder dropping. Server-side. |
| `potion_brew_pre` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `PotionBrewEvent.Pre` | A brewing stand at `pos` is about to brew (`PotionBrewEvent.Pre`, in `BrewingStandBlockEntity.doBrew`): `stacks` is a copy of its five slots (three bottles, the ingredient, the fuel). Set `cancel` to skip vanilla's brew: the stand then takes `stacks` as the mods left them, uses no ingredient and plays no brew sound, and `potion_brew_post` fires with those slots; `brewing_complete` doesn't, as nothing was brewed. Uncancelled, changes to `stacks` are dropped. Server-side. |
| `potion_splash` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A thrown splash or lingering potion applied its hit effects. |
| `prepare_hopper_transfer` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A hopper at `pos` began an item transfer attempt. This runs after its cooldown and enabled checks, before pushing or pulling any items. Server-side, including the single-player server. |
| `prepare_item_craft` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A crafting-grid result was prepared for a server player; `pos` is the crafting table, or None for the inventory grid. Change `result` before it is sent. An accepted result retains valid damage even when it exceeds that stack's maximum durability. |
| `prepare_item_enchant` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Enchanting offers were calculated for a server player at `pos`. Change `costs` before they are sent to the menu. |
| `prepare_smithing` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A smithing result was prepared for a server player at `pos`. Change it before it is sent. |
| `projectile_impact` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ProjectileImpactEvent` | A projectile hit something (NeoForge's `ProjectileImpactEvent`, posted where vanilla's projectile has found what it meets, before the hit acts): `projectile` (its entity id) of type `kind` (`minecraft:arrow`), shot by `owner` (a mob's or a player's entity id, 0 for none) in the level `dimension`. It hit the entity `entity` of type `entity_kind` (a player is `minecraft:player`; a dragon's part is `minecraft:ender_dragon`), or, with `entity` None, the block at `block` on its `face` (the face's normal, `[0, 1, 0]` for the top), at the point `at`. Set `canceled` and the impact does not happen: no damage, no knockback, no sound, no block reaction, the arrow does not stick, a pearl does not teleport, a potion or a fireball does not burst. Cancellation alone leaves the projectile alive. Resolve the live owner with `host.entity(event.projectile)` and call `EntityRef::discard` to consume it instead. Removal suppresses the hit even if the event is not canceled. A decision invalidated by listener writes is not recomputed. A flying bobber skips hooking and redirection to the hit point, but its ordinary move still stops against solid blocks. Water is not an impact. Bobbers notify before vanilla's entity deflection and world-border bounce; rockets notify after deflection and cancellation skips their impact explosion only. Covers arrows, tridents, thrown items, fireballs, wither skulls, wind charges, llama spit, shulker bullets, bobbers and rockets. Other families skip breeze deflections and hits on wind charges. A dragon part or End crystal is told by the id of the dragon or crystal. Heard only when a mod also sets `hears_projectile_impacts`. Server-side. Version 2 adds bobbers and rockets; listeners retaining the earlier family set filter out `minecraft:fishing_bobber` and `minecraft:firework_rocket`. |
| `projectile_launch` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server projectile was added to the level. `player` is set only for a player launch; other sources have an empty `name`. |
| `raid_spawn_wave` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A raid wave has been spawned at `pos`; `wave` is its one-based wave number and `raiders` counts the raiders, not the mobs they ride. |
| `raid_state_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server raid at `pos` changed to `state` (`triggered`, `victory`, `loss` or `stopped`). `raid` identifies its owning level, including a parked level. A terminal raid already removed from storage cannot be read. |
| `recipe_book_categories` | both | listen | `HostSide` | — | Supply categories and vanilla-shaped displays for custom recipe types. Asked lazily once per recipe table. Use the Host's recipe table to build `RecipeBookCategory::displays` from the type's own opaque recipe JSON. Each category names its menu, book identity and per-book search group. The display/index load boundary returns typed errors; screen integration is separate. This declaration does not register a settings book. |
| `recipe_property_sets` | both | collect | `&dyn DataLoadContext` | `RegisterRecipePropertiesEvent` | Items a mod's own recipe type takes in a station slot, added to the recipe property sets (`RecipeManager.RECIPE_PROPERTY_SETS`): what a furnace, blast furnace, smoker, campfire, smithing table slot or brewing stand slot accepts, and what a client predicts it accepts. Push one `RecipePropertyAddition` per set to add to; the vanilla recipes' own items stay in. Called once per load, after the recipes are resolved, with the `DataLoadContext` of the load that owns them (`origin()` `ServerDataLoad` on a server, `ClientPreload` on a client's own load), and again after `/reload`. A new namespaced key creates a set when its namespace belongs to the listener's manifest (its ID or `namespaces`) or to a content package it serves (`served_by`). Later listeners may extend existing sets. Invalid or foreign new keys, unknown vanilla keys, and air are logged and skipped; unknown items are ignored. Custom sets are synced to clients alongside vanilla sets. The answer must be the same every time. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `recipe_sources` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Additional ingredient containers for server player `player` placing or checking a recipe in the mod crafting menu `menu` (`namespace:path`) at `pos`. The player's state is not swapped in. Push block positions to `sources`; containers are used after the player's inventory, in mod order and then push order. The engine silently skips `pos`, duplicates (first wins), unavailable or non-lendable containers, and all containers after the first 64 accepted sources, without per-call logging. Chest-like block entities and mod containers with declared slots are lendable. No answer preserves vanilla placement. The engine takes ingredients and writes changed containers back, firing `container_changed`; this hook only names sources. Asked once per recipe-book placement/apply or server transfer check, not per tick. |
| `recipes_received` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RecipesReceivedEvent` | The server's recipes of `recipe_types` arrived (NeoForge's `RecipesReceivedEvent`): from now on the recipe helpers (`native_recipes`, `native_recipes_of_type`) give the server's recipes of those types in place of the window's own, so rebuild what you made of them. Vanilla serializers expose resolved candidates, not native ingredient predicates; matching on the server remains authoritative. The window retains the resolved recipes of the types not sent. It comes after a join, once the window has its player, and after every `/reload`, on a server whose mods asked for them (`native_datapack_sync`), single player included; with no types when the server stopped sending them and the window is back on its own recipes. A recipe's recipe book ids are not part of what arrives: `recipe_book_ids` answers them. Client-side. |
| `register_block_state_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterBlockStateModels` | Register the block-state definition types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterBlockStateModels`, its `registerDefinition`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterBlockStateModelsEvent::register` to bind each type identifier to its provider callback; a blockstate file whose `neoforge:definition_type` names a registered id invokes that provider directly on every load and reload. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one. |
| `register_block_tint_sources` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.BlockTintSources` | The tint sources of blocks (NeoForge's `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.BlockTintSources`, vanilla's `BlockColors.register`): `register(layers, blocks)`, and a model face with `tintindex` i is multiplied by layer i of its block's list; a face past the list is not tinted. A layer is one of vanilla's sources (`VanillaTint`) or the mod's own `BlockTintSource`. Blocks are ids, a mod's or vanilla's, and a later registration for a block replaces the earlier one, vanilla's own colours included. Asked once at start on the client, before resources load; every resource load, a pack reload included, applies the registrations again. An id no block has is logged and skipped. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_cauldron_fluid_content` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterCauldronFluidContentEvent` | Say which fluid, and how much, one of your cauldron blocks holds (NeoForge `RegisterCauldronFluidContentEvent`), so the fluid capability (`FLUIDS`) fills and drains it as it does vanilla's water and lava cauldrons. Asked once the block registry is resolved, before any world opens, and again on a resource reload. Give `event.contents.register(block, fluid, total, level)` the block and fluid keys your `register` hook requested, the millibuckets the cauldron holds when full, and the name of its integer level property, or none for a cauldron only ever full. The engine refuses, with one logged warning naming both sides, a block that already has a content, a fluid that already has a cauldron, an unknown block or fluid, a level property the block lacks or that isn't an integer, and a total of 0 or one that doesn't divide evenly by the levels; the rest of a mod's contents still apply. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_cauldron_interaction` | both | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterCauldronInteractionEvent` | Register cauldron item interactions, by exact item or by item tag, on both sides before snapshot publication. Vanilla dye tags take precedence, then item tags in registration order, then exact items; registering the same key again overwrites the entry in place. Registered callbacks run on the server through `Interaction::interact`. A panic or phase refusal discards that listener's entries, including queued registrations to all dispatchers, before the next listener runs. |
| `register_client_commands` | client | collect | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RegisterClientCommandsEvent` | The client's own commands (NeoForge's `RegisterClientCommandsEvent`), which `command` runs: push a `Command` per usage (`x <mode:word>`). Asked when a connection starts and again when the server sends its own tree, so the chat box colours, completes and lists them as the game's own; a name the game already has keeps that command's behaviour. A client command has no operator level: one with a nonzero `level` is left out. |
| `register_client_item_extensions` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterClientExtensionsEvent` | Register this load's worn equipment models, textures and item providers together. Registration is client-only and repeats on every resource load, before any reload listener applies. The validated bindings are installed with the candidate models. Models are baked once and may use any namespace owned by the registering mod (its manifest namespaces or a content package it serves via `served_by`); frame callbacks choose references, never geometry. A duplicate item provider or an unknown texture returned by a completed listener refuses preparation with a named diagnostic. A panic or phase refusal discards that listener's models, textures and item providers together. Do not retain references from a discarded registration. Server-only packages cannot listen. |
| `register_client_tooltip_component_factories` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterClientTooltipComponentFactoriesEvent` | Register client tooltip component factories for custom `TooltipComponentData` types (NeoForge's `RegisterClientTooltipComponentFactoriesEvent`). Asked once at client startup. Call `event.factories.register::<MyData, MyClientComponent>(\|data\| ...)` to map each custom `TooltipComponent` produced by `ItemPart::tooltip_image` or `render_tooltip_gather_components` into a `ClientTooltipComponent` that measures and draws itself. A later registration for the same type replaces the earlier one. A listener that panics leaves all previously registered factories in place and publishes none of its own. |
| `register_color_resolvers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ColorResolvers` | The colour resolvers block tints blend over the biomes around a block (NeoForge's `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ColorResolvers`): `register(resolver)` adds the mod's own `BiomeColorResolver` and returns the `ColorResolver` to pass to `ClientHost::biome_tint` or `BlockAndTintGetter::block_tint`. The game blends a mod's resolver over the biome blend radius and caches it per section while it meshes, as it does vanilla's grass, foliage and water. Asked once at start on the client, before `register_block_tint_sources` and before resources load. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_commands` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterCommandsEvent` | The commands native mods add to the server's command tree: push each usage with its executor attached by `Command::executes`. Asked whenever the server builds its command tree, as the reference loader's `Commands` does: when a world opens (a dedicated server, a LAN host, single player's server thread), before its functions load, so a function line may name your command; on each `/reload` and data pack change, so a command you stop pushing is refused by the next functions loaded; and after a mod calls `host.commands().refresh_commands()`. Every player's chat box completes them as vanilla's, within their level. A usage under a root vanilla owns merges into it, as `RegisterCommandsEvent` registers into vanilla's `CommandDispatcher` (Brigadier's `CommandNode.addChild`): vanilla's usages and permissions stay, the mod's children are added beside them, and an executor at a vanilla node replaces vanilla's (see `Command`). A lower-level child opens traversal through a prefix without changing that prefix's own execution permission. When accepted usages execute at the same node (including optional prefixes), each later accepted registration overwrites that node's executor (matching Brigadier's `CommandNode.addChild`, which sets `child.command = node.getCommand()`) together with Sunrise's paired execution permission level for that node. Only that node's executor runs, attributed to its mod; if the owner is disabled, execution is refused rather than handed to another mod. Clients get the command tree, not executors. An argument's kind is one of `word`, `text`, `integer`, `integer(min,max)`, `long`, `long(min,max)`, `number`, `boolean`, `player`, `item`, `block_pos` and `vec3`, or a command argument type a mod registered (`Registrar::register_command_argument`), named by its namespaced id. |
| `register_configs` | both | collect | `&dyn ConfigContext` | `ModContainer.registerConfig` | The config files native mods keep (NeoForge's `registerConfig`): push yours, each of a kind, with the text written to `config/` when the file is missing. Asked by the window when the game starts (its `Client` and `Common` configs), by a dedicated server when it starts (`Common` and `Server`) and by a server before each world it opens (`Server`); a game reads only its own kinds, so push them all every time. Receives a `ConfigContext` with settings reads and the scoped `ConfigFiles` service. A file outside `config/`, or one already registered, is skipped. |
| `register_configuration_tasks` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `RegisterConfigurationTasksEvent` | Append tasks for this connecting profile, on the server thread after the built-in configuration steps. Runs for every peer, including vanilla. The profile has no player handle yet. Use `event.connection.has_channel` to check what the peer negotiated and `event.register(task)` to give this connection an owned task. Tasks start in registration order, including tasks of the same type, and can finish, wait or fail this peer. A panic while reading a task's type refuses admission and disables that task implementation for its mod. If a registration listener itself panics, only its staged tasks, diagnostic and journaled writes are discarded. Other listeners still configure the same peer, and the panicking registration listener is skipped for later peers. Delayed tasks complete via `IPayloadContext::finish_configuration_task` with their task type from either a server main-thread or network-thread configuration payload handler. There is no task timeout: answering keep-alives can hold configuration indefinitely. A silent or disconnected connection still ends normally. |
| `register_debug_entries` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterDebugEntriesEvent` | Register F3 debug entries once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterDebugEntriesEvent`). Each mod receives an empty batch. Push `DebugEntry::text` with its line callback or `DebugEntry::renderer` for a renderer toggle. Call `DebugEntry::with_reduced_debug_info` to opt an entry into showing under reduced debug info (default `false`, matching vanilla's `DebugScreenEntry.isAllowed`). Text callbacks run only while their entry is visible. Malformed or duplicate IDs (including built-in vanilla entry IDs) are logged and left out. |
| `register_debug_renderers` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterDebugRenderersEvent` | Collect world-space debug renderers at each client renderer-list refresh (NeoForge's `RegisterDebugRenderersEvent`). Push `DebugRenderer::new(draw)` for each renderer that should run until the next refresh. Use `event.is_debug_entry_enabled(id)` to gate a renderer on a vanilla or startup `DebugEntry` switch, or push unconditionally. |
| `register_dimension_transition_screen` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterDimensionTransitionScreenEvent` | Register loading-screen factories once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterDimensionTransitionScreenEvent`). Each mod receives an empty batch. Conditional pairs take priority over incoming, then outgoing. The first valid factory for a key wins. Unmatched travel, login and death use vanilla for a new screen. The client keeps whichever presentation is already open on an in-place load update. |
| `register_enchantment_damage_effects` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Register namespaced enchantment damage value effects once before content loads.
