| `register_entity_layers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.AddLayers` | The layers a mod adds to living entity renderers (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.AddLayers`): `add(target, layer)`, and every living entity of the type, or every player of the player model, in view draws `EntityRenderLayer::submit` after its model and the layers added before. The final body model is borrowed for `submit`; it is `None` when no body was posed, as after a successful replacement renderer. `entity_types`, `is_living`, `player_models` and `has_mod_renderer` tell what renderers this load built. A target that is no living entity type is refused by name. Asked on the client on every resource load, the first one and each reload, after the renderers resolve (`register_entity_renderers`): layers don't carry over from one load to the next, so add them again each time. In the ordered body pass, layers submit in level join order, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions. If this listener panics, none of its registrations take effect; later listeners still register in the same order. |
| `register_entity_renderers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterRenderers` | The code-defined renderers of entity types (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterRenderers`, `registerEntityRenderer`): `register(type, renderer)`, and every entity of the type `type` (`mymod:bolt`) in view is drawn by `EntityRenderer::submit` in place of the type's own drawing. A later registration for a type replaces the earlier one; a type the game does not know is logged with its id and draws nothing new. Asked once at start on the client, before resources load; the game resolves each type's renderer on every resource load. This build draws the registered renderers of content mods' thrown types, of the living types drawn as mobs (in place of the model and its vanilla layers), of primed TNT (in place of the flashing cube), of experience orbs (in place of the billboarded orb quad) and of paintings (in place of the painting's quads) and of item frames and glow item frames (in place of the frame model and the item or map on its face); general entity types draw only through their renderer, if registered. That renderer reads the published generation of synced attachments through `EntityRenderState::attachments`. Other entity families keep their own drawing, including the dragon and end crystals. General, living, primed TNT and experience orb renderers submit at each entity's slot in level join order. If this listener panics, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_entity_spectator_shaders` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEntitySpectatorShadersEvent` | Register the screen effect spectating an entity type puts on, once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterEntitySpectatorShadersEvent`), as vanilla's creeper, spider and enderman do. Pass each `EntitySpectatorShader` to `event.register(shader)`. The first registration for an entity type wins, vanilla's own counting as first, so a mod can't change what the vanilla mobs show; a skipped or malformed registration is logged. The effect applies while the first-person view follows that entity, and the third-person view or leaving it clears it. |
| `register_fluid_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterFluidModelsEvent` | The models of your world fluids (NeoForge's `RegisterFluidModelsEvent`): call `event.register(model)` with a `FluidModel` per fluid whose look doesn't say all of it. | `register_game_tests` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `RegisterGameTestsEvent` | Register namespaced test functions before the world's test definitions load. | `register_gui_layers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterGuiLayersEvent` | Register, wrap or replace a HUD layer with an owned draw callback (NeoForge's `RegisterGuiLayersEvent`). | `register_item_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterItemModelsEvent` | Register the item model node types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterItemModelsEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Any valid `Identifier` (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla or engine item model type id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_item_tint_sources` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ItemTintSources` | Register the item tint sources a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterColorHandlersEvent.ItemTintSources`). Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla tint source id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_json_animation_types` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent` | Register the custom animation targets and interpolations a mod adds for keyframe animation files to name (NeoForge's `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent`). Use `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent::register_target` and `RegisterJsonAnimationTypesEvent::register_interpolation` to bind each identifier directly to its callbacks. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; duplicates and collisions with the built-in targets (`minecraft:position`, `minecraft:rotation`, `minecraft:scale`) or built-in interpolations (`minecraft:linear`, `minecraft:catmullrom`) are rejected as registration errors. |
| `register_layer_definitions` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterLayerDefinitions` | Register suppliers for entity model layers (NeoForge's `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterLayerDefinitions`). | `register_load_conditions` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Register named conditions once at mod load, before server data admission. | `register_model_loaders` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `ModelEvent.RegisterLoaders` | Register the model loaders a mod adds (NeoForge's `ModelEvent.RegisterLoaders`). The callback's quads replace the cuboid geometry unless the loader explicitly delegates to the cuboid decoder. An absent required loader rejects its declaring model file. Duplicates and collisions with built-ins (`neoforge:composite`, `neoforge:obj`, `fusion:model`) are registration errors. |
| `register_particle_groups` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterParticleGroupsEvent` | Register the render groups a mod's particles draw in (NeoForge's `RegisterParticleGroupsEvent`). Called once at client startup. Use `RegisterParticleGroupsEvent::register` to bind an ID to a factory run once per particle engine construction. Sprite loads and reloads only resolve each particle type against the constructed groups. |
| `register_particle_providers` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterParticleProvidersEvent` | Binds a logical client's sprite-set providers to already registered `ParticleType`s (NeoForge's `RegisterParticleProvidersEvent`). Each provider creates particles from typed options, ticks its live particles in one batch, and extracts quads for visible particles. Later registration for the same type replaces the previous provider. Registration runs once before resources load; each reload rebinds the sprite sets and discards live provider particles. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `register_preset_editors` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterPresetEditorsEvent` | Register client editor factories for world-preset keys once at startup (NeoForge's `RegisterPresetEditorsEvent`). Use `RegisterPresetEditorsEvent::register` to bind each preset identifier directly to its factory. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same preset replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a vanilla preset (`minecraft:flat`, `minecraft:single_biome_surface`) overrides its built-in editor. Unregistered presets retain their built-in editor, or their disabled Customize button. |
| `register_range_select_item_model_property` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | Register the numeric `minecraft:range_dispatch` item model properties a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterRangeSelectItemModelPropertyEvent::register` to bind each property identifier directly to its decode-time factory, which parses the `range_dispatch` node's JSON object once on each load/reload and returns the per-stack numeric callback. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla property id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_render_pipelines` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterRenderPipelinesEvent` | Register the mod's own render pipelines, once at client startup (NeoForge's `RegisterRenderPipelinesEvent`; 1.21.1 mods used `RegisterShadersEvent`): call `event.register(pipeline)` with each `RenderPipeline`. | `register_select_item_model_property` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent` | Register the categorical `minecraft:select` item model properties a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterSelectItemModelPropertyEvent::register` to bind each property identifier directly to its decode-time factory, which parses the `select` node's JSON object once on each load/reload and returns both the per-case `when` value decoder (`&Tag -> Result<T, String>`) and the per-stack categorical callback (`-> Option<T>`). Any valid `Identifier` (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one (last-writer-wins), and registering a built-in vanilla property id overrides that built-in when decoding item definitions. |
| `register_special_model_renderer` | client | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterSpecialModelRendererEvent` | Register factories for special item model types (NeoForge's `RegisterSpecialModelRendererEvent`). Called once at client startup, before resources load. The last registration of a type wins, including for vanilla's types. Each `minecraft:special` node is decoded and baked afresh on resource reload; the baked renderer submits to a scoped collector when the item is drawn. A listener that panics or incurs a phase refusal leaves the previous factories and replacement reports unchanged. |
| `register_sprite_sources` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterSpriteSourcesEvent` | Register the atlas sprite source types a mod adds (NeoForge's `RegisterSpriteSourcesEvent`). Asked once at start on the client, before resources load. Use `RegisterSpriteSourcesEvent::register` to bind each type identifier directly to its provider callback; an entry of an atlas definition's `sources` whose `type` is a registered id invokes that provider at its place in the list, on every load and reload. Any valid `<namespace>:<path>` identifier (including `minecraft:`) is accepted; a later registration for the same id replaces the earlier one, and registering a built-in vanilla source id overrides that built-in when decoding atlas definitions. |
| `register_texture_atlases` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterTextureAtlasesEvent` | Declare atlases beside the game's (`RegisterTextureAtlasesEvent`). Call `event.register(atlas)` with each `TextureAtlasRegistration`, which names the atlas definition that lists its sprites. Called at first client load and every resource reload, before textures are stitched. |
| `register_villager_professions` | server | listen | `&RegistrationContext` | `RegisterEvent` | Declare villager professions once per mod load, before world admission. The engine resolves every reference atomically with the owning resource tables. Vanilla replacement and overlapping custom acquisition POIs are refused. NeoForge adds a profession through `RegisterEvent` on `Registries.VILLAGER_PROFESSION`; this hook declares it as data, so only the registration moment corresponds. If this listener panics or a phase call is refused, none of its registrations take effect. |
| `render_arm` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderArmEvent` | Before this window's player's first-person `left` or right arm is drawn (`RenderArmEvent`, in `renderPlayerArm` and `renderMapHand`): the empty main hand's arm, and the arms under a held map, never while the player is invisible. `player` and `player_entity_id` are the local player's UUID and entity id. `draw`'s pose stack starts at the arm model's origin, in blocks, posed as vanilla's arm is (with any `render_hand`), lit with the light at the eyes; add geometry with `native_draw_cube`, `native_draw_quad`, `native_draw_block` and `native_draw_item`. Set `canceled` to leave out vanilla's arm; what the mods drew stays. Client-side. |
| `render_fog` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ViewportEvent.RenderFog` | Final world fog, after the computed color hook, ordinary calculations and Sunrise's fog option, before upload. Title panoramas skip it. Invalid color or distance values retain the original whole fog. |
| `render_frame_post` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RenderFrameEvent.Post` | Once a frame, in a world or not, right after the frame was rendered (NeoForge's `RenderFrameEvent.Post`). `elapsed` is the frame's time. It fires for a frame that was skipped too; `flip_frame` is the moment a frame was presented. `world_partial_tick` is the interpolation used by that frame, held while paused or frozen; `partial_tick` is the advancing window clock. Outside a world both use the window clock. Neither is recomputed after listeners run. Client-side. |
| `render_frame_pre` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `RenderFrameEvent.Pre` | Once a frame, in a world or not, after Sunrise's extras and just before the frame is rendered (NeoForge's `RenderFrameEvent.Pre`). `elapsed` is the frame's time. `world_partial_tick` is the interpolation about to be drawn, held while paused or frozen; `partial_tick` is the window clock, which keeps advancing. Outside a world both use the window clock. Listener writes do not change this frame's captured interpolation. Client-side. |
| `render_gui_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiEvent.Post` | After the complete in-world HUD layer walk (NeoForge's `RenderGuiEvent.Post`), including under F1, and before the open screen draws. Place a box with `event.corners.place` (boxes stack away from their edge) and draw with `event.draw`. Use `ClientHost::debug_overlay_visible()` to avoid drawing over the debug overlay. Not heard when a mod canceled `render_gui_pre`. A panicking listener's drawing and corner placement are both discarded. Client-side. |
| `render_gui_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderGuiEvent.Pre` | Before the in-world HUD layer walk (NeoForge's `RenderGuiEvent.Pre`), including under F1. Return `Flow::Cancel` to skip the walk (including layer Pre and Post), `render_gui_post` and the HUD chat layer. Focused chat is drawn by its screen, not the layer walk. Toasts draw after the active screen or overlay and are outside this veto. F1 hides ordinary toasts and audio stream failure notices, but not other callback error toasts. Does not fire when a server dialog or the connection screen covers the world HUD. Client-side. |
| `render_hand` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderHandEvent` | Before one first-person hand of this window's player is drawn (`RenderHandEvent`, client): a hand that holds an item or a map, the empty main hand's arm, and the empty off hand, where vanilla draws nothing. Adjust `render`'s pose, draw in the hand with `draw`, or cancel the default geometry; what the mods drew stays. Equip progress includes the item's swap scale. `draw`'s pose stack starts in the hands' view space, in blocks, +x right, +y up, -z into the screen, under the hand sway and before vanilla's per-item transforms; both hands share it, and `render.hand` says which one is asked. It is lit with the light at the eyes. `HandDrawContext::render_arm` submits either physical arm with the local avatar's skin, sleeve and model width, at the current draw pose and light. It joins the hand's own vertices, ahead of vanilla's: the hand's field of view, the view bob, a shader pack's hand pass and `hand_drawn` see it as they see vanilla's hand. Unlike NeoForge, the pose stack is `draw`'s own: turning it moves what you draw, `render`'s pose moves the default geometry, and player render state is not exposed. |
| `render_item_in_frame` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderItemInFrameEvent` | Before a visible item frame's item or map is drawn. Cancel hides the item, not the frame. |
| `render_level_stage` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderLevelStageEvent` | Draw world geometry at one stage of a world view (NeoForge 26.3's `RenderLevelStageEvent`), asked for each `RenderLevelStage` in frame order. `draw` is the view's `WorldDrawContext` and shares its limits with `submit_custom_geometry`; its lines and quads draw at `stage`, its `name_tag` labels with the world's name tags. Client-only, noncancellable. |
| `render_living_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderLivingEvent.Post` | After a living entity is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderLivingEvent.Post`): once a frame for each mob and player in view whose `render_living_pre` wasn't canceled, after its model, its vanilla layers and the mods' layers. `entity` is its render state, as `render_living_pre` has it; `draw` is posed at its feet, turned with its body, and what it draws is kept. `event.model` borrows the final `crate::FrameModel` the entity's layers saw, including custom pack models and the pose under layers-only draws. It is read-only and scoped to this callback. Use `part(name)` or the hierarchy to read a part's local `pose()` and effective `visible` flag after animation and Pre edits. A successful replacement renderer or other draw without a body pose supplies `None`. The order today: before this hook its model, its vanilla layers and the mods' layers are drawn; after it, the frame's later passes draw its name tag, its leash, and the flames of a burning entity and its shadow, each as its own pass. This hook neither sees nor replaces the flames and the shadow, and a canceled `render_living_pre` doesn't stop them. A type with a mod's registered renderer (`register_entity_renderers`) has the same later passes, the renderer in the model's place. The reference draws the leash and the name tag before this hook: that move is the follow-up in #1285. For a player, its `render_player_post` follows. In the ordered body pass, each entity's hooks finish before the next entity's submission begins, including any general entity renderer at its own level join slot. |
| `render_living_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderLivingEvent.Pre` | Before a living entity is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderLivingEvent.Pre`): once a frame for each mob and player in view (one the frame culls is not asked), before its pose, model and layers. `entity` is its render state (its id, kind, custom name, the frame's partial tick, its body and head turns and walk cycle, `LivingEntityRenderState`). `draw` is posed at its feet, turned with its body: +z the way it faces, +x its left, +y up, in blocks, with the helpers block entities draw with (`cube`, `quad`, `block`, `item`, `model_part`); what it draws is kept. Set `canceled` to skip all of the entity's drawing this frame: its model, armour, held items and layers, the mods' layers, its name tag, leash and glowing outline, and `render_living_post`, and the renderer a mod registered for its type (`register_entity_renderers`), which draws in the model's place; a mod drawing a model of its own draws it here and cancels. The flames of a burning entity and its shadow, which the frame queues after the entity's drawing, still draw. A player's `render_player_pre` comes first and edits the same `event.model`; a cancel here still calls `render_player_post` with `model: None`. `event.model` lends the selected body's baked hierarchy and bind pose as a `crate::FrameModelEdit`. Use `model.part("nose")` to get a villager's nose index, then `model.hide(index)` to leave it and its descendants out of the body and layers drawn on that model (such as a spider's eyes). Armour, held items and independent layers are unaffected. Missing names return `None`; invalid indices return `false` without an edit. Edits last for this draw only; `draw.keep()` does nothing here. In the ordered body pass, this hook follows level join order, including this window's player at its own slot, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions. |
| `render_name_tag` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderNameTagEvent` | Name tag visibility and optional replacement text. |
| `render_player_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderPlayerEvent.Post` | After a player is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderPlayerEvent.Post`): once a frame for each player whose `render_player_pre` wasn't canceled, after its `render_living_post`, or right after its `render_living_pre` when only that one was canceled. `player`, `who` and `draw` are as `render_player_pre` has them, and what it draws is kept. `event.model` borrows the same final `crate::FrameModel` as `render_living_post`, read-only for the callback. Its poses and effective visibility include this draw's animation, Skin Customization and Pre edits. It is `None` if no body pose was produced, including when only Living Pre canceled. The player's name tag, leash, and the flames of a burning player and its shadow are each drawn in a later pass of the same frame, as in `render_living_post`. A canceled pre hook doesn't stop the flames or the shadow. In the ordered body pass, this player's pair finishes at its slot in level join order, before the next body submits, including bodies drawn by a general entity renderer. |
| `render_player_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderPlayerEvent.Pre` | Before a player is drawn (NeoForge's `RenderPlayerEvent.Pre`): once a frame for each other player in view and for this window's player in third person (including front view) when it holds the camera, before its `render_living_pre`. `player` is its render state, `who` its profile name and whether it is this window's own player, and `draw` the frame `render_living_pre` gets. Set `canceled` to skip the player's own drawing this frame, `render_living_pre`, `render_living_post` and `render_player_post` included. Canceling only `render_living_pre` skips Living Post but still calls Player Post with `model: None`. Either cancel leaves eligible flames and shadows to the frame; a replacement body should not submit them again. `event.model` borrows the selected player's hierarchy and bind pose as a `crate::FrameModelEdit`. Look up `model.part("head")` and call `model.hide(index)` if it exists. That leaves the head and its children out for this draw after Skin Customization. Living Pre sees the same draw-local edits. Armour, held items and other independent layers, including the cape, are unaffected. In the ordered body pass, the pair wraps this player's living hooks at its slot in level join order, interleaved with general entity renderer submissions, for local and remote players alike. |
| `render_tooltip_gather_components` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderTooltipEvent.GatherComponents` | `RenderTooltipEvent.GatherComponents`: the elements of `stack`'s tooltip (`ItemStack.getTooltipLines` as `TooltipElement::Text` lines, plus any `TooltipElement::Component(TooltipComponent)` supplied by the built-in bundle tooltip or the item's `ItemPart::tooltip_image`), once `item_tooltip` has run and before any wrapping, factory preparation, or measuring. Add, change, reorder or remove elements; each element is then prepared into a `PreparedTooltipComponent` (custom components via factories registered in `register_client_tooltip_component_factories`) before `render_tooltip_pre` runs. Gather wraps logical text in the item's tooltip font when it cannot fit on either side of the cursor. Assign a positive GUI-pixel width to `event.max_width` to further constrain wrapping (`-1` by default). Return `Flow::Cancel` and nothing is drawn; neither `render_tooltip_pre` nor `render_tooltip_texture` is asked. A panicking destructor of custom data published here disables this listener for the session without disabling other listeners. Asked each frame the tooltip shows, never for one the stack hides. Text is used in memory without serialized component limits. Client-side. |
| `render_tooltip_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Pre` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Pre`: a tooltip whose components have been prepared and are about to be measured, positioned and drawn. `stack` is the item the tooltip describes, or `None` for a non-item tooltip. `components` are the read-only prepared components (`PreparedTooltipComponent`, in display order, built-in and custom alike) as `render_tooltip_gather_components` and component preparation left them; measure them with a font borrowed via `draw.with_tooltip_font(font, ...)` or inspect them with `as_text`, `as_bundle`, and `downcast_ref`. `place` holds the anchor point in GUI pixels before `positioner` adds its offset and keeps the box on screen; cursor anchors truncate to whole GUI pixels, and a widget positioner may ignore the anchor. `screen` is `[screen_width, screen_height]` in GUI pixels. `positioner` is the active `ClientTooltipPositioner` that places the tooltip box once measured. `font` is the font identifier used for measurement and drawing (starting at `minecraft:default` or the item font queried again after Gather); change it to measure and draw with a replacement font without rewrapping already-split text lines. A prepared component's `width` and `height` return `None` on failure, distinct from a valid size of zero. A failed component measurement or draw aborts the whole tooltip. `draw` is the tooltip's GUI drawing context; paint from Pre stays visible on cancellation or failure. Return `Flow::Cancel` and the tooltip box and components are not drawn and `render_tooltip_texture` is not asked. Asked each frame a tooltip shows, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `render_tooltip_texture` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Texture` | `RenderTooltipEvent.Texture`: the look of a tooltip box about to be drawn. Set `texture` to a tooltip style (`<namespace>:<name>`) and the box draws as vanilla draws a `tooltip_style`: the GUI sprites `<namespace>:tooltip/<name>_background` and `<namespace>:tooltip/<name>_frame` from the resource packs, each nine-sliced as its `gui.scaling` says or stretched without one. A style whose background or frame no pack has keeps the fills, where vanilla draws the missing texture. Left `None`, the box is Sunrise's solid fills, in `background` and the `border_top` to `border_bottom` gradient, as ARGB. `stack` is the item the tooltip describes, `None` for a text tooltip. Asked each frame a tooltip shows, only while a mod listens. Client-side. |
| `resolve_player_skin` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Select the local player's skin texture and model together, at vanilla's `AbstractClientPlayer.getSkin` moment. Asked once per frame with a local avatar, before the third-person body and skin layers, first-person arms and inventory previews are extracted. Client-side, with a read-only host; it does not change the player's profile, cape, tab-list entry or what other clients see. |
| `resource_pack_status` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player's client answered a resource pack in play (vanilla's `ServerboundResourcePackPacket`): `status` is `Action`'s number, 0 successfully loaded, 1 declined, 2 failed download, 3 accepted, 4 downloaded, 5 invalid URL, 6 failed reload, 7 discarded. Heard when the packet arrives, after the server has handled it; it cannot be stopped. The player's state is swapped in, so the helpers and `PlayerRef::tell` work. The pack a dedicated server offers in the configuration (`resource-pack` in `server.properties`) is answered before the player has joined, and those answers are not heard. Once for each answer, on a server. |
| `right_click_block` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickBlock` | A player right clicks a block (NeoForge's `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickBlock`): once per hand, where `useItemOn` starts, on the clicking client and on the server (`click` says which, with the block, the hit, the hand and its stack, and the player), for a spectator too (the server then only lets one open a menu). Before the block's own use (a chest, a door, a lever) and the item's (`Item.useOn`, `native_use_on`). |
| `right_click_empty` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickEmpty` | The local player right-clicks empty space with an empty `hand`. Client-side only; the server does not receive this event. |
| `right_click_item` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickItem` | `PlayerInteractEvent.RightClickItem`, immediately before `Item.use`. Cancel skips its Part and vanilla behaviour; its result defaults to `Pass`. `Success`, `SuccessServer` and `Consume` end the client's hand loop. `Success` swings on the client, `SuccessServer` on the server, and `Consume` has no swing. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `screen_char_typed_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Post` | A typed character that neither the receiving screen nor its mounted widgets took (`ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.charTyped` returning false), once per printable character that `screen_char_typed_pre` let through: `c` and `modifiers` as there. Not cancellable (matching NeoForge's `ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Post`, which is an unhandled post-dispatch notice). Window characters (including IME commits and text without a physical key) and the control socket's `type` fire it on the receiving screen when the matching Pre did not cancel. A held edit modifier makes a physical key a shortcut instead of text; a controller's keyboard does not synthesize this hook. |
| `screen_char_typed_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Pre` | A character was typed at the open screen (`ScreenEvent.CharacterTyped.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.charTyped`): `screen` is the screen it is, `c` the character, `modifiers` GLFW's bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8), and `field` a copy of the focused text box. Set `canceled` and that one character never reaches the focused child or text box. |
| `screen_closing` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Closing` | The `screen` that was open is being replaced (`ScreenEvent.Closing`), after `screen_opening` let it. |
| `screen_exclusion_areas` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The hook `native_screen_exclusion` asks: a mod adds the rectangles it draws over `screen` (a side panel, a button column) to `areas`, in GUI pixels, `[x, y, width, height]`. NeoForge has no counterpart in the game; recipe viewers ask for it through their own API (`EmiExclusionArea`, JEI's `IGuiContainerHandler.getGuiExtraAreas`). Client-side. |
| `screen_init_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Init.Post` | A screen initialized or rebuilt its children (`ScreenEvent.Init.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.init` / `Screen.rebuildWidgets`). Use `event.widgets` to list and remove children or mount buttons, text fields, and custom widgets. A layout-only resize keeps the existing children and focus and does not fire this hook. A panicking listener publishes no edits. |
| `screen_key_pressed_post` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Post` | A key press that neither the receiving screen nor its mounted widgets took (`ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.keyPressed` returning false): `key`, `repeat` and `modifiers` as in `screen_key_pressed_pre`. If the mod browser's search editor declines, the container still handles raw bindings (including rebound inventory close and sort) before this hook can fire. Slot swap, pick and drop can act before an unhandled result; a translated mapping reached after Post cannot sort the container. Set `canceled` to keep the game's own key handling (bindings, hotbar and toggles) from seeing it. A key that has no vanilla name fires nothing. Fires for window key presses and the control socket's `key ... down`, `key ... repeat`, and the press phase of `key ... tap`, on the receiving screen and only when the matching Pre did not cancel it. A controller does not synthesize this hook. |
| `screen_key_pressed_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Pre` | A key went down over the open screen (`ScreenEvent.KeyPressed.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.keyPressed`): `screen` is the screen it is, `key` vanilla's name for the key (`key.keyboard.e`) — a key with no name fires nothing — `repeat` whether it is the keyboard's auto-repeat of a held press (Sunrise's extra; NeoForge folds repeats into the event), and `modifiers` GLFW's bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8). Set `canceled` and the press goes no further: no focus move, no Escape-close, no inventory or hotbar key, no typing by the key itself. |
| `screen_key_released_post` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyReleased.Post` | A key release that neither the receiving screen nor its mounted widgets took (`ScreenEvent.KeyReleased.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.keyReleased` returning false): `key` and `modifiers` as in `screen_key_released_pre`. Set `canceled` and the release counts as handled: the game's own key handling never sees it, so a held mapping stays down; cancel a release only when you cancelled the press it ends. Fires for window key releases and the control socket's `key ... up` and release phase of `key ... tap`, on the receiving screen and only when the matching Pre did not cancel it. A controller does not synthesize this hook. |
| `screen_key_released_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.KeyReleased.Pre` | Before the currently open screen handles a named key's release. Window releases, control-socket `key ... up`, and the release phase of `key ... tap` reach the current receiving screen, even if it did not see the press. Cancel skips the screen handler and Post. A cancelled window Pre still releases the device key and applies mapped keyboard release cleanup. A cancelled socket Pre skips the ordinary control-key release; a held mapping can stay down. Cancelling an unhandled release in Post instead skips normal mapped release on either route, after the screen has declined. |
| `screen_mouse_button_pressed_post` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Post` | A mouse press after the receiving screen handled or declined it (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Post`). The matching Pre must not have cancelled. `screen`, `button`, `modifiers` and `double_click` are the press Pre values, even if the press closed or replaced that screen. Every listener sees `was_click_handled`, the receiving handler's original answer. A hit on the first child counts as handled even when that child declines and does not take focus. |
| `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Pre` | A press about to go to `Screen.mouseClicked` (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonPressed.Pre`). `screen` is the receiving screen and `screen.mouse` is the pointer position. `button` is the GLFW index (0 left, 1 right, 2 middle, 3 back, 4 forward; beyond those, the platform's own number). `modifiers` are GLFW bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8). The receiver and press Post get the same `double_click`: true for a real press when the last handled real press was of this button on this screen instance less than 250 ms ago. Cancel to skip the screen handler, Post and gameplay mappings; a cancelled real press still counts as handled for double-click detection. |
| `screen_mouse_button_released_post` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Post` | A mouse release after the receiving screen handled or declined it (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Post`). The matching Pre must not have cancelled. `screen`, `button` and `modifiers` come from the release's Pre, not the earlier press or a screen the release opened. Every listener sees `was_release_handled`, the original answer from the receiving handler. Completing quick-craft is not itself a handled release: the child still answers. An early swallowed container release is handled. |
| `screen_mouse_button_released_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Pre` | A mouse button went up over the open screen (`ScreenEvent.MouseButtonReleased.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseReleased`), with the same `screen`, `button` and `modifiers` as `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre` and fired at the same point. Set `canceled` and the release goes no further: a pressed slot keeps what it took and no drag, slider or scrollbar lets go. |
| `screen_mouse_dragged_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseDragged.Post` | A held-button pointer movement that the receiving screen (and its mounted widgets) did not handle (`ScreenEvent.MouseDragged.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseDragged` returning false): `button`, `delta` (GUI pixels, `screen.mouse` the new position) and `modifiers` as in `screen_mouse_dragged_pre`. Not cancellable. Fires on the receiving screen only when unhandled and when the matching Pre did not cancel it, on the window only, for real mouse input and not a pad's or keyboard focus's. |
| `screen_mouse_dragged_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseDragged.Pre` | Before a real held-button movement reaches screen drag handlers. `screen.mouse` is the current GUI position; `delta` is its GUI-space movement. Cancel keeps the new cursor position but skips all screen drag handlers. Same receiving screens as `screen_mouse_button_pressed_pre`, including dialogs, confirmation prompts, books, lecterns and sign editors. |
| `screen_mouse_scrolled_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Post` | A mouse wheel scroll that the receiving screen (and its mounted widgets) did not handle (`ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Post`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseScrolled` returning false): `scroll` and `modifiers` as in `screen_mouse_scrolled_pre`. Not cancellable. Fires on the receiving screen only when unhandled and when the matching Pre did not cancel it, on the window only, for real mouse input and not a pad's or keyboard focus's. |
| `screen_mouse_scrolled_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Pre` | The wheel turned over the open screen (`ScreenEvent.MouseScrolled.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.mouseScrolled`): `screen` is the screen it is, `screen.mouse` where the pointer is (so there is no position of its own), `scroll` the wheel's movement in x and y as the game's own scroll handler scales it (a line times the wheel sensitivity: with Discrete Scrolling on, a notch is ±1, its sign only; a touchpad's pixels are divided by 30), and `modifiers` GLFW's bits (shift 1, control 2, alt 4, super 8). Set `canceled` and nothing the screen would scroll moves: not the bundle under the pointer, nor a list, slider, scrollbar or map. Nothing else reads the wheel while a screen of the game's is open, so no hotbar slot changes either. |
| `screen_opening` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Opening` | `Gui.setScreen` is about to replace the `current` screen with `new` (`ScreenEvent.Opening`; the screen `playing` is none: vanilla's null). Answer `ScreenOpening::Cancel` to keep the current one, `ScreenOpening::ModScreen` to open a mod's own screen instead, or `ScreenOpening::Vanilla` to open another vanilla screen instead. The waiting screen and a warning's live return dialog can replace a receiver with the same `dialog` id. `Cancel` keeps its mounted widgets and focus, without emitting Closing or the replacement's Init.Post. A server's container close that leaves no screen fires no Opening and can't be cancelled; `screen_closing` still gets the screen it closes. One that opens the title or the death screen fires Opening for it. |
| `screen_preedit_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Preedit.Post` | After a screen's preedit handler (`ScreenEvent.Preedit.Post`) and only when no text field took the composition (the handler returned false: vanilla's focused field takes it, so this is a preedit no field wanted) and `screen_preedit_pre` did not cancel. Reports the receiver captured at Pre. A built-in mounted `TextField` can consume preedit; a custom `ScreenWidget` does not. Same arguments as Pre. |
| `screen_preedit_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Preedit.Pre` | Before a screen's preedit handler (`ScreenEvent.Preedit.Pre`, vanilla's `GuiEventListener.preeditUpdated`): the input method is composing `full_text` (empty when the composition ended), with the caret at byte `caret_position`, split into `blocks` of which `focused_block` is the one being converted. Set `canceled` and no text field sees it, and `screen_preedit_post` does not fire. Fires on the window while a screen is open, even when no text field is focused. |
| `screen_render_background` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Background` | The open screen is about to draw its contents (`ScreenEvent.Render.Background`, after vanilla's `renderBackground` and before `render`): draw here for what must sit under the screen's widgets, items and tooltips. Not cancellable. The screen has drawn its background panel; its widgets, slots and items draw over this hook. Screens without a panel, such as the title and chat, fire it before their contents. It does not fire when `screen_render_pre` cancelled. Fires once a frame on the window, for the screens `screen_render_post` covers: a screen of the game's, the title's own screens, Win and Options. Not while playing with no screen, nor for a server dialog, the server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book or a sign editor drawn over gameplay. Draw with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`; `screen.rect` is the screen's container panel (or the whole window). |
| `screen_render_foreground` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Foreground` | The open screen has drawn its contents (`ScreenEvent.Render.Foreground`, vanilla's point before the deferred tooltips and the preedit overlay). Not cancellable. Tooltips, the dragged stack and the preedit overlay draw over this hook; use `screen_render_post` for drawing over the screen's tooltips. It does not fire when `screen_render_pre` cancelled. Fires once a frame on the window, for the screens `screen_render_post` covers: a screen of the game's, the title's own screens, Win and Options. Not while playing with no screen, nor for a server dialog, the server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book or a sign editor drawn over gameplay. Draw with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`; `screen.rect` is the screen's container panel (or the whole window). |
| `screen_render_inventory_mob_effects` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.RenderInventoryMobEffects` | Ahead of the effect panels beside the survival and creative inventories (`ScreenEvent.RenderInventoryMobEffects`, vanilla's `EffectsInInventory.extractRenderState`): `available_space` is the width in GUI pixels right of the container, at least 32 (with no effect to show, or less room, vanilla draws nothing and this does not fire). `compact` picks icons over full panels (vanilla's choice is compact under 120) and `horizontal_offset` shifts the stack right (negative left). Set `canceled` and the panels are not drawn: draw your own with `screen_render_post`. Once a frame while the screen draws them, on the window only; if the effects space changes within a frame (the recipe book opening between two layout builds), it is asked again for the new space. The exclusion areas follow this choice in the same frame. |
| `screen_render_post` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Post` | The open screen has drawn itself and its tooltips (`Screen.render`, the point of `ScreenEvent.Render.Post`): draw over it with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`, `screen.rect` being its container panel (or the whole window) and `screen.id` the screen it is. Once a frame on the window, never while playing with no screen; `&self`, so it reads and draws only. The Win and Options screens call it on their own path, after their drawing. |
| `screen_render_pre` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Pre` | Before the open screen draws itself (`ScreenEvent.Render.Pre`, vanilla's `Screen.renderWithTooltips`): draw to put something under it, or set `canceled` and the screen is not drawn, and neither is its `screen_render_background` or `screen_render_foreground`. The world, the HUD layers and the toasts still draw, and `screen_render_post` still fires. Fires once a frame on the window, for the screens `screen_render_post` covers: a screen of the game's, the title's own screens, Win and Options. Not while playing with no screen, nor for a server dialog, the server pack, link and code-of-conduct prompts, a book or a sign editor drawn over gameplay. Draw with `native_fill`, `native_sprite` and `native_text`; `screen.rect` is the screen's container panel (or the whole window). |
| `screen_room` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Room for a mod's own panel right of the open container screen's window, in GUI pixels: `right` starts at 0; raise it to the width your panel takes beside the window, its gap included, and leave it be otherwise. Later mods see earlier mods' answers: keep the larger (`event.right = event.right.max(mine)`). When the window, the recipe book open beside it and that room all fit across the screen, the window and the book move left by half the room (rounded down), so the three stay centred as vanilla's recipe book moves the window right to make room for itself (`RecipeBookComponent.updateScreenPosition`); when they don't fit, nothing moves. This API is right-only: mod panels reserve the right while the existing recipe book occupies the left; left reservations are not part of this hook. The query's `ScreenView::rect` is pre-reservation. Later draw/input views expose the shifted window; check that rectangle's right edge plus the room against the viewport. Keep this query pure and cheap. Its answer is memoized for the frame and screen; recursive queries during dispatch see zero. Asked once a frame while the player's inventory, a container or a mod's menu screen is open, not for the creative inventory, a merchant's or a beacon's screen, nor while the recipe book covers the window of a narrow screen; an answer that changes geometry builds the layout again in the same frame (slots, book and effect panels move with the window). Not finite or below 0 counts as 0. Beyond NeoForge: a mod there reads the container screen's `leftPos` but has no event to move it, so it draws in the space that's left. |
| `screen_transparent_background` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenEvent.Render.Pre (transparent background)` | `Screen.renderTransparentBackground`: draws the translucent dark background behind an in-game screen (inventory, container, creative, book, sign, editor). Set `canceled` (or return `Flow::Cancel`) to suppress the vanilla dark gradient, allowing a mod to draw its own background or apply menu background blur via `Host::apply_screen_blur`. API @API:screen_transparent_background@. |
| `screenshot` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ScreenshotEvent` | An F2 screenshot has been read back, before either PNG is written (`ScreenshotEvent`). Runs on the client thread with a `ClientHost`. `image` is the in-memory frame (`getImage`): RGBA8, top to bottom, `width * height * 4` bytes. Change it and the normal PNG is that image. A length other than `width * height * 4` writes nothing and reports screenshot failure. The HDR copy is not this image. Change `file_name`, a relative path inside the player's screenshots folder, or set `message` to replace the success chat line. `None` uses the normal translated, clickable line for the final file name. Return `Flow::Cancel` to write no files and post `message`, or the translated cancellation line when `message` is `None`. Invalid paths (including symlinks) report screenshot failure; parent folders are created. HDR copies follow the final name with `-hdr.png`. Agent captures and world icons do not fire this hook. |
| `sculk_bloom` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A sculk catalyst at `pos` has begun blooming after taking experience. The catalyst block state has changed. Server-side. |
| `section_visibility` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | A chunk section in the player's current level is about to mesh: return one `SectionVisibility` or `None`. `input` is the raw section with its one-block border, read before any mod hid a state. `SectionCell::State` gives the canonical `BlockState`. Asked on the client world thread before the section goes to a meshing worker, including remeshes after a neighbouring column arrives or unloads, a resource reload, or `ClientHost::refresh_render_visibility`. A first all-air compile is skipped if no mod contributes section geometry. Other levels drawn through level views do not call this hook. Answers only take away, and every mod's are combined, so no mod brings back what another hid. An answer that breaks a bound is refused whole, logged once, and the section draws without it. With no mod listening, the mesher pays nothing. |
| `server_about_to_start` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerAboutToStartEvent` | The server is about to open its worlds: nothing of the world is loaded yet (NeoForge's `ServerAboutToStartEvent`, "called before the server begins loading anything"). Vanilla's `MinecraftServer.loadLevel` start. Server-side: the single player server thread once Play is pressed, a dedicated server once its settings are read. Each is followed by `server_started` or `world_lifecycle`'s `WorldOpenFailed`. `dedicated` tells a dedicated server from the single player one. |
| `server_chat` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerChatEvent` | NeoForge's `ServerChatEvent`: a player's chat message, through their chain, is about to be broadcast. `player_id` is the server's entity id for the player, `player` their UUID, `username` their name and `raw_text` what they typed. `message` starts as that text; replace it and players see the new message (`withUnsignedContent`: a signed message keeps its signature), and the log shows it. Set `cancel` and nobody sees it, and it doesn't count toward the chat spam limit. Commands (`/say`, `/msg`...) are not chat here. Server-side, with no player's state swapped in. A component deeper than 512 nodes refuses capture or the whole writeback; no event field is applied if any Text field fails conversion. |
| `server_started` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStartedEvent` | The server has opened its worlds and is about to run its tick loop (NeoForge's `ServerStartedEvent`): every level is loaded, no player has joined, and the first tick has not run. Fired where `WorldOpenReady` was, on the single player server thread and on a dedicated server, once per open that succeeds (a failed open fires `WorldOpenFailed` instead). `dedicated` tells a dedicated server from the single player one. |
| `server_starting` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStartingEvent` | Fired once before the server accepts play. On a dedicated server this follows world and data loading, and the adopted packs' English is available. On an integrated server it follows `server_about_to_start` immediately, where text uses the client's active language. A failed startup still reaches `server_stopped`. |
| `server_stopped` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStoppedEvent` | Fired once at the end of server teardown, after `server_stopping` and the final save attempt and storage release. Also fired when startup or teardown fails after startup began, including when a shutdown listener panics, on integrated and dedicated servers. Failed opens save only worlds already adopted by the server (the prepared level is installed; the session lock alone is not adoption). A failed save still reaches this hook, but does not emit `WorldCloseFinal`. |
| `server_stopping` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStoppingEvent` | The server begins teardown (NeoForge's `ServerStoppingEvent`), including after startup or the tick loop failed: the start of vanilla's `MinecraftServer.stopServer`, with the worlds still loaded and the players still connected and not yet saved, so a mod can save its own data or tell them. `world_lifecycle`'s `WorldCloseFinal` follows once the worlds are saved. A server whose loop fails still fires this before the final save attempt and `server_stopped`. |
| `server_tick` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerTickEvent.Pre, ServerTickEvent.Post` | The authoritative server tick's phases: `ServerTickStart` before any level is ticked (NeoForge's `ServerTickEvent.Pre`), `ServerTickEnd` after the last (`.Post`). Read blocks with `native_server_block`, by dimension: which level `self.world` holds here is not defined. A paused pass fires nothing; a frozen world carries `world_running` false except during a requested step. `host.server_has_time()` says whether optional work still fits before the next tick is due (`ServerTickEvent.hasTime`). |
| `server_tick_rate_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `/tick rate` set the server's rate, or a `/tick sprint` ended (`MinecraftServer.onTickRateChanged`, before its autosave wait is adjusted). Vanilla calls this for every `/tick rate`, the rate it already had included, and at each end of a sprint: on its own, by `/tick sprint stop` or by `/tick freeze`, the rate then being what it was. `host.tick_stats()` reads the rate. A client told the server's rate is not a server: nothing fires there. Server-side. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. |
| `setting_changed` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `ModConfigEvent.Reloading (a ModConfigSpec value set on the config screen)` | The player changed setting `key` of mod `mod_id` on its page in Options > Mods (`Registrar::register_setting`); the new value is kept already, so read it with `setting_bool` and its siblings. Only mod `mod_id` hears it: a mod is told of its own settings, never another's. |
| `shader_params` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The values native mods hand their shader packs in this frame, `partial` ticks past the last tick (0 to 1): a pack's WGSL reads them as `frame.mods` (eight `vec4<f32>`). Write the slots your own pack reads; a mod that bundles its pack as `native-<mod id>` is the one that pack is for. Asked once a frame while a pack is on; a value that isn't finite reads as 0 (`native_shader_params_now`). |
| `sign_changed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player finished editing a sign: `ServerGamePacketListenerImpl.updateSignText` has taken their update packet and every check has passed (they were editing that sign, within reach, the sign is not waxed), right before the lines go on the sign. `pos` in `dimension`, the `front` or back side, and the four `lines` as written, plain text with the formatting codes still in (vanilla strips them afterwards). Rewrite `lines` (made four lines again) and that is what the sign gets, or set `cancel` and the sign keeps its old text. With the player's state swapped in, so `PlayerRef::tell` and the helpers that act on the player reach them. Once for each update a player sends on a server. Not told: the host's own editing of a sign on a LAN or single-player world (the editor writes into their world directly), and the sign changes that are no edit of the text (dyeing, glow ink, waxing, `/data`, `/setblock`, mods' `native_with_mod_block_entity`). |
| `sleep_finished_time` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `SleepFinishedTimeEvent` | Enough players have slept through the night in level `dimension` and the clock is about to move to the morning (NeoForge's `SleepFinishedTimeEvent`, vanilla's `ServerLevel.tick` when it moves the clock to `wake_up_from_sleep`). `adjustment` says where the clock goes and may be changed: it comes in as `Marker(minecraft:wake_up_from_sleep)`, vanilla's own, and a mod may set `Absolute`, `Relative` (from `current`, the day time now) or another `Marker`; a later mod reads what an earlier one left. `new_time` is the morning vanilla resolved, read only, for a mod that computes from it. Contract version `2`: in version `1`, `new_time` was the value a mod wrote; a time is now written as `Absolute`. `current` and the result are times of the level's own clock (its dimension type's default clock, else the Overworld's). The game resolves `adjustment` once after every mod: a time before `current`, a time past `i64::MAX`, or a marker the level's clock does not define is refused, as `setTimeAddition` refuses one, and the clock moves to the morning. Only the Overworld's clock defines markers today, so on any other clock every `Marker`, vanilla's included, falls back to the morning. When even the morning is past `i64::MAX`, `new_time` is `current` and the clock stays. On the server, only when the `advance_time` rule is on; `time_skip` then hears the jump to the resolved time and may still cancel it. The players wake and the weather resets as usual either way. Set `canceled` and the clock is left where it is, as NeoForge's cancel leaves it: `time_skip` is not asked either. |
| `sound_engine_load` | client | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `SoundEngineLoadEvent` | The sound engine opened its output (`SoundEngineLoadEvent`): once at window start, after each resource reload (with the `context` of the new resource generation) and when the output device changes. Nothing fires with no sound output (no device, or audio turned off). Client-side only. |
| `spawn_change` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The world's spawn point changed to `pos` in level `dimension`, facing `angle`. |
| `sponge_absorb` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | A sponge at `pos` absorbed water and became wet. Server-side. |
| `spread_players_teleport` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.SpreadPlayersCommand` | A server entity is about to be placed by `/spreadplayers` (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent.SpreadPlayersCommand`, vanilla's `SpreadPlayersCommand.setPlayerPositions`): once per entity, after the spread worked out its spot. `entity` is its network id, `player` its UUID (None for a non-player), `from` where it stands and `target` the spot. A mod may move `target` (a value that is not a number is ignored). Canceling leaves the entity where it is; the spread's other entities, its random draws and its feedback are unchanged. |
| `standalone_models` | client | collect | `&RegistrationContext` | `ModelEvent.RegisterStandalone` | Models no blockstate names, registered once on the client before resources load. Use `StandaloneModelsEvent::register_model` for plain files or `StandaloneModelsEvent::register` to bind a baker and its file dependencies. Every resource load rebakes both from the active pack files. Failed bakes publish no model and log the registering mod. Look up results through `RenderAssets::model` in or after `model_baking_completed`. |
| `submit_custom_geometry` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `SubmitCustomGeometryEvent` | Submit unlit geometry after particle submission, before opaque rendering (NeoForge 26.3 `SubmitCustomGeometryEvent`, vanilla `LevelRenderer.submitFeatures`). Client-only, noncancellable. `view` holds only camera, partial tick and game time, not full extracted level state or visible sections. Submit from extracted state. |
| `substitute_inline_images` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Propose inline images for one nonempty, translated text run in chat history or on either face of a sign, after legacy formatting and before wrapping, measuring and bidi reordering. Existing inline objects are not passed to the hook. The `run` is one resolved style span; ranges cannot cross spans or line breaks. Push UTF-8 byte ranges into `images`, in priority order. The first valid, nonoverlapping proposal wins in hook dispatch order. Missing sprites and invalid ranges are ignored, leaving the original characters available to later proposals. |
| `sweep_attack` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `SweepAttackEvent` | NeoForge's `SweepAttackEvent`: a player's damaging melee hit on a `target` (`minecraft:zombie`, `minecraft:oak_boat`) is about to sweep or not, after `critical_hit`. `vanilla_sweep` is `Player.isSweepAttack` (a charged, grounded, not critical sword hit below the speed limit); `sweeping` starts as it and may force or stop the sweep; `canceled` skips the later mods and keeps `sweeping` as it is (a mod doing its own sweep cancels with `sweeping` false). A critical hit that sweeps has both sounds. Fires on the Game that runs the attack, and on the attacking client, which only asks. |
| `tab_complete` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A guest asked to complete `command`; owned API `Suggestion` records may be added to or filtered from `suggestions`. `range` is the replaced byte range. The game snapshots completions only when a listener subscribes. |
| `tags_updated` | both | listen | `&dyn ReloadContext` | `TagsUpdatedEvent` | Tags changed, and what depends on them should be rebuilt (NeoForge's `TagsUpdatedEvent`); `cause` says how. The place to drop caches derived from the data and to rebind what depends on it. |
| `target_hit` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A projectile hit the target block at `pos`, which now has `strength` output power. Server-side. |
| `teleport_command` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `EntityTeleportEvent.TeleportCommand` | A server entity is about to be teleported by `/tp` or `/teleport` (NeoForge's `EntityTeleportEvent.TeleportCommand`, in `TeleportCommand.performTeleport`, once per teleported entity): `dimension` is the level it arrives in, `entity` its network id, `from` its feet now and `to` where the command sends it. A mod may move `to` (a value that is not a number is ignored). Canceling leaves the entity where it is; the command still reports success. `entity_teleport` and `player_teleported` hear it after. Server-side. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `texture_atlas_stitched` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `TextureAtlasStitchedEvent` | An atlas was stitched and published (`TextureAtlasStitchedEvent`): `atlas` names it. Fires once per atlas, the blocks' first and then the GUI's, during atlas publication before the model manager applies. Model lookups still see the previous generation (none at startup). It precedes `client_setup` on startup and `client_resources_reloaded` on later reloads. Listeners ordered after the model manager apply after both stitches. A refused prepare fires nothing. Look the sprites a mod draws up here, once, and keep what it finds until the next stitch of that atlas: `texture_sprites` for `TextureAtlas::Blocks`, `gui_sprite` for `TextureAtlas::Gui`. Never look them up by name every frame. Client-side. A panic rolls back the callback's journaled writes; the mod is reported and its callback skipped for the session without suppressing other mods' notifications. |
| `thunder_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The server changed its thunder state through `/weather`. Set `cancel` to keep the previous thunder state and timer. Server-side. |
| `tick_target_millis` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | How many milliseconds a client tick lasts (`Minecraft.getTickTargetMillis`, the target the window's `DeltaTracker.Timer` divides the frame's time by): `millis` comes in as vanilla's (50 at 20 ticks a second, longer when the server's `/tick rate` is slower); set it to speed up or slow down the client's fixed-step clock, a "timer". Asked once a frame, in menus too, before the frame's client ticks run (at most ten a frame). An answer that is not a finite number above zero keeps vanilla's. Only the window's clock changes: a server still ticks at its own rate. Client-side. Beyond NeoForge: there is no counterpart. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `time_skip` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The overworld clock is about to jump, from sleep or `/time`. Set `cancel` to keep `from`. |
| `tnt_primed` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | TNT at `pos` is about to be lit (primed): a flint and steel or a fire charge on it, fire burning it, a redstone signal, an arrow, or a dispenser's flint and steel or TNT item. `player` is the server player whose click lit it, with their state swapped in (so `PlayerRef::tell` answers them), None when no player is behind it (fire, redstone, an arrow, a dispenser). Set `cancel` and it is not primed: a TNT block stays where it is (one that fire or a signal had already taken out is put back, without a block update), a dispenser's TNT item stays in it. Not asked: TNT set off by a blast (`explosion_detonate` spares blocks), a TNT minecart and a sulfur cube. Server-side and in single player. |
| `toast_add` | client | cancel | `&dyn ClientHost` | `ToastAddEvent` | A client toast is about to enter its queue (`ToastAddEvent`). Return `Flow::Cancel` to discard only this candidate, before slot allocation, animation or sound. `kind` identifies its family; music is `Other`. Replace `title` and `description` to change the displayed components. For advancements the title is the frame label and the description is the advancement name. Existing recipe/system toasts updated in place do not fire another add event. Requests from `ClientHost::add_toast` enter as `ToastKind::Mod`, including requests that would replace an existing mod toast. Integrated-server error notices are new system toast candidates on the owning client, not on the server or its guests. Pending chunk I/O reports stay with that window when the world closes. Client-lib and title producers prepare candidates for the game to admit with a `ClientHost` before applying. Diagnostics naming a mod's panicking callback are not candidates for this hook and cannot be edited or cancelled. |
| `trade_select` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server player selected trade `index` from `villager` in the trading menu. |
| `trade_with_villager` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `TradeWithVillagerEvent` | A server player's take from a villager or wandering trader, once per trade, after its statistic and `villager_trade` criterion. `merchant` is the trader's entity id, `kind` its type, and `offer` the post-take snapshot: the use is counted and the result is what that iteration took. `host.players().acting_player()` names the player. |
| `use_item_on_block` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `UseItemOnBlockEvent` | `UseItemOnBlockEvent`: after a phase was chosen, before its Part or vanilla behaviour. Cancel skips that behaviour and returns `cancellation_result` (initially `TryWithEmptyHand`). In `ItemBeforeBlock`, `Pass` continues to the block, skipping the item's `on_item_use_first` Part; any result other than `Pass` ends the block interaction. A block-phase `TryWithEmptyHand` can still try the empty-hand behaviour. On a server-registered cauldron click, the client waits for block fallthrough before `ItemAfterBlock`; a consuming server item result doesn't skip this local phase. Other clicks can predict vanilla on a client without the mod; the server corrects it. |
| `validate_tickets` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `LoadingValidationCallback` | `dimension`'s level has loaded the saved chunk tickets of `controller` (NeoForge's `LoadingValidationCallback.validateTickets`), before its first tick and after `level_load`, once for each open and each controller registered with a validator that has tickets in the level. At startup all built-in levels are available before validation begins. `validation` holds that controller's tickets, block owners and entity owners apart; the mod that owns the controller removes the ones that no longer hold (a machine that is gone), and they stop forcing their chunks. A callback's removals are visible to that callback immediately, but are discarded if it panics or incurs a phase refusal. Server-side. |
| `vanilla_game_event` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `VanillaGameEvent` | A vanilla game event is about to reach its listeners (NeoForge's `VanillaGameEvent`, vanilla's `Level.gameEvent`): every event, in `dimension`. `event` is its id (`minecraft:step`), `pos` where it happened and `cause` the network id of the entity that caused it (None for none). `affected_state` is the block state the event touched (`GameEvent.Context.affectedState`, with `cause` its `sourceEntity`): the block a player broke or placed, the block a mob stepped on; None where vanilla passes none (`Context.of(entity)`). Canceling keeps the sculk sensors, wardens and allays from hearing it. A dispenser fluid transfer with no open transaction in its level publishes before overflow insertion or preparation. With a caller-owned transaction in that level, publication waits for the outermost commit. Server-side. |
| `vault_change_state` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A vault at `pos` changed to `state`. Its block state is updated. Server-side. |
| `vault_loot` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A vault at `pos` is about to eject a loot item. |
| `vehicle_create` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A boat or minecart was created at `pos`, after it joined the level. `kind` is the vehicle type. Server-side. |
| `vehicle_damage` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A vehicle is about to take damage from a player's attack. `kind` is `boat` or the minecart type; mods may change `damage` or set `cancel` to leave its damage and wobble unchanged. The attack itself still completes. Server-side. |
| `vehicle_destroy` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A boat or minecart is about to be destroyed at `pos`. TNT minecarts that prime instead of breaking are not heard. Server-side. |
| `vehicle_moved` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A boat or minecart (`kind`, `minecraft:oak_boat`) moved since the last tick (`VehicleMoveEvent`): `from` and `to` are its positions, heard after the tick moved it. Once a tick for each vehicle that carries a server player, whose state is then swapped in (`host.players().acting_player()`, `PlayerRef::tell` and the item helpers reach them; `player` is their UUID), and for one a server player rode and left, until it has stood still for ten seconds (`player` is none, nobody is swapped in). It only hears. Cheap when nothing moves: one position compare a tick for each watched vehicle. Not heard: a vehicle moved before anyone rode it, a mount (horse, pig...) or a vehicle carrying only a mob. Server-side, in the level the vehicle is in. |
| `viewed_level_sound` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | A sound of a level the server opened for viewing (#3716) reached this client, and the client asks where in the player's own level to play it. `level` is the viewed level's handle (what `host.level` gives for it) and `position` the sound's position there; `sound`, `source` and `seed` are the server's. `play_at` starts as None, and a sound left None is not played. Set it to a position in the player's level and the sound plays there, as the player's own level plays a sound packet (no distance delay), with the `volume` and `pitch` the listeners leave and the same seed. A `play_at` that is not finite, or a volume or pitch that is not finite and at least 0, plays nothing. Only a server of `sunrise:levels` revision 3 sends these. A sound bound to an entity of the viewed level has that entity's position there, and plays at `play_at` without following it. Sounds of the player's own level ask `play_level_sound_at_position` and `play_level_sound_at_entity`, never this hook. With no listener the sound is dropped after one check, with nothing decoded or allocated. Client-side. Diverges from upstream: new hook, a Sunrise addition with no NeoForge counterpart. |
| `village_siege` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `VillageSiegeEvent` | A village siege found a spawn position on the server. Set `canceled` to stop the siege. There is one siege per dimension. |
| `visuals` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The world frame's local, world-space rim and colored-light sources. Push requests; Sunrise validates and limits them before rendering. Called only while a world is visible. |
| `weather_change` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The server changed its weather parameters. `raining` and `thundering` are the requested final states. Set `cancel` to keep the previous weather parameters. Server-side. |
| `whitelist_state_update` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The dedicated server's whitelist changed to `enabled`. |
| `wind_burst` | server | collect | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Before a wind burst interacts with blocks, `wind_burst(dimension, center, blocks)` receives the shuffled candidate positions. Remove positions to spare those blocks; entity knockback and the shuffle's random draws are unchanged. Not called when mob_griefing prevents a breeze from interacting with blocks. Bundles need "hooks": @API:wind_burst@. |
| `window_resize` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `WindowResizeEvent` | After a window resize or applying a changed GUI scale, fullscreen, or Unicode font setting. `width` and `height` are physical pixels; `gui_width` and `gui_height` are scaled GUI pixels. Fires only when physical or scaled dimensions change. |
| `world_border_bounds_change_finish` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A timed world-border size change finished on the server, after its final size was applied. Server-side, including the single-player server. |
| `world_border_bounds_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The server world's border bounds changed, either immediately or when its timed size change was requested. |
| `world_border_center_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The server world's border center changed. |
| `world_lifecycle` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `ServerStartingEvent, ServerStartedEvent, ServerStoppingEvent, ServerStoppedEvent` | The world's phases, read-only; `error_text` carries the engine's error text for `WorldOpenFailed` and `WorldCloseFailed`, empty otherwise. Server-side. A server loop or final save failure fires `WorldCloseFailed`. A normal stop with only individually reported record failures still fires `WorldCloseFinal`. |
| `world_saved` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | The server completed a world save cycle. `wait` says chunk writes were flushed before returning. |
| `xp_orb_targeting` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `XpOrbTargetingEvent` | An experience orb looks for a player to follow (NeoForge's `XpOrbTargetingEvent`, `ExperienceOrb.followNearbyPlayer`), on a server. `following` starts as the default: the nearest living player within `scan_distance` (8) blocks who isn't a spectator, or `None`. Set another player, or `None` to follow no one. The orb only goes for a living player who isn't a spectator, in its level, and only moves once they are within 8 blocks. Sunrise looks again on every orb tick (vanilla keeps the player it follows while they stay within 8 blocks), so this is asked every tick for each orb: answer fast. |
| `zoom` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The zoom a native mod asks for, applied while playing (the spyglass's own view wins): set `zoom` when it is none yet. |

## Part hooks

### `BlockEntityPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `block_entity` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | Initial saved data for a declared block entity (`EntityBlock.newBlockEntity`), fired inside the write that creates it (`LevelChunk.setBlockState`), on a server and on a client of one. `state` is the actual placed state for a block declared through `stateful_block_entity`; its properties are available through the Host's state registry. The declaration owns the type id. No answer creates no entity for a state-aware factory; other declared types start with an empty compound. The level is read-only, as `newBlockEntity` gets no level. |
| `block_entity_client_tick` | client | listen | — | — | A client tick of this client's copy of the mod block entity (`ClientLevel.tickBlockEntities`), when its type is yours and its block's state ticks on the client (`block_entity_client_ticks`): once per client tick while the level runs, in vanilla's ticker order among the others, whether or not the copy is drawn. `event.subject` is a `BlockEntityClientScope`: borrow a read-only `BlockEntityReadView` through `with_view` for the resident copy's handle, position, level, block state, data slots, item slots and merged NBT (`block_entity_update_tag`), and borrow `ClientHost` sequentially through `host()` to start, stop and fade sounds or add particles. Never called on a server, and never again for a copy removed or whose chunk unloaded. |
| `block_entity_client_ticks` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Whether block `block`'s block entity ticks on the client (`EntityBlock.getTicker` asked with the client level): set `ticks` when it is yours, and `block_entity_client_tick` runs for it once per client tick. Asked once for each mod block, for all of its states, when the client level initializes its ticker table (before the first column's block entities or the first ticker pass), if a part has a client tick. Set `ticks` to true to make it tick, and never clear a value an earlier mod or mixin set. |
| `block_entity_data_received` | client | listen | — | — | The synchronized `tag` has been merged into this client's block entity. `source` distinguishes chunk data from a later update packet. Empty chunk compounds send no notice; empty update-packet compounds do. Absent tags send no notice. Called once for each accepted tag, only for a matching mod block entity that the client created. `event.subject` is a `BlockEntityClientReadScope`: `with_view` lends a read-only view of the resident block entity with the merged data already applied, and `host()` borrows `&dyn ClientHost`. This notice cannot change the received tag. Missing tags and vanilla block entities call nothing.
