Related NeoForge receive paths `IBlockEntityExtension.onDataPacket` and `handleUpdateTag` perform loading; they are not counterparts of this notice, which runs after the merge. |
| `block_entity_load_additional` | server | listen | — | — | The engine applied saved data to this mod block entity on the logical server (`BlockEntity.loadAdditional`). `tag` is the applied mod payload, and `event.subject` is a `BlockEntityLevelReadScope`: `with_view` lends a read-only `BlockEntityReadView` carrying the instance's handle, `worldPosition`, level, block state and updated data, and `host()` borrows `&dyn LevelHost`, so a listener can store the applied data under its dimension and position, also after a later `/data merge`. The notice cannot change the tag or write to the world. On a holder without declared inventory slots, `tag` includes any saved `Items` field as mod-owned NBT, even rows that look like canonical item stacks. Lists keep their saved order. For a declared inventory, modeled stacks remain separate from the mod payload. |
| `block_entity_on_load` | both | listen | — | `IBlockEntityExtension.onLoad` | The mod block entity has been added to its level, with saved data already applied, before its first tick (`IBlockEntityExtension.onLoad`). Fires once for each instance on both logical sides, including placement. A chunk loaded again creates new instances and calls this hook again. On a server, `block_entity_load_additional` reports the saved-data application first. `event.subject` is a `BlockEntitySideScope`: `Server(scope)` lends a mutable `BlockEntityView` and `&mut dyn ServerHost` sequentially, while `Client(scope)` lends a read-only `BlockEntityReadView` and `&mut dyn ClientHost` sequentially. `block_entity_save_additional` is the separate server-side serialization hook. |
| `block_entity_render_bounding_box` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IBlockEntityRendererExtension.getRenderBoundingBox` | Where the mod block entity at `pos` draws, for culling (NeoForge's `getRenderBoundingBox`): `bounds` is min x, y, z then max x, y, z, in blocks from the block's lower north-west corner. It starts as the block with a block of margin around it; widen it when your `block_entity_submit` draws further (a tall shaft, a beam), or set an infinite value for one never culled. Asked once a frame for each within its view distance (`block_entity_view_distance`) whose chunk is drawn. |
| `block_entity_save_additional` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | Write live mod state into `tag`, a copy of the instance's stored payload (`BlockEntity.saveAdditional`). Edits affect only the output; the entity, its inventory, identity, position, and engine-managed attachments and shapes are not changed. Parts of the entity's type run in dispatch order. For a holder without declared inventory slots, `tag` holds any persisted `Items` as mod-owned NBT. Canonical-looking item rows keep their saved list order; they are not reconstructed in slot order. The listener may replace or remove this field. Declared slots remain engine-owned inventory, serialized by the engine after the listeners run. `event.pos` and `event.level` are the instance's own `worldPosition` and level, read-only and filled by the engine, so a listener can find its live state by dimension and position, including during unload or a removed block's loot roll. Every call has a level: the hook is not asked without one. Malformed payloads, including overlong modified-UTF-8 names and strings, fall back to the stored payload before any output is used. If the region writer cannot encode or admit the complete chunk record, it retries that snapshot with stored block-entity payloads instead of listener edits. |
| `block_entity_submit` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `EntityRenderersEvent.RegisterRenderers` | Draw the mod block entity at `pos`, of block `block` (`mymod:machine`), on this client: once a frame while it is in view (vanilla's `BlockEntityRenderer.submit`: within its view distance, 64 blocks unless `block_entity_view_distance` says otherwise, its chunk faded in, and its bounds, `block_entity_render_bounding_box`, in the frustum), when its `id` is yours. `state` is the block's state (`blockEntity.getBlockState()`: read its `facing` with `native_block_state_property`), `entity` holds what the server sent (`block_entity_update_tag`). Add geometry with `native_draw_quad`, `native_draw_cube`, `native_draw_block` and `native_draw_item` under `draw`'s pose stack, which starts at the block's lower north-west corner, in blocks; `draw` also has the partial tick, the game time and the block's light. When `DrawContext::draw_scene` reuses this renderer, `entity` is supplied by the scene, the draw is full bright, and `pos` is scene-local, not a position to look up in the client level. |
| `block_entity_tick` | server | listen | — | — | A server tick of a resident mod block entity of your registered `kind`. The game skips the callback if the current block does not support that entity type, as in `LevelChunk.BoundTickingBlockEntity.tick`. The first invalid visit logs a warning; a valid tick clears it for the next episode. `event.subject.with_view` lends its live storage. To reach the level or another object, return from the loan and call `event.subject.host()`. Ask for another loan afterwards; it returns `None` if the level, chunk load, generation or block-entity type validity (`BlockEntityType.isValid` for the live block state) no longer matches. In-place block-state updates to another valid state remain borrowable with the live state. For a Part-state edit, first get the handle with `event.subject.with_view(\|view\| view.handle())`, then borrow `event.subject.host()` to call `edit_state`. Keep a handle only to use across calls. |
| `block_entity_ticks` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | Whether block `block`'s block entity ticks (`EntityBlock.getTicker`): set `ticks` when it is yours. Asked for each of the block's states when a world opens. Set `ticks` to true to make it tick, and never clear a value an earlier mod or mixin set. |
| `block_entity_update_tag` | server | replace | — | — | What clients get of the block entity at `pos` when its `id` is yours (`BlockEntity.getUpdateTag`): set `tag` to it, read from `entity` alone. Asked on the server whenever a chunk holding it is sent and whenever its block changes, on the server thread; a client's copy of the block entity (made by `block_entity` there) merges it into its `nbt`. Leave it unset to send no tag of yours. A block change puts the one-shot fields of `BlockEntityView::set_client_update_fields` over it, and sends them alone when it is unset. |
| `block_entity_view_distance` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | How far from the camera the mod block entity of block `block` draws, in blocks (vanilla's `BlockEntityRenderer.getViewDistance`): 64 unless a part answers. Set `distance` for your own block: further for something seen from afar (a beacon-like beam, a large display), nearer for small detail. It is asked once for each mod block, the first time this client draws block entities, and kept: never per frame. A value that isn't finite and above 0 is ignored. Beyond NeoForge: the renderer's own method, no event. |
| `can_place_item` | server | replace | — | — | `Container.canPlaceItem`: whether `slot` of this mod block entity's container admits the offered nonempty `stack`. `Some(true)` or `Some(false)` replaces the declared `ModContainer::insert_rules` answer; `None` keeps it. A subscribed slot refuses insertion if the entity snapshot, block state or offered stack cannot be represented. Face, input-slot and stack limits still apply. |
| `menu_quick_move` | server | cancel | — | — | A player shift-clicked slot `slot` of the menu of the mod block entity (`AbstractContainerMenu.quickMoveStack`, the method a Java menu overrides), on the server, before the game's own routing: move the stack where your block wants it. `subject` lends sequential access to the live resident block entity (`subject.with_view(...)`), the server `Host` (`subject.host()`), and atomic transactions (`subject.transaction(...)`); `with_view` returns `None` if the block entity was removed or replaced during the call. `slot` is the menu's index: below `container_slots` it is a container slot (the block's own, a linked slot, or a crafting result); from there on the player's inventory (27 main slots, then the hotbar). After the live slot allows pickup, `stack` copies what it holds. Take what you move out of `stack` and return `Flow::Cancel`. The same source keeps the remainder (`None` empties it) and the client gets the menu again. A result handler consumes its own inputs; the remainder stays available for the inputs it leaves until the grid changes. Left unhandled, the game undoes the listener's journaled writes before routing the same menu click without a second pickup check. A later Part sees accepted proposals. |
| `menu_slot_take` | server | listen | — | — | A player took a stack out of slot `slot` of the mod menu type `menu` open on the mod block entity (`Slot.onTake`), on the server: a notice after the item left the slot, which refuses nothing (the pickup rule, `menu_slot_rules`' `may_pickup`, refuses). `subject` lends sequential access to the live resident block entity (`subject.with_view(...)`), the server `Host` (`subject.host()`), and atomic transactions (`subject.transaction(...)`); `with_view` returns `None` if the block entity was removed or replaced during the call. `player` is the player's UUID; `slot` is the menu's index, the block's own slots first (linked containers and the player's inventory are never heard). `taken` is a copy of the stack that left the slot, components included, and `left` what the slot holds afterwards (`None` when it was emptied). Vanilla hands `onTake` the taken stack on a click but the stack left in the slot on a quick move, so both are carried: for a quick move `taken` is the amount moved (the slot's stack before, less `left`). |
| `on_chunk_unloaded` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `IBlockEntityExtension.onChunkUnloaded` | The chunk holding this mod block entity is unloading (`IBlockEntityExtension.onChunkUnloaded`): told once for each of its mod block entities, on each side that holds a copy of it, before the copy is dropped. `pos` is where it stands, `block_entity` its handle (the one `host.block_entity` gave while it was loaded) and `entity` its data. Release what your mod keeps for it here. On a server its chunk may have left the level already, so don't count on the handle's reads. As with `chunk_unload`, changes made in it aren't saved: the chunk was saved before it unloads. Not told when the world closes, or on a client when its level goes (a respawn, a dimension change, leaving). |
| `pre_remove_side_effects` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | `BlockEntity.preRemoveSideEffects` of a mod's block entity: an authoritative server is removing `entity` from `pos`, where the block `state` stood, and its updates may have side effects (not under flag 256, `UPDATE_SKIP_BLOCK_ENTITY_SIDEEFFECTS`). Put in `drops` the stacks it held that the game doesn't: they scatter as `Containers.dropItemStack` scatters them, after the block entity's own container. The block entity is still in place, so its attachments read as they were. Answered from inside the level's write: don't change the world here. This also runs when a menu button callback removes the backing block through the Host. Set `keep_contents` to skip the base container slots. Each `drops` entry is a slot: `None` draws the empty slot's scatter position but spawns nothing. Then `spawns` creates each (stack, position) with default pickup delay, and `experience` awards each (amount, position) as experience orbs. Each output list accepts at most 1024 entries; invalid positions or stacks refuse the outputs. Positions must be finite. Default outputs draw nothing. |
| `stateful_block_entity` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | — | Opt a block with a registered block entity type into state-aware creation. Asked once per block when a level's immutable factory ownership table is built. Set true and never clear an earlier answer. For these blocks, `block_entity` receives the actual state and no answer creates no entity. Existing entities survive property changes within the same block. |

### `BlockPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `add_destroy_effects` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `IClientBlockExtensions.addDestroyEffects` | Asked on the client before a non-air state's destruction debris. `state` is the destroyed state, not what the client now sees at `pos`; an invisible state can still be asked. Return `Flow::Cancel` to replace or omit the burst, or `Flow::Continue` for the normal burst (even if the state normally suppresses terrain particles). The break sound is independent. `random` is the client level's random. |
| `add_hit_effects` | client | cancel | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | `IClientBlockExtensions.addHitEffects` | Asked on the client for the non-air, visible block being hit, after its optional hit sound and before any outline sampling. Return `Flow::Cancel` to replace or omit the crumb, or `Flow::Continue` for the normal crumb (even if the state normally suppresses terrain particles). The supplied face is the one being hit. `random` is the level's random, also used by a call to `LevelRef::add_breaking_particles` during this callback. |
| `adjacent_block_path_type` | server | replace | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getAdjacentBlockPathType` | The optional contribution of the neighbour `event.state` at `event.pos` (`IBlockExtension.getAdjacentBlockPathType`). `event.path_type` is that neighbour's own classified type, not the node's type. The readonly host reads the owning level. `Some` immediately becomes the node's type and ends the scan, even if equal to its current type; `None` keeps that neighbour's fluid and vanilla fallback, never suppressing it. Vanilla water contributes through its classified path type: a solid waterlogged block remains `Blocked`. Custom fluids default to `WaterBorder`. Neighbours are visited x, then y, then z, each from -1 to 1, excluding the node's own column. No mob is supplied. These answers run after static classification; per-search evaluator caches remain. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `affect_neighbors_after_removal` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | `Block.affectNeighborsAfterRemoval` for a mod's block: an authoritative server removed the block `state` from `pos` (`moved`: a piston pushed it) and has run its template class's own updates. Push onto `updates` the neighbour updates it wants, in order: `OutputSignal` (`updateNeighbourForOutputSignal`), `Neighbours` (`updateNeighborsAt`) and `NeighboursExcept(direction)` (`updateNeighborsAtExceptFromFacing`); the level runs each at once, as the block's own override would. Answered from inside the level's write: it reads, and cannot write blocks or spawn entities. |
| `analog_output` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | — | `BlockState.getAnalogOutputSignal`: the signal a comparator reads from the block `state` at `pos`, `face` the side that touches the comparator (or the conductor in front of it). `output` holds vanilla's answer: change it when the block is yours (0 to 15; anything else is clamped). Asked only for blocks `has_analog_output` answered for, from inside the level's update: don't change the world here. When the answer changes without the block's state changing, call `level.update_neighbors(host, pos, state, NeighbourUpdate::OutputSignal)`; a change of state through `native_set_block` or `native_set_block_property` (neighbour updates on) tells the comparators itself, as vanilla's `Level.setBlock` does. Authoritative servers only. Version 2 supplies `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost`: use `container_signal` for vanilla's comparator read, which may unpack unopened loot. The level's random stream remains in vanilla order. Other world edits stay forbidden. |
| `animate_tick` | client | listen | `&mut dyn ClientHost` | — | A client's random display tick of the block `state` at `pos` (`Block.animateTick`): `ClientLevel.animateTick` picked `pos` among the 667 random positions within 16 and the 667 within 32 blocks of the player it samples each client tick. Add particles with `host.add_particle` and play sounds with `host.play_local_sound`: a torch's smoke, a furnace's flames, redstone ore's dust. `level` is the client's level, `random` its random (`ClientLevel.random`, the one vanilla lends `animateTick`), so a port keeps Java's odds: `random.next_float() < 0.04` as `random.nextFloat() < 0.04f`. Only parts on the picked state's block are called, inside the pick: after the block's own vanilla effects, before its fluid's and the next pick. A block with no part answering costs the sampler one table lookup. Never called on a server, nor for a screen's scene. A resource pack's Polytone `disable_particles` on the block stops the part's call too, as it stops the block's own. |
| `beacon_color_multiplier` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getBeaconColorMultiplier, IBlockStateExtension.getBeaconColorMultiplier` | The colour the block `state` at `pos` gives the beam of the beacon at `beacon_pos` (`IBlockExtension.getBeaconColorMultiplier`), asked as the beacon builds its beam sections one block at a time. `color` starts at the block's own answer: the beacon's white and a stained glass block's or pane's dye colour, empty for the rest. Set `Some(rgb)` to tint the beam as stained glass does (a colour unlike the section below starts a new section mixed half and half with it), or `None` to let the beam pass untinted, or stop at an opaque block other than bedrock, as vanilla does. The beam takes the red, green and blue channels; alpha is ignored. Asked on both sides, since a client builds the beam it draws from the blocks it has, with the level read-only: register the mod on both and answer the same on each. With no part attached to the state, the game checks the hook subscription and the block key before any event conversion, so the cost is one table lookup per block above a beacon. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `block_jump_factor` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | — | The jump factor of a block state this part is attached to (`Block.getJumpFactor`, which `Entity.getBlockJumpFactor` reads for the player, the cube, the panda, the rabbit and every other living jumper) is being read; vanilla's values are 0.5 for honey and 1.0 for the rest. A part may change `factor` (`event.factor`). The entity reads the block at its feet and, when that gives 1.0, the block below it, so a changed factor takes part in that choice exactly as the block's own would. It runs for each read, on both sides and on the client's replayed ticks, so register the mod on both and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs. It is given the loop `context` of the side that ticks the entity, and takes no `Host`. NeoForge's counterpart `Block.getJumpFactor()` is a method of the block, with no bus event, so this is a part method only; a mod reacting to its own blocks uses this part. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `block_path_type` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.getBlockPathType` | The optional classification of `event.state` at `event.pos` (`IBlockExtension.getBlockPathType`). Asked before every vanilla rule, including air and `is_pathfindable`. `Some` replaces the whole static classification; `None` declines to vanilla. The read-only level host is the level that owns `pos`, so the same state may answer differently at different positions. No mob is supplied: engine callers in NeoForge pass null. The server's 4096-slot positional cache stores the final static classification, including Open, until a matching block-updated notification or slot collision. It survives time, reload and chunk lifetime. Ordinary writes clear only on actual change with UPDATE_CLIENTS and full status at least BLOCK_TICKING; direct block-entity notifications clear even unchanged state. Neighbour and mob-specific adjustments run afterwards, outside that cache. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `block_render_visibility` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Hides a block state from the mesher: set `event.hidden` and the state draws nothing, as if its blocks were air. Its neighbours' faces toward it show, it no longer hides what is behind it, and its fluid layer and block entity are not drawn; light, collision and the server are untouched. The answer depends on the state alone and is resolved at mesh time, never per frame: the game asks once for every state when the client joins a world, and again for every state each time a mod calls `ClientHost::refresh_render_visibility` (a toggle, an ore scanner). Attach the part to vanilla blocks with `block_part_tag`. With no mod answering, the mesher pays nothing. |
| `block_signal` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | The redstone signal the block `state` at `pos` gives on its `face`, the side that touches the block reading it: `BlockState.getDirectSignal` when `direct` (what passes through a block it touches, as a lever's does), else `getSignal`. `signal` holds vanilla's answer: change it when the block is yours (0 to 15; anything else is clamped). Asked very often, from inside the level's update, so answer from your own state: check the block first, and don't read or change the world here. When the answer changes, and once the block is placed, call `level.update_neighbors(host, pos, state, NeighbourUpdate::Neighbours)`. Authoritative servers only. |

| `contents_changed` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server-only, non-cancellable backing-block notice at `Container.setChanged` / `BlockEntity.setChanged`, after the write and before dirty/comparator updates. Default `ITEMS` and item `STORAGE` handlers coalesce implicit notices per backing block at root commit, for vanilla containers and engine-managed mod slots. During `menu_quick_move`, implicit notices publish only after a committed move settles its source slot (including when the source is emptied), and never after an unhandled or refused move. If the original backing container was removed or replaced, neither its replacement nor watchers receive the deferred notice. Explicit `set_changed` notifies immediately, even for equal contents and inside a transaction; the notice survives abort. Direct `set_item_slot`, writes to storage a mod provides itself, and rollback restores synthesize no notice. Nested writes to the backing block whose notice is active still apply, with dirty/comparator updates, but do not re-enter or queue a second notice for that block. Another block notifies normally. Double containers notify each backing component, not the other half merely refreshed for comparators. Vehicle/menu placeholder coordinates are excluded. The source level, pos and state always name the changed backing block. `observer` is `None` for its own Parts, or `Some` for Parts of a block entity watching that source through `watch_contents`. Source Parts run first, then a snapshot of observers in registration order; a stale observer is skipped. A self-watch never duplicates the source callback. Registration changes affect the next notice. Replaces the Create port positional `contents_changed` mixin for depot, package and schematic storage; there is no matching NeoForge bus event. Each Part runs in the existing storage journal. A panic discards that Part's tentative writes (including successful nested transactions), not the backing change that raised the notice or another Part's writes. |
| `dispense_method` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `DispenserBlock.getDispenseMethod`, before the item's `dispense_item`: the block the dispenser faces chooses what the dispense does, for any item, including vanilla items, on the authoritative server (not droppers). `state` is the block at `pos` moved by `facing` (air included), and the part is asked only for that block. An answer wins: no item part is asked and vanilla's behaviour doesn't run. The answer is `dispense_item`'s: a remainder of None empties the slot, `Some(stack)` replaces it (the hook owns consumption: no extra item is removed afterwards), and `event` is the level event played with the smoke: 1000 the click, 1001 the fail sound, or 0 for no sound or smoke. Leave answer None and `dispense_item` decides. In an open transaction in the dispenser's level, sound and smoke follow the behaviour's published effects at the outermost commit and are discarded on abort. The remainder is detached until the dispenser writes its slot. An invalid remainder is reported without writing or notifying the slot, and no other behaviour runs. A disabled owner's subscribed method skips the slot write and dispense events unless an earlier enabled Part supplies a reply the engine accepts. |

The queued entity writes apply to the swept entity inside its own tick, in `StepBasedCollector` order, and stop when it dies. `EntityWrite::Hurt { damage_type, amount }` deals the registered type in the level without a causing or direct entity. Armor stands, projectiles and vehicles ignore contact hurt; experience orbs accept hurt only from `step_on`. |

| `fall_on` | server | collect | `&mut dyn LevelRandomHost` | `LivingFallEvent` | An entity landed on a block state this part is attached to (`Block.fallOn`), after falling `event.distance` blocks. Writable `event.distance` and `event.damage_multiplier` feed the fall calculation before hurt and sounds. The multiplier starts at the block's vanilla value (normally 1.0). Damage is `floor((distance + 1e-6 - safe_fall_distance) * damage_multiplier * fall_damage_multiplier_attribute)`, with safe fall distance 3 by default. Halving distance is not halving damage. Zero multiplier or a distance within the safe fall prevents damage and generic fall sounds. An entity that cannot be hurt by the fall (creative, fall immune) is told with zero multiplier and keeps its immunity, and a cancelled fall still lands. It runs inside the entity's move, on the level random host: it reads blocks and draws the level's random in vanilla's order, and cannot write. SetBlock takes an API BlockState. The complete requested batch is checked before queuing: an invalid key or ordinal refuses all writes and sibling outputs from that callback, including distance and multiplier. The original landed-state guard is checked once per batch, not between its ordered writes. Players, mobs and dropped items land here (an item as an `EntityRef` of kind `minecraft:item`, with zero multiplier: items take none); projectiles and the other entities do not. |

| `fire_odds` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | `IBlockExtension.getFlammability, IBlockExtension.getFireSpreadSpeed, IBlockExtension.isFlammable` | How fire treats the block `state` at `pos` on either side (`FireBlock.setFlammable(block, igniteOdds, burnOdds)`). | `is_fire_source` | server | replace | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.isFireSource` | On an authoritative server, whether the block `state` at `pos`, under a fire, keeps that fire burning (`FireBlock.tick`'s check of the dimension's `infiniburn` tag), asked on every tick of the fire above it: `Some(true)` makes the fire endless, as netherrack does in the overworld (rain doesn't put it out and it never burns out), and `Some(false)` refuses a block the tag lists. | `is_ladder` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IBlockExtension.isLadder` | Whether a living entity climbs the block state this part is attached to (`IBlockExtension.isLadder`, which `LivingEntity.onClimbable` asks of the block the entity stands in). `entity` is the climber's id and `pos` the block it stands in; `state` is that block's state. `ladder` starts with vanilla's answer, whether the block is in `#minecraft:climbable`, and a part may set either value: `true` makes the block climbable as a ladder is, `false` refuses it though the tag lists it. | `is_sticky_block` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.isStickyBlock` | Whether the block state this part is attached to is sticky to a piston (`IBlockExtension.isStickyBlock`): set `sticky` to decide. `sticky` starts with vanilla's answer, true for `minecraft:slime_block` and `minecraft:honey_block`. | `piston_carries_block_entity` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | Whether a piston carries your block `state` at `pos` with its block entity: asked at the last step of `PistonBaseBlock.isPushable`, after the block's push reaction, for a block that is otherwise pushable, whenever a piston's structure takes the block in. Asked on both sides, as a client replays the move on its own copy: answer the same on both, from the arguments, without touching the world. Only attached block parts are asked; blocks without a subscribed part pay only the dispatch guards. A block that only needs to move or not, or to be popped or pushed only, is the push reaction's question, asked before this one. The order is reaction first, then this shared block-entity rule for push-pull, ignore-entity and push only; a wrong-direction push-only answer refuses before this hook. Popped destroys and drops without asking it. This hook is asked only for an otherwise-pushable block. A live owned payload is required: data-only or unmaterialized vanilla entities remain refused. | `set_placed_by` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | Runs on the server once for the placed block, after vanilla's `Block.setPlacedBy` work and before `ItemPart::on_block_placed_by` for a block-item placement. `state` is the placed block's state, read before the game-level steps that can remove it (such as a wither pattern). `stack` is the original, read-only stack before consumption, or `None` if no stack was supplied. `placer` is `None` for a dispenser or for code placement. `LevelRef::set_placed_by` also reaches this part, without firing the item notice. Writes through the `ServerHost` participate in the current transaction, if one is open. |
| `should_check_weak_power` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.shouldCheckWeakPower` | `IBlockExtension.shouldCheckWeakPower`: set `check` to decide whether indirect power is read through this block. `check` starts with vanilla's conductor answer. `direction` is the input side of the block being powered, opposite the queried block's output side. Authoritative servers only. |
| `should_display_fluid_overlay` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IBlockExtension.shouldDisplayFluidOverlay` | Whether a fluid's side beside a block state uses the fluid's overlay sprite (water's `water_overlay`) instead of its flowing sprite (`IBlockExtension.shouldDisplayFluidOverlay`): set `event.overlay`. `overlay` starts with vanilla's test, true beside a `HalfTransparentBlock` or `LeavesBlock` (for a content block, its template's class), so a part can turn the overlay on or off. `event.fluid` is the fluid as it is asked: a source at amount 8, or a flowing fluid at amount 7, not falling. A fluid with no overlay sprite, such as lava, draws its flowing sprite whatever the answer. The answer depends on the state and the fluid alone and is resolved at join or explicit visibility refresh, never on a meshing worker: the client asks once for every state and fluid where it resolves `block_render_visibility` (joining a world, `ClientHost::refresh_render_visibility`), and the meshing workers read the table. The level and position are dropped: the meshing workers run no mod code. With no part answering, nothing is asked and meshes are unchanged. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `should_hide_adjacent_fluid_face` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IBlockExtension.shouldHideAdjacentFluidFace` | Whether a block state hides the face a fluid beside it draws toward it (`IBlockExtension.shouldHideAdjacentFluidFace`): set `event.hidden` and the fluid's face toward the block is not drawn. `event.direction` points from the fluid to the block (Sunrise's reading, not checked), and `event.fluid` is the fluid as it is asked: a source at amount 8, or a flowing fluid at amount 7, not falling. `hidden` starts false. Vanilla's tests still apply first: a fluid beside the same fluid, or behind a face that occludes it, draws no face there whatever the answer. The answer depends on the state and the fluid alone and is resolved at join or explicit visibility refresh, never on a meshing worker: the client asks once for every state and fluid where it resolves `block_render_visibility` (joining a world, `ClientHost::refresh_render_visibility`), and the meshing workers read the table. The level and position are not arguments. With no part answering, nothing is asked and meshes are unchanged. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `should_redstone_wire_connect_to` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | `BlockBehaviour.shouldRedstoneWireConnectTo`: set `connected` to say whether redstone wire connects to the block `state` at `pos`. `direction` points from the wire toward the neighbouring block, or is None when the wire asks about the block above or below. `connected` holds vanilla's answer: change it when the block is yours. Asked whenever wire settles its shape: answer from `state` and `pos` without touching the world. When the answer changes, and once the wire is placed, update its neighbours (`level.update_neighbors`). Authoritative servers only. |
| `smoke_source` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `CampfireBlock.isSmokeSource` on both sides for the block `state` under a campfire, as the campfire is placed or the block under it changes: `Some(true)` for your own blocks makes the campfire a signal fire whose smoke rises higher, as over a hay bale. The level is read-only. The client asks during placement prediction. `None` keeps vanilla's hay-bale check; `Some(false)` overrides it. |
| `sound_type` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IBlockExtension.getSoundType` | The sound type of a block state this part is attached to (`IBlockExtension.getSoundType`) is being read, to play one of its sounds. `pos` is the block and `state` its state; `entity` is nullable, as NeoForge's: the id and type (`minecraft:player`, `minecraft:creeper`) of the entity stepping on it, landing on it, placing it or digging it (a sniffer), and `None` for a break and for a placement by no entity (a dispenser); `cause` says which sound is about to play (`BlockSoundCause::Step`, `Fall`, `Place`, `Break` or `Dig`). It is asked only where NeoForge asks it, not for the hit sound while a block is mined (that has its own client hook upstream) nor for sculk growth. A part may change `sound` (`event.sound`), which starts as the state's own sound type: its event names, volume and pitch. The game then applies vanilla's factors for `cause` to the volume and pitch and plays that one sound. An answer whose volume or pitch is not finite and at least 0, or one of whose events is not a `namespace:path` id, is dropped whole and the state's own sound type is used. An event the resources lack plays nothing, as in vanilla; a mod's own event (from its content mod's `assets/<id>/sounds.json`) is sent to players by name. It runs for each sound, on the side that plays it, so register the mod on both and keep the listener a pure function of its inputs: the placer's client and the server ask for the same placement and must agree. It is given the loop `context` of the side that asks, and takes no `Host`. It cannot play no sound, several sounds, or a sound somewhere else (`playStepSound` and `playFallSound` can). Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `state_at_viewpoint` | client | collect | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IBlockExtension.getStateAtViewpoint, IBlockStateExtension.getStateAtViewpoint` | The block state the camera at `viewpoint` sees of the block `state` at `pos` (`IBlockExtension.getStateAtViewpoint`), so a partial fluid block, a tank or a waterlogged mod block can show underwater fog only while the eye is really inside the liquid. `seen` starts at `state`: set it to the state whose fluid and powder snow the camera should read. Asked as the client finds the fluid at the camera (`Camera.getFluidInCamera`) for the block at the eye, the block above it and the blocks at the near plane's centre and corners: the fog, the field of view in a fluid, the water vision, the sky's dark disc and conduit power's underwater vision read it. The first-person water overlay uses the player's tick-sampled eye fluid instead; change its eligibility with `camera_submersion`. The client host is read-only; answer from the event alone, as the game asks every frame. With no part on any block the subscription check skips the asks; otherwise each block read costs one table lookup. Diverges from upstream: new hook. |
| `state_for_placement` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | Choose a block's placement candidate (`Block.getStateForPlacement(BlockPlaceContext)`), with `state` the block's own default state. This is not a placement notice: code holding a `ServerHost` can call `LevelRef::state_for_placement` to ask for a candidate without placing anything. The Part still has only its `LevelHost` through `LevelSide`, not the caller's `ServerHost`. Answer only for your blocks: put the state you want in `answer` as `Some(Some(state))`, including a registered state of another block. If this is an actual placement, survival and entity obstruction use that candidate at the placement position, but the selected block's `getStateForPlacement` is not called. A mod selecting a multi-cell block is responsible for checking its footprint: `setPlacedBy` does not recheck its companion cell. Sunrise also requires creative-operator permission to select a game-master block. An unknown state falls back to ordinary placement. Refuse with `Some(None)`: the block goes nowhere and the stack is kept, as vanilla's `null`. Left empty, the block's own placement runs. `context` is vanilla's `BlockPlaceContext` (the clicked face, the clicked position, `source` distinguishing `PlacementSource::Player { yaw, pitch, sneaking }` from a non-player `PlacementSource::Directional { direction }`, whether the clicked block is being replaced, and the item key in hand, not an item stack or its components). For a directional source, whether the clicked block is replaced is the game's answer from the clicked block, as `DirectionalPlaceContext.replacingClickedOnBlock()` is, and a block whose own rule reads the placer's vertical look (pointed dripstone) has no vanilla answer: left empty, that placement is refused (`PlacementStateError::NoVerticalLook` for `LevelRef::state_for_placement`), and answering replaces that rule. `fluid` is the fluid in the position the block would go in, which a waterlogged block reads. Build the state from `state` with `native_state_with_property`, and know whether `state` is yours with `native_state_block`. It is asked for every placer that goes through vanilla's placement (a player's `BlockItem.place`, a dispenser's `DirectionalPlaceContext`). The item's `must_survive` controls the ordinary final survival check. Standing/wall candidate survival and entity obstruction are independent. A trait mod returns `Some(Some(state))` to choose a state, `Some(None)` to refuse, or `None` to leave the answer to the next mod or vanilla. Changes to the event's fluid copy are mod-provided data forwarded to later Parts, never consumed by the engine. Asked on both sides, as vanilla asks it: a client asks it from its placement prediction, with its loaded copy of the level (match `LevelSide`). Reads a client can't answer, tag tests among them, are `None` there, and `BlockEntityPartState::state_in` gives its default: never answer from those as if they were the server's. A client refusal keeps its stack unspent until the server answers. Answer `None` on a client where only a server can decide: the template's rule then predicts, and the server still decides. |
| `step_on` | both | collect | `LevelRandomSide` | — | `Block.stepOn` in `Entity.applyEffectsFromBlocks`, before inside-block effects. It runs on the block at `getOnPosLegacy` for a grounded body affected by blocks, including an item whose idle tick did not move. `state` and `pos` name that same contact; the position need not be the actual supporting cell. Each applicable effects pass gets its own callback. |
| `tick_block` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn LevelWriteHost` | — | An authoritative server's `ServerLevel.tickBlock` is about to run a due scheduled tick of the block `state` at `pos` (`BlockState.tick`): the block is still the one the tick was scheduled for. Change the block with `native_set_block_property` and set `handled` to skip the block's own tick; a block changed without `handled` takes its own tick as it is now, if it is still the tick's block. If the block's owner subscribes and is disabled, the occurrence is held before dispatch, including for any enabled Parts attached to the block. Once re-enabled, the held occurrence is dispatched once with its original deadline, priority and order. |
| `tool_modified_state` | both | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `IBlockExtension.getToolModifiedState` | The state the block `state` becomes under a tool action (`IBlockExtension.getToolModifiedState`: an axe's strip, scrape or wax-off, a shovel's flatten or douse, a hoe's till), asked of the block a tool is used on (`tool.pos`). `final_state` starts with vanilla's answer, `None` for a block the tool does nothing to: set it to `Some(new)` to allow the action or replace its result (a mod log an axe strips, a path a shovel makes), and `Cancel` it to deny the action, so the tool does nothing. `tool.simulated` says the game is only asking what the tool would do (`LevelRef::block_transform`, NeoForge's `simulate`): answer the same and change nothing in the world. A part answers before the global `block_tool_modification`, which still hears the result and may change or cancel it; a cancelled part skips it. Asked for the block's own parts only, so a block with none costs nothing. A disabled owner's subscribed transform suppresses the unchanged template result, not the ability's notification or a listener's replacement. The global hook still sees the candidate, and later abilities can be tried if this one has no result. |
| `update_shape` | server | replace | `&mut dyn LevelTicksHost` | — | An authoritative server is about to compute the state the block `state` at `pos` takes now that its neighbour in `direction` is `neighbour` (`BlockBehaviour.updateShape`). `state` may not be in the world yet (a piston landing it): decide from `state`, not from the block at `pos`. Answer only for your own blocks: put the new state in `new` (`state` keeps the block as it is; air breaks it, with its drops, on a neighbour update) and the block's own `updateShape` doesn't run, nor the ticks it would schedule. The level is read-only, as vanilla's `LevelReader`; ticks go through `native_schedule_block_tick` and `native_schedule_fluid_tick` with `ticks` (`ScheduledTickAccess`). It runs often, at least six times per `setBlock` that updates shapes (more for redstone wire's diagonals and knock-on updates): keep it short. A disabled owner's subscribed update keeps the input state instead of running the template. |
| `use_item_on` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `BlockBehaviour.useItemOn`: the block's own behaviour, with the held item, on the server for each player. An answer replaces the template's behaviour; `TryWithEmptyHand` tries `useWithoutItem` on the main hand, and `Pass` goes on to the item's `useOn`. A disabled owner's subscribed interaction passes without running the block template. |
| `use_without_item` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `BlockBehaviour.useWithoutItem`: the captured state's empty-hand method, after useItemOn asks for it, on the server and main hand only. A disabled owner's subscribed interaction passes without running the block template. |
| `weak_changes` | server | collect | `&dyn LevelHost` | `IBlockExtension.getWeakChanges` | Whether this block receives block entity changes through an adjacent conductor. Starts false. |

### `EntityPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `can_rider_interact` | client | collect | — | `IEntityExtension.canRiderInteract` | Whether this candidate counts normally in the client's ordinary entity pick despite sharing the camera's root vehicle. Mirrors NeoForge's `IEntityExtension.canRiderInteract`, false by default. Only parts attached to the candidate's type are asked, in dependency order; `allowed` starts false and the last write wins. `event.subject` is an `EntityClientScope`: `with_view` lends a read-only `EntityClientView` of the candidate (handle, kind, position, rotation, UUID), and `host()` borrows `&mut dyn ClientHost` sequentially. Asked only at the same-vehicle branch of `ProjectileUtil.getEntityHitResult`, after a non-inside ray intersection qualifies against the running nearest distance. True lets this candidate update that distance normally. Inside-box selection does not ask it. Both the tick and frame pick use this rule. Attack-range component rays have no same-vehicle restriction and do not ask it. No server reach or interaction checks change. |
| `general_entity_tick` | server | listen | — | — | The authoritative tick of one general entity row of type `kind` (`mymod:robot`) in the server level's entity phase: `ServerLevel.tickNonPassenger`'s `entity.tick()` for a row the type registry owns (#792 stage 4). The row's common bookkeeping has run (its previous position is its current one); a rider is ticked after its vehicle, depth-first as `tickPassenger` recurses. A passenger dismounted before its join-order slot can tick as a root if it has not already ticked this pass. Rows that joined this tick first tick next pass; parked levels and rows outside entity-ticking chunks do not tick. Canceling `entity_tick_pre` skips the row's own tick; cancellation alone does not skip its passengers' ticks. A listener can separately change the riding graph. `event.subject` is an `EntityServerScope`: `with_view` lends a live `EntitySubjectView` of the row (handle, kind, position, velocity, rotation, UUID and checked velocity, rotation and fall-distance writes), and `host()` borrows `&mut dyn ServerHost` sequentially for attachments (`view.handle()` is their holder) and other world operations. Told only for general entity types: vanilla families tick their own paths (#6509 owns bringing them to one join-order walk), so a part attached to `minecraft:zombie` hears no tick from this hook. Server-side. |

### `FluidPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fluid_rules` | server | listen | `&dyn LevelHost` | — | How a mod's world fluid flows at `pos`, starting from what it declared (NeoForge's `BaseFlowingFluid` and `FluidType` getters: `getTickDelay`, `getDropOff`, `getSlopeFindDistance`, `canConvertToSource`). `fluid` is the id it was declared with. Asked for every flow step and every cell a spread looks at: answer fast. An answer outside what `WorldFluid` accepts is brought back within it. Server-side. |

### `ItemPart`

| Hook | Side | Contract | Host / context | NeoForge | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `apply_enchantments` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.applyEnchantments` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.applyEnchantments`: the enchanting table applies `enchantments`, the list it chose for the clicked row as (id, level), to `stack`, the item in its input slot (`EnchantmentMenu.clickMenuButton`). `result` arrives with vanilla's result: a book becomes an enchanted book with the same count and components, then each level is added as `ItemStack.enchant` adds it, keeping the higher one. Replace or change it, and the input slot holds it; `player_enchant_item`, the `enchanted_item` trigger and `enchant_item` then see it. The levels and lapis paid stay as without the part. The result goes through the stack boundary: an unknown item, invalid damage or an oversized count is refused, and so is a count other than the input's, since the table holds one input; a refused result keeps vanilla's. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on the server, with a read-only `Host`. Loot, trades and spawn gear (`EnchantmentHelper.enchantItem`) don't ask. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `can_equip` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.canEquip` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canEquip`: may `stack` go into the equipment slot `slot` of the living entity `entity`, of type `kind` (`minecraft:player`)? `slot` is an `EquipmentSlot` name: `head`, `chest`, `legs`, `feet`, `mainhand` or `offhand`, and `body` or `saddle` from a dispenser. `can_equip` arrives holding the data answer, as `LivingEntity.isEquippableInSlot` and `Equippable.canBeEquippedBy` read the stack's own `equippable` component (a stack's override replaces the item's): yes when it names that slot and its `allowed_entities` is absent or lists the entity type (an empty list allows none); with no component, removed or never there, yes for `mainhand` only. Set it either way. Asked where vanilla asks: the armour slots of a player's inventory menu (`ArmorSlot.mayPlace`, which a shift-click into one reaches too), a dispenser equipping what stands in front of it (`EquipmentDispenseItemBehavior`, one entity at a time) and a mob picking an item up off the ground (`Mob.equipItemIfPossible`). It decides whether the stack fits the slot, not what the slot holds, whether the entity may wear one at all (`canUseSlot`) or the dispenser's own rules for the entity. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on both sides, as the client's menu predicts the slot. `host` is read-only and reads live entity and world state directly, for example `entity.entity().position(host)`. A missing or despawned target refuses wherever a part is asked; the no-listener data path performs no conversion or entity lookup. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `can_fit_inside_container_items` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IItemExtension.canFitInsideContainerItems` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canFitInsideContainerItems`: may `stack` go inside an item that holds items? `fits` arrives with vanilla's answer (`Item.canFitInsideContainerItems`): every stack fits except a shulker box. Set it `true` or `false`. It is asked, on both sides, wherever that rule is: - a shulker box's slots (`ShulkerBoxSlot.mayPlace`), in its menu and a guest's; - a bundle (`BundleContents.canItemBeInBundle`): a click that puts the stack in a bundle, and the `/item` bundle contents; - the item slots a mod gives an item (`item_container`) and its bundle slots. |
| `can_grindstone_repair` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IItemExtension.canGrindstoneRepair` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canGrindstoneRepair`: does the grindstone repair `stack` as it repairs a damageable item? `can_repair` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is damageable (`ItemStack.isDamageableItem`); set it `true` or `false`. It is asked at two places, on both sides (the server's menus and the client's own result): - `GrindstoneMenu.mergeItems`, for the top stack when both inputs are single stacks of the same item. `true` takes the repair branch: one item, the enchantments of both merged, then all but the curses removed. Durability is merged only when the result has durability, as vanilla checks it again on the copy. `false` takes the other branch: two matching stacks of an item that stacks give two, and anything else gives nothing. - The input slots' `mayPlace` (damageable or enchanted): a granted stack can be placed. This site is Sunrise's reading. |
| `can_perform_action` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.canPerformAction` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.canPerformAction`: can `stack` perform the item ability `ability`, a plain, case-sensitive name such as `shield_block`, `shears_harvest` or `shears_carve` (a mod's own abilities also use plain names, such as `my_mod_chop`)? `can_perform` arrives with vanilla's answer for that ability: the item kind's (shears shear) or the stack's component (`blocks_attacks`, a shield's, blocks); set it either way and the stack grants or loses the ability. Loot conditions use the shared item defaults; action sites retain their own vanilla answers until #3330. Asked where vanilla checks the item for an action: a raised item blocking attacks (`shield_block`: a granted stack blocks as a bare `blocks_attacks` component does, a denied one never blocks nor is raised); shearing a sheep, mooshroom, snow golem or bogged, a dispenser's shears and a bee nest's honeycombs (`shears_harvest`); carving a pumpkin (`shears_carve`). It decides whether the stack may do the action, not what the action does to the world or the tool (`block_tool_modification` is the block's side of a tool action; item wear is its own). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on both sides, so the client's prediction agrees with the server: answer from the stack and `ability` alone, and fast. An item with no such part costs one table lookup. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `contained_fluid` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The fluid a stack of a mod's own container item holds, as its `neoforge:fluid_container` item model draws it (`FluidUtil.getFirstStackContained`): `fluid` arrives `None` (holds none); set it to the fluid's key, taken once at registration with `r.fluid_key`. Asked on the client each time a stack of an item outside `minecraft:` is drawn through a fluid container model; vanilla's items keep their own answer and never ask. A fluid with no look in an item draws the missing texture; a key no fluid is registered under draws the stack as holding none, and the game logs it once per item. Answer from the stack alone, and fast. |
| `crafting_remainder` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.getCraftingRemainder(ItemStack)` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.getCraftingRemainder(ItemStack)`: what `stack` leaves when consumed by crafting, furnace fuel or brewing. `remainder` arrives holding the item's data remainder. Replace it, or set it to `None` to leave nothing. A part that leaves it alone preserves the data remainder. Recipe-specific book and banner copies are handled by the recipe. Asked for each consumption on the authoritative server, with a read-only `Host`. Oversized counts, unknown items and invalid damage are refused by the stack boundary, retaining the original data remainder. API stacks cannot have count zero; use `None` to leave nothing. Native panics follow the normal Part crash path, attributed to the owning mod and Part. |
| `creator_mod_id` | both | collect | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.getCreatorModId` | `IItemExtension.getCreatorModId`: the mod that made `stack`, which a recipe viewer or tooltip names, instead of the mod that registered its item. `mod_id` arrives as the item id's namespace; set it to another mod (a universal bucket holding that mod's fluid, a facade of its block) or `None` for no mod. Asked by `ItemResource::creator_mod_id`, from client tooltips and viewers as well as the server: answer from the stack alone. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked: an item with none costs one table lookup. |
| `damage_item` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IItemExtension.damageItem` | `IItemExtension.damageItem`: the wear `stack` is about to take (`ItemStack.hurtAndBreak`: a tool that mines or hits, armour that takes a hit, a shield, a rod, an elytra, a tool a dispenser uses, a mob's armour), after Unbreaking has had its say, as NeoForge passes it. `entity` is who wears or uses it: the acting player on a player's paths, the mob, armour stand or dragon wearing it, `None` for a dispenser's tool. `amount` is the points about to be lost; change it (0 or less leaves the item whole) or set `canceled` for no wear at all. Set `break_now` and the item breaks whatever durability it has left, through the vanilla break (the sound and particles, the stack emptied, `player_destroy_item` for a player): NeoForge's `onBroken`. It wins over `canceled`. Not asked when Unbreaking already saved the item, nor for a creative player's tools (they take no wear). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item hear it: an item with none costs one table lookup, and a game with no such part none at all. On the paths of a player the player's state is swapped in, so `host.players().acting_player()` answers them. Once for each wear, on a server. |
| `dispense_item` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | `DispenserBlock.getDispenseMethod`: choose a behaviour for any item, including vanilla items, on the authoritative server (not droppers). The block the dispenser faces is asked first (`dispense_method`), and its answer keeps this part from being asked. Leave answer None for vanilla. Otherwise answer `(remainder, event)`: a remainder of None empties the slot, `Some(stack)` replaces it (the hook owns consumption: no extra item is removed afterwards). `event` is the level event the dispenser plays with its smoke: 1000 the click, 1001 the fail sound (`OptionalDispenseItemBehavior` without success), or 0 for no sound or smoke. In an open transaction in the dispenser's level, sound and smoke follow the behaviour's published effects at the outermost commit and are discarded on abort. The remainder is detached until the dispenser writes its slot. An invalid remainder is reported without writing or notifying the slot, and no other behaviour runs. To drop one normally, answer with `host.dispense_default`'s remainder and 1000. |
| `empty_contents` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IDispensibleContainerItemExtension.emptyContents` | `IDispensibleContainerItemExtension.emptyContents`: `stack` (a bucket of a mod fluid, a jar, a canister) is about to be emptied at `pos`, by `player` (the acting player) who clicked `hit` (the block and face), or by a dispenser (`player` and `hit` are `None`). Leave `answer` `None` for vanilla's emptying. Answer `Some(true)` when the part placed the contents itself through the host: the stack becomes an empty `minecraft:bucket` (a creative player keeps it), no vanilla sound plays and the use counts as a success. Answer `Some(false)` for a failure: nothing is placed, a player keeps the full stack and a dispenser drops it, as vanilla's does when emptying fails. Asked for a player once `bucket_empty` let the pour go on, and for a dispenser before its own placement; an item with this part reaches the dispenser's bucket behaviour even when it is no bucket of a mod fluid, and a `dispense_item` answer still comes first. A server's client does not predict the pour of an item with this part. The part is asked in a transaction with the game's apply: a part that changes the player's hand or the dispenser's slots while answering `Some(true)` is refused and its journaled writes are undone, as a failed use (the player keeps the stack, the dispenser fails). A player's successful pour counts for the `placed_block` criterion. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Server-side. |
| `held_item_tint` | client | replace | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | Tint one third-person held-item submission in the world, for a player, mob or armor stand. `entity_id` is the holder's current client-level entity id, `hand` is the logical main or off hand (independent of the dominant arm), `stack` is the rendered stack, and `partial_tick` is the frame's tick fraction. |
| `highlight_tip` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemExtension.getHighlightTip` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.getHighlightTip`: the name drawn above the hotbar when `stack` is selected. `name` arrives with what `Hud.extractSelectedItemName` draws (the hover name in its rarity colour, italic when the stack has a `custom_name`); the text the part leaves is drawn in its place, centred at the same height and fade, cut at the screen edge as a long custom name is. Asked once each client tick while the name shows. The display timer still restarts only when the selected item or its hover name changes, so a tip that changes every tick doesn't keep the name up. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Client-side. |
| `hurt_enemy` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A player's hit landed on the mob `entity` of type `kind` (`minecraft:zombie`), holding `stack`: where `Item.hurtEnemy` is called, before the post-attack enchantments and the weapon's wear. It only hears. Another player as the target is not told. Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). |
| `inventory_tick` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | — | A server tick of `stack`, the stack a player carries in `slot`: where `Inventory.tick` calls `Item.inventoryTick`, for every stack, each tick, `/tick freeze` included (it stops the world, not the player). `slot` is as `Inventory` saves it (0 to 35; the armour 100 feet to 103 head; -106 the off hand), and `selected` says it is the held slot. `ItemPlace::PlayerSlot { player, slot }` reads and changes the stack, which stays in its slot: an emptied one empties the slot, and the player's client gets the change. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item hear it. Server-side, for every living player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). |
| `is_combine_repairable` | both | listen | `&dyn LoopContext` | `IItemExtension.isCombineRepairable` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isCombineRepairable`: may two of `stack`'s item be combined in a crafting grid to repair it (`RepairItemRecipe.canCombine`)? `repairable` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack has durability (`minecraft:max_damage` and `minecraft:damage`); set it `true` or `false`. It is asked for each of the two stacks, once they are found to be single stacks of the same item, and both must answer `true`. A `true` for a stack with no durability (a maximum damage of 0) is refused with a warning, and vanilla's `false` is kept, because the result needs a positive maximum. The anvil's combining of two of the same item keeps vanilla's test and doesn't ask. It is asked on both sides, wherever the grid's result is matched: a crafting table, the inventory grid, a crafter and the client's own result. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. It is given the loop `context` of the side that runs the match and takes no `Host`, so answer from the stack and `context` alone, and fast: a match may ask it many times. With no part on the stack's item the cost is one table lookup, and the stack isn't converted. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. Diverges from upstream: new hook. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `is_not_replaceable_by_pick_action` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.isNotReplaceableByPickAction` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isNotReplaceableByPickAction`: must a pick (the pick-block key) leave `stack`, in hotbar `slot` (0 to 8), where it is? `player` is the player picking. `not_replaceable` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is enchanted; set it `true` to keep the stack in place or `false` to let the pick swap it out. Asked in `Inventory.getSuitableHotbarSlot`'s second pass, when a pick moves an item into the hotbar and no hotbar slot is empty: from the selected slot on, for each hotbar stack, until one answers `false`, which is the slot the pick uses; when all answer `true`, the selected slot is used. A pick that selects a matching hotbar stack, or finds an empty slot, doesn't ask, nor one `player_pick_item` cancelled. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on the server (the host's own player and its guests), with a read-only `Host`. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `is_piglin_currency` | both | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.isPiglinCurrency` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isPiglinCurrency`: is `stack` barter currency to a piglin (`PiglinAi.isBarterCurrency`)? `currency` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is a `minecraft:gold_ingot`; set it `true` to make the stack currency, `false` to refuse it (a gold ingot included), or leave it to keep vanilla's answer. Asked where vanilla asks: whether a piglin wants a stack on the ground (currency is wanted whenever its off hand holds no `#minecraft:piglin_loved` item, with no inventory-space check); whether an adult piglin that isn't admiring takes the stack from a player's hand, on the server and in the client's prediction of that click; and when a piglin stops holding the item it admired, where an adult barters currency (`minecraft:gameplay/piglin_bartering`, thrown toward the nearest player) after admiring it, loses it when hit, and equips or keeps anything else. A currency stack picked up from the ground is admired, and so bartered, only when it is also in `#minecraft:piglin_loved`, as vanilla admires loved items only; otherwise the piglin equips or keeps it. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked, on both sides, so register the part on both for the client's prediction to agree with the server: answer from the stack alone, and fast. An item with no such part costs one table lookup. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `is_primary_item_for` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.isPrimaryItemFor` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.isPrimaryItemFor`: is `stack`'s item primary for `enchantment` (an id such as `minecraft:sharpness`), so enchantment selection may offer it? Selection chooses the enchanting table's offers and clues, loot `enchant_with_levels`, trades and spawn gear. `primary` arrives true for a plain book; otherwise it is the resolved `supports_enchantment` answer and membership in `primary_items` when that set is present. Set it true to offer the pair or false to withhold it, including for a plain book. |
| `item_bar` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | — | The small bar `GuiGraphicsExtractor.itemDecorations` draws under an item, wherever the game shows one: `bar` arrives holding vanilla's value (the damage bar, or a bundle's fullness already applied) and may be replaced (width `0..=13`, colour `rgb`) or set to `None` to hide it. Asked each frame a stack is drawn. Client-side. |
| `item_decorations` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemDecorator.render` | Append an item's custom decorations after its default bar, cooldown and count. Coordinates are the icon's top-left in GUI units, as the batch takes them; no item translation is active. Runs wherever the shared item-decoration path is used (hotbar, menus, tooltips, and the other screens that draw item icons: advancements, beacon, stats, trading). The NeoForge counterpart is `IItemDecorator.render`; registration through `RegisterItemDecorationsEvent` is a separate moment. |
| `item_interact_mob` | both | replace | `HostSide` | — | `ItemStack.interactLivingEntity`: after ordinary entity interactions pass, for an alive native Mob and a nonempty stack in `hand`. Both logical sides answer independently. A consuming client result ends the hand loop; only Success swings there. SuccessServer and Consume consume without a client swing; Fail, Pass and TryWithEmptyHand fall through. A server-only answer leaves client off-hand prediction unchanged. ClientHost permits reads and local effects, not authoritative writes. Armor stands, other players and dragon parts are not covered. A client without this mod predicts vanilla here; the server corrects it. |
| `item_name` | both | collect | `&dyn LoopContext` | `Item.getName` | Vanilla's `Item.getName(ItemStack)`: the name of `stack` from its components, so one mod item has many names (a data model named after the mob in it, a filled container after its contents). `name` arrives as `None`, which keeps the default: the stack's `minecraft:item_name` component, else the item's translated name. Set it to `Some(text)` to name the stack; a translatable `Text` (`Text::translate`) renders in each viewer's own language. It replaces the default rather than filling in for a missing `item_name`, as the vanilla items that override `getName` do. A `minecraft:custom_name`, or a written book's title, still wins over it (`ItemStack.getHoverName`), and the part isn't asked then. |
| `item_stacked_on_other` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` | NeoForge's `ItemStackedOnOtherEvent` and vanilla's `Item.overrideStackedOnOther` / `Item.overrideOtherStackedOnMe`, fired inside a resolved pickup click (a direct left/right `Pickup` when no drag is active, or a one-slot left/right `QuickCraft` drag release; never on multi-slot or middle-button drags, active-drag resets, or creative catalogue slots), with the cursor and both stacks frozen in `event`. |
| `makes_piglins_neutral` | server | listen | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.makesPiglinsNeutral` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.makesPiglinsNeutral`: does `stack`, worn by `wearer`, keep calm piglins from targeting it, as gold armour does (`PiglinAi.isWearingSafeArmor`)? `neutral` arrives with vanilla's answer, whether the stack is in `#minecraft:piglin_safe_armor`; set it `true` to make the piece count as gold, `false` to make it not count though the tag lists it, or leave it to keep the tag's answer. Asked for each stack in a player's armour slots, in the order feet, legs, chest, head, until one counts. A player wearing a piece that counts is not the target a calm piglin picks (`NEAREST_TARGETABLE_PLAYER_NOT_WEARING_GOLD`); an angry piglin attacks anyway. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. It is asked whenever the server builds a player's view for the mobs, several times a tick, so the answer must be a pure, fast function of `stack` and `wearer`. An item with no such part costs one table lookup. Server-side. The contract beyond the method's name and parameters is Sunrise's reading, not checked against NeoForge's. |
| `must_survive` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `BlockItem.mustSurvive`: whether placing this item must pass the block's `can_survive` predicate in the final `BlockItem.canPlace` check. Asked on both sides after placement state selection, with a read-only level host. Standing/wall items select candidates using unconditional survival checks and their own placement-state override; this hook does not skip them. `Some(false)` skips survival only, not replaceability, bounds or entity obstruction. `Some(true)` requires survival. `None` keeps vanilla's requirement (false for scaffolding, true for other block items). Dispatch is by `item` id; this event has no held-stack components. |
| `on_block_placed_by` | server | listen | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `BlockItemExtension.onBlockPlacedBy` | `BlockItemExtension.onBlockPlacedBy`: the selected block has been placed, its final block state and the original item's block-entity data applied, and `Block.setPlacedBy` completed (including companion blocks). Runs once, at the primary position, before the item's placement sound, game event, placement trigger and stack consumption. `state` is the live block state after the callback, which can have changed the block or run its own effects. `stack` is the pre-shrink stack; `placer` is the placing player, or None for a dispenser. `level` is the level the block was placed in. `face` and `click` are the placement context's clicked face and world-space hit point. Failed, refused and canceled placements never notify, nor do successful writes that leave a different block at the primary position. Only Parts attached to the original item hear this server notice. |
| `on_dropped_by_player` | server | cancel | `&dyn Host` | `IItemExtension.onDroppedByPlayer` | `IItemExtension.onDroppedByPlayer`: `player` (the acting player's handle) pressed the drop key on `stack`, the whole selected stack before anything leaves it; `all` is true when the whole stack goes, false for one item (`ServerPlayer.drop(boolean)`). Set `canceled` and the stack stays in the slot: nothing is taken out, no item entity is made, no drop statistic is awarded and `item_toss` does not fire; a server's player is sent the slot back, undoing their predicted drop. Not asked for a menu's throw, nor for a spectator (who drops nothing). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked: an item with none costs one table lookup. Server-side, with a read-only `Host`. |
| `on_entity_item_update` | server | cancel | — | `IItemExtension.onEntityItemUpdate` | `IItemExtension.onEntityItemUpdate`: each tick of a dropped item entity, before the entity's own tick (`ItemEntity.tick`: its fluids, movement, pickup delay, merging and lifetime), and after `entity_tick_pre`. `event.subject` is an `ItemEntityServerScope`: `with_view` lends a live `ItemEntitySubjectView` for the dropped item's handle, position, velocity, stack, pickup delay, age, target, owner and checked writes, and `host()` borrows `&mut dyn ServerHost` sequentially for attachments and world operations. Return `Flow::Cancel` to skip that tick's vanilla work, as NeoForge's `true` return does. The item skips movement and its own merge pass, but another item can still merge into it. `entity_tick_post` still hears it. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to the item ID held at dispatch entry hear it. Read the live stack via `event.subject.with_view` and `view.with_stack`; component conversion is deferred until that stack view is projected. Server-side, for every dropped item that ticks. |
| `on_entity_swing` | both | cancel | `HostSide` | `IItemExtension.onEntitySwing` | `IItemExtension.onEntitySwing`: the player in place (`entity`, its `Entity.getId` when known) is about to swing `stack` in `hand`: where `LivingEntity.swing` starts, for an attack, a use or a swing the server asked for. Set `canceled` and no swing starts: no animation and no swing packet. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Both sides, as vanilla's: answer the same on both, and change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. |
| `on_item_use_first` | server | replace | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IItemExtension.onItemUseFirst` | `IItemExtension.onItemUseFirst`: the held item's first say on a click on a block, on the server for each player, after `use_item_on_block` (`ItemBeforeBlock`) let it through and before the block's own `useItemOn`. An answer ends the click: neither the block nor the item's `useOn` runs. `Pass` goes on to the block. |
| `on_left_click_entity` | server | cancel | `&mut dyn ServerHost` | `IItemExtension.onLeftClickEntity` | `IItemExtension.onLeftClickEntity`: `player` (the acting player's UUID), holding `stack` in the main hand, is about to attack the entity `entity` (`Entity.getId`) of type `kind` (`minecraft:cow`, `minecraft:player`, `minecraft:item_frame`): where `Player.attack` starts, once the global `attack_entity` let the blow through and before any damage, knockback, enchantment effect, `hurt_enemy` or the weapon's wear. Set `canceled` and the attack ends there: the target is not hurt, `hurt_enemy` does not fire and the stack takes no wear. Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Server-side, for every player of the world (the helpers read the acting one). |
| `on_use_tick` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Tick` | `Item.onUseTick` of `stack`, used by `entity` with `remaining` ticks left, always above 0: what a mod's own item does each tick it is used (cook, charge, drain). It comes where `ItemStack.onUseTick` calls the item, once `living_entity_use_item_tick` let the use go on: after a consumable's crumbs, for consumables, shields and mods' items (vanilla's other items do their own, and a kinetic weapon has none on the server). Both sides, as `living_entity_use_item_tick`: change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. Runs every tick of every use: check the item first. |
| `placement_context` | both | replace | `LevelSide` | — | `BlockItem.updatePlacementContext`: replace `context` before selecting the block's placement state, on both sides with a read-only level host. `Some(Some(context))` rewrites the target and click, `Some(None)` refuses placement, and `None` keeps the item's vanilla rewrite (including scaffolding's column walk). The placing item cannot be changed. The actual pre-use `stack` supplies the item key and components. `context.source` distinguishes a player placement (`PlacementSource::Player { yaw, pitch, sneaking }`) from a directional non-player placement (`PlacementSource::Directional { direction }`): the effective placement rules, which a rewrite can change independently of `placer`. `placer` supplies the actual player's handle and eyes on this side, or is absent for a dispenser. A player's original destination may be occupied: a part can redirect the placement even when vanilla would refuse it there. A dispenser still needs a replaceable front cell before asking the part. With no answer, vanilla checks the original destination before its own rewrite. A part's rewritten destination must be loaded, in bounds, and replaceable with the supplied context. Non-finite click or player view values are refused. A rewritten directional context's `replace_clicked` is read from the block at `hit_pos`, not from the answer, and a directional question vanilla throws on (`PlacementStateError::NoVerticalLook` or `ReplacingClickedCycle`) refuses the placement. Survival and obstruction still apply at the final position. |
| `release_using` | both | listen | `HostSide` | `LivingEntityUseItemEvent.Stop` | `Item.releaseUsing` of `stack`: `entity` let it go with `time_left` ticks of its `use_duration` left, so it was held for `duration - time_left` (a bow-like item shoots by that charge). Set `used` as vanilla's return, and the stack's use cooldown starts (`ItemStack.releaseUsing`). After the vanilla item's own release, once `living_entity_use_item_stop` let it through. Both sides, as vanilla's: change the world only where this `Game` runs the world, not on a server's client. |
| `should_cause_block_break_reset` | client | listen | `&dyn ClientHost` | `IItemExtension.shouldCauseBlockBreakReset` | NeoForge's `IItemExtension.shouldCauseBlockBreakReset`: does a break restart because the main hand changed? `stack` is the stack the break began with (vanilla's `destroyingItem`), `held` the main hand now (`None` for an empty hand). `reset` arrives with vanilla's answer: not `ItemStack.isSameItemSameComponents(held, stack)` (count left out, damage compared). `false` keeps the break and its progress going; `true` aborts it and starts it again from 0. A `true` for an unchanged stack restarts the break every tick, so it never finishes. Asked on each `continueDestroyBlock` and `startDestroyBlock` of the block the break targets, on the local player's client (single player and a server's client alike). Only the `ItemPart`s attached to `stack`'s item are asked. Client-side. |
